# <a name="_heading=h.e1vwocudtwdr"></a>**THE ÆTHERIUM CODEX — THE FOUR-PILLAR ENGINE**
## <a name="_heading=h.yk7ms3hhs48l"></a>**Document 3: Social, Mental, and Metaphysical Resolution**
### <a name="_heading=h.5s555enzsohb"></a>**Canonical Draft — April 16, 2026**
-----
## <a name="_heading=h.nh1r5pw2jq0g"></a>**PREAMBLE**
Document 2 established the combat engine: tempo, Fulcrum Points, engagement states, reactions, critical system, and nested clock architecture. That engine was demonstrated primarily through Physical pillar examples. Document 3 specifies how the same engine operates through the other three pillars — Mental, Social, and Metaphysical — with equal mechanical weight and identical resolution architecture.

The engine does not change. The CONTENT of each OODA cycle changes. The verbs (23+Variable canonical effects) are universal. The fuel (pillar-specific FP pools) is structurally identical. The processing speed (Wits + Mastery tempo) is shared. What differs is which skills deliver which effects against which defenses.

This document uses canonical formulas from the Player’s Guide V4.1, effect definitions from SRD v3 Amendment 1, and the Skill × Effect Compatibility Matrix developed in this session.

-----
# <a name="_heading=h.v8i6t7ctjo40"></a>**PART I: THE SHARED RESOLUTION FRAMEWORK**
## <a name="_heading=h.v4bam3s25vwt"></a>**What Is Identical Across All Four Pillars**
Every statement in this section applies to Physical, Mental, Social, AND Metaphysical combat without exception.

**Tempo:** Wits + Mastery = beats per phrase. Same pool, same commitment spectrum (Light 1 / Standard 2 / Heavy 3), same phrase structure.

**FP Cost:** (ESL × 2) − 1 drawn from the relevant pillar pool.

**Attack Check:** d20 + Attribute + Skill + Modifiers vs. target’s Composite Defense.

**Composite Defense:** Dodge + Deflection for the relevant pillar (Player’s Guide V4.1).

**Damage Application:** Aggregated per phrase. At phrase end, total compared against Damage Threshold. Threshold exceeded = condition track movement.

**Five Degrees of Success:**

|Result|Degree|Effect|
| :- | :- | :- |
|≥ TN+10 OR natural 20 that succeeds|Critical Success|Full effect + isotope expression + 1 FP refund|
|≥ TN|Success|Full effect|
|1-4 below TN|Success with Flaw|Full effect + target gains free reaction trigger|
|5-9 below TN|Failure|No effect|
|≤ TN-10 OR natural 1|Critical Failure|No effect + all engaged actors gain 1 bonus tempo|

**Reactions:** Triggered by specific events, cost FP not tempo, one per trigger, appropriate reactive effect required.

**Condition Tracks:** 5 steps, penalties of -1/-2/-5/-10/Incapacitated. Steps replace within a track, stack across tracks. 0 HP = immediate Incapacitated.
## <a name="_heading=h.2iiaim30t221"></a>**What Differs Per Pillar**

|Component|Physical|Mental|Social|Metaphysical|
| :- | :- | :- | :- | :- |
|**HP Pool**|Health|Resolve|Reputation|Pattern|
|**HP Formula**|(Vigor×3)+(Might×3)+(Mastery×5)|(Wits×3)+(Intellect×3)+(Mastery×5)|(Presence×3)+(Composure×3)+(Mastery×5)|(Integrity×3)+(Anima×3)+(Mastery×5)|
|**FP Pool**|Physical FP|Mental FP|Social FP|Metaphysical FP (ÆP)|
|**FP Formula**|(Vigor+Tc)×Mastery|(Acuity+Tk)×Mastery|(Guile+Ts)×Mastery|(Dominion+Tₘ)×Mastery|
|**Dodge**|Finesse+Wits+(Mastery/2)|Acuity+Dominion+(Mastery/2)|Guile+Wits+(Mastery/2)|Anima+Acuity+(Mastery/2)|
|**Deflection**|Might+Armor+Shield+(Mastery/2)|Intellect+Composure+(Mastery/2)|Presence+Intellect+(Mastery/2)|Integrity+Vigor+(Mastery/2)|
|**Soak**|((Vigor+Mastery)/2)+Armor|((Wits+Mastery)/2)+Talents|((Composure+Mastery)/2)+Status/Title|((Integrity+Mastery)/2)+Faith/Pact|
|**Threshold**|(Vigor+Mastery)×2|(Wits+Mastery)×2|(Composure+Mastery)×2|(Integrity+Mastery)×2|
|**Track Name**|Vitality|Clarity|Poise|Pattern Integrity|
|**Skills**|Athletics, Skullduggery, Conveyance, Survival|Lore, Medicine, Artifice, Discovery|Streetwise, Performance, Influence, Valuation|Metaphysics, Operate, Insight, Praxis|
## <a name="_heading=h.3tj55sstpwvk"></a>**The Condition Tracks (Player’s Guide V4.1)**
**Physical — Vitality**

|Step|Penalty|State|
| :- | :- | :- |
|-1|-1|Winded — panting, bruised, slightly off-balance|
|-2|-2|Reeling — clumsy movements, constant pain|
|-3|-5|Wounded — significant injury hampers every move|
|-4|-10|Crippled — severely injured, speed halved|
|-5|Incapacitated|Unconscious/Dying|

**Mental — Clarity**

|Step|Penalty|State|
| :- | :- | :- |
|-1|-1|Distracted — focus frayed, thoughts wander|
|-2|-2|Shaken — fear or pressure, hard to concentrate|
|-3|-5|Frightened — actively afraid or overwhelmed|
|-4|-10|Befuddled — Confused condition, can’t distinguish friend from foe|
|-5|Incapacitated|Catatonic — mentally shut down|

**Social — Poise**

|Step|Penalty|State|
| :- | :- | :- |
|-1|-1|Flustered — momentarily embarrassed|
|-2|-2|Humbled — significant blow to confidence|
|-3|-5|Disgraced — major public failure, object of scorn|
|-4|-10|Rapt — socially overwhelmed, Flat-Footed + Disadvantage|
|-5|Incapacitated|Broken — complete social breakdown, flee/surrender/comply|

**Metaphysical — Pattern Integrity**

|Step|Penalty|State|
| :- | :- | :- |
|-1|-1|Frayed — unease, personal reality wavers|
|-2|-2|Strained — minor uncontrolled metaphysical effects|
|-3|-5|Corrupted — pattern damaged, minor signs of mutation/decay|
|-4|-10|Unraveling — Staggered condition (one action per turn)|
|-5|Incapacitated|Dissolving — pattern collapse, plane ejection or coma|

**0 HP Rule:** Physical or Mental 0 HP = Unconsciousness. Social or Metaphysical 0 HP = Emotional Collapse (confusion effect).

**Track Rules:** Steps replace within track (not self-cumulative). Steps across different tracks ARE cumulative. Metaphysical track steps produce temporary narrative detriments (cosmetic, not beneficial).

# <a name="_heading=h.dogzgphopo30"></a>**PART II: THE MENTAL PILLAR (S0-S3)**
## <a name="_heading=h.2psyazc4uk3e"></a>**What Mental Combat IS**
Mental combat is the application of trained intellect against complex systems, cognitive hazards, and environmental challenges. The “enemy” is usually a non-sentient system with its own Resolve pool — a cipher, a pathogen, a Kan-tech lock, a forensic mystery — that must be worn down through sustained intellectual labor.

When the environment attacks back (memetic hazards, reality bleed, psychic intrusion, system counter-measures), the character defends with their Clarity track and Resolve pool.

Mental “damage” to non-sentient targets is PROGRESS THROUGH INTELLECTUAL LABOR, not psychic combat. Depleting a cipher’s Resolve means you decoded it. Depleting a pathogen’s Resolve means you synthesized the cure.
## <a name="_heading=h.veat41otd63c"></a>**Non-Sentient Systems as Targets**
Puzzles, ciphers, locks, pathogens, investigations, and other complex systems use a simplified version of the character sheet:

|Stat|Derivation|
| :- | :- |
|Resolve Pool|Complexity rating (GM-assigned, typically 10-50)|
|Composite Defense|Difficulty TN (GM-assigned based on system sophistication)|
|Soak|Resistance to brute-force approaches (high for elegant ciphers, low for crude locks)|
|Threshold|How much progress triggers a breakthrough (system “gives” at threshold crossings)|

No condition track. When Resolve hits 0, the system is defeated/solved/cured. Progress is cumulative across sessions if the character returns to the work.
## <a name="_heading=h.lvbiwco4whfe"></a>**The Four Mental Skills**
### <a name="_heading=h.smc9pue4otpi"></a>**Lore (Academic Knowledge, History, Arcana)**
**Attack formula:** d20 + Intellect + Lore + Mastery vs. system’s Composite Defense

**What it does:** Cross-references retained knowledge against the problem. Brute-force academic assault — overwhelming a cipher with every linguistic precedent, identifying a ward’s historical origin, recalling the architectural tells of a specific college’s construction.

**Compatible effects (from matrix):** Damage, Affliction, Nullify, Weaken, Senses, Create, Illusion

**S0 example:** Translating a line of ancient text (Light, 1 Mental FP) **S1 example:** Identifying the college origin and approximate age of a ward network (Standard, 3 Mental FP) **S2 example:** Cross-referencing a complete dossier to map an enemy faction’s doctrinal vulnerabilities (Standard, 3-5 Mental FP) **S3 example:** Conducting a multi-day research operation analyzing institutional archives to identify a historical precedent that undermines an enemy’s legal claim (Heavy across multiple S0 tempos filling an S3 clock)
### <a name="_heading=h.qokx20b0qad3"></a>**Medicine (Healing, Anatomy, Psychology, Diagnostics)**
**Attack formula:** d20 + Intellect + Medicine + Mastery vs. system/target Composite Defense

**What it does:** Applied science of body and mind. Diagnostic assessment of biological state, pathogen deconstruction through laboratory work, surgical application of anatomical knowledge, psychological anchoring against cognitive hazards.

**Compatible effects (from matrix):** Damage, Affliction, Nullify, Weaken, Protection, Immunity, Healing, Panacea, Senses, Chronos, Create, Enhanced Trait, Transform

**S0 example:** Rapid triage assessment of an ally’s condition (Light, 1 Mental FP) **S1 example:** Performing field surgery on a wounded fireteam member (Standard, 3 Mental FP) **S2 example:** Synthesizing a counter-agent for a Kan-tech pathogen at a field laboratory (Heavy across multiple S0 tempos, fills S2 clock) **S3 example:** Developing a vaccination protocol for a regional plague (sustained S0 labor across days, fills S3 investigation clock)
### <a name="_heading=h.9shgtqxz4x9d"></a>**Artifice (Crafting, Engineering, System Interaction)**
**Attack formula:** d20 + Relevant Attribute + Artifice + Mastery vs. system Composite Defense

**What it does:** Practical application of engineering and crafting knowledge. Building, repairing, disabling, analyzing mechanical and magical systems. The full SRD v3 crafting system runs through Artifice.

**Compatible effects (from matrix):** Damage, Affliction, Nullify, Weaken, Protection, Healing, Panacea, Senses, Create, Move Object, Enhanced Trait, Summon, Transform

**S0 example:** Picking a tumbler lock (Standard, 3 Mental FP) **S1 example:** Disabling a Kan-tech alarm system protecting a facility (Standard, 3-5 Mental FP) **S2 example:** Constructing field fortifications for a defensive position (Heavy, sustained across S2 clock) **S3 example:** Engineering a Forge ward network for a district (sustained S0 labor across weeks, fills S3 project clock)
### <a name="_heading=h.dfbkfgfykcf5"></a>**Discovery (Deduction, Investigation, Precision Perception)**
**Attack formula:** d20 + Wits + Discovery + Mastery vs. system/target Composite Defense

**What it does:** Finding the linchpin. The precision strike of intellectual labor — where Lore overwhelms with volume, Discovery finds the one detail that changes everything. Extremely narrow (4 compatible effects) but devastating when it lands.

**Compatible effects (from matrix):** Nullify, Weaken, Senses (primary), and nothing else

**S0 example:** Spotting the hidden variable in a rigged card game (Light, 1 Mental FP) **S1 example:** Tracking a target through an urban environment by microscopic clues (Standard, 3 Mental FP) **S2 example:** Noticing the structural flaw in a Chiaroscuro illusion during an extended investigation (Standard, 3-5 Mental FP) **S3 example:** Identifying the single point of failure in an enemy faction’s operational security through sustained surveillance (Heavy across multiple S0 tempos filling S3 clock)
## <a name="_heading=h.d819pfwq9sq9"></a>**Mental Reactions**
**Triggers:** - Memetic hazard activating in your presence - Psychic intrusion attempt targeting you - Environmental cognitive pressure (reality bleed, forbidden knowledge) - System counter-attack (trap triggers, cipher defenses activate)

**Appropriate reactive effects:** - Protection (cognitive fortification — bracing mentally, maintaining coherence) - Senses (pattern recognition — reflexively identifying the threat) - Nullify (analytical counter — recognizing and dismissing the intrusion)

**Cost:** Mental FP at (ESL × 2) − 1. No tempo consumed.
## <a name="_heading=h.ojwsr9qnjrus"></a>**Mental Track Recovery**
Mental track recovery occurs during downtime at rates determined by track state severity. Medicine (psychological anchoring, cognitive therapy) and rest accelerate recovery. Severe Mental track damage (-3 or below) often requires specific narrative intervention — therapy from a trained practitioner, confronting the source of the trauma, or Schema pressure point engagement.

-----
# <a name="_heading=h.sx3pdjwl6lrl"></a>**PART III: THE SOCIAL PILLAR (S0-S3)**
## <a name="_heading=h.p9guwvxpyo9g"></a>**What Social Combat IS**
Social combat is the battle for credibility, leverage, and control. You attack a target’s Reputation pool (Social HP) through argument, deception, intimidation, and leverage. You defend your own Reputation through composure, wit, authority, and social positioning.

The Social HP pool (Reputation) represents internal social stamina — your confidence, composure, and rhetorical endurance in a specific encounter. It is NOT the world’s perception of you (that’s the Background system). A character at 0 Social HP has been outmaneuvered in THIS encounter — they haven’t necessarily been ruined permanently.

Social HP regenerates at the same drip rate as all other pillar pools. Getting into back-to-back social conflicts before recovery is mechanically draining — the condition track reflects your internal state, not external perception.
## <a name="_heading=h.9yt51ocgkw82"></a>**The Four Social Skills**
### <a name="_heading=h.t5jod7sxfc16"></a>**Influence (The Hammer — Direct Social Damage)**
**Attack formula:** d20 + Presence + Influence + Mastery + Achieved ESL of active tool vs. target’s Social Composite Defense

**What it does:** The ONLY social skill designed to consistently deplete Reputation. Direct argument, command, negotiation, logical inevitability. This is where the setup pays off — once Valuation has stripped Soak and Streetwise has applied conditions, Influence delivers the killing blow.

**Compatible effects (from matrix):** Damage (primary), Affliction, Nullify, Weaken, Protection, Healing, Panacea, Senses, Mind Link, Create, Enhanced Trait, Summon

**S0 example:** Demanding compliance from an individual (Standard, 3 Social FP) **S1 example:** Issuing a command to a small group under your authority (Standard, 3 Social FP) **S2 example:** Delivering a closing argument to a tribunal (Heavy, 5+ Social FP) **S3 example:** Conducting a week-long diplomatic negotiation between factions (sustained S0 labor filling S3 clock)
### <a name="_heading=h.1k2cqkw14jnc"></a>**Performance (The Feint and Crowd Weapon)**
**Attack formula:** d20 + Presence/Guile + Performance + Mastery vs. target’s Social Composite Defense (1-on-1) or Crowd’s Combined Defense (crowd targeting)

**What it does:** Two modes. One-on-one: the feint — projecting a false persona or intent, stripping the target’s Social Dodge for the next attack. Crowd: weaponizing the audience as an environmental hazard against the target. Unity Academy and Chiaroscuro Institute’s signature social tool.

**Compatible effects (from matrix):** Damage, Affliction, Nullify, Weaken, Protection, Healing, Panacea, Senses, Mind Link, Chronos, Create, Illusion (primary), Enhanced Trait

**S0 example:** Feinting in a one-on-one negotiation (Standard, 3 Social FP — target loses Social Dodge against next attack) **S1 example:** Performing for a small audience to shift their attitude (Standard, 3 Social FP) **S2 example:** Delivering a speech to a city square crowd (Heavy, 5-9 Social FP) **S3 example:** Conducting a multi-day propaganda campaign through Kan-tech broadcast infrastructure (sustained S0 labor filling S3 clock)
### <a name="_heading=h.836fz5c9916q"></a>**Valuation (The Armor Sunder — Defense Stripping)**
**Attack formula:** d20 + Acuity + Valuation + Mastery vs. target’s Social Composite Defense

**What it does:** Appraises what a person values, fears, or needs, then translates that knowledge into mechanical advantage. Does NOT deal damage — strips Social Soak by the Achieved ESL. Fed by investigation (Discovery, Lore, Medicine providing raw data). The cold read without prior data is hard; the informed appraisal with a dossier is devastating.

**Soak stripping is PROSPECTIVE.** It applies only to hits delivered AFTER the Valuation action, not retroactively to earlier damage in the phrase.

**Compatible effects (from matrix):** Nullify, Weaken (primary), Protection, Senses, Illusion, Enhanced Trait

**Five-degree resolution for Valuation:**

|Degree|Result|
| :- | :- |
|Critical Success|Social Soak stripped to 0 for remainder of encounter + target’s Schema Anchor revealed|
|Success|Social Soak reduced by Achieved ESL|
|Success with Flaw|Social Soak reduced by half Achieved ESL, but you tip your hand — target knows what leverage you hold|
|Failure|Tool ineffective, Soak intact|
|Critical Failure|Target’s Social Soak increases for the encounter as guard goes up|
### <a name="_heading=h.5ngr3sbiz509"></a>**Streetwise (The Condition Applier — Targeted Suppression)**
**Attack formula:** d20 + Guile + Streetwise + Mastery + Achieved ESL of tool vs. target’s Social Composite Defense

**What it does:** Deploys criminal leverage, blackmail, or intimidation to functionally cripple the target’s social maneuverability. The defacto method for inflicting social status conditions. Unlike other skills, Streetwise targets specific Background dots by name — you must have knowledge of the Background to suppress it.

**Compatible effects (from matrix):** Damage, Affliction (primary), Nullify, Weaken, Protection, Movement, Senses, Create, Illusion

**Targeted lockout mechanic:** - Attacker declares which specific Background dots they’re suppressing - Attacker must have narrative knowledge of those Backgrounds - Socially Restrained [Value] suppresses up to [Value] dots in the named Background - Partial suppression: target retains access to remaining dots (5-dot Allies suppressed by 3 = 2 functional Allies dots) - Wasted suppression: if you inflict Restrained 5 but only know about 3 dots, the extra 2 are lost
## <a name="_heading=h.nan908w8pfpg"></a>**Social Reactions**
**Triggers:** - Direct social attack targeting you (accusation, insult, demand) - Social Tool deployed against your defenses - Public revelation of damaging information about you - Attempt to strip your Soak (Valuation) or suppress your Backgrounds (Streetwise)

**Appropriate reactive effects:** - Protection (counter-argument — defending your position, maintaining composure) - Damage (retort — immediate counter-accusation) - Illusion (redirect — deflecting the attack, changing the subject) - Nullify (dismissal — neutralizing the social tool through authority or indifference)

**Cost:** Social FP at (ESL × 2) − 1. No tempo consumed.

**Critical distinction for crowd combat:** Performance targeting a CROWD does not trigger a reaction from the ultimate target. The performer attacks the crowd’s defense, not the target’s. The target only gets to react when the crowd’s condition APPLIES TO THEM on a subsequent round. This is what makes crowd weaponization powerful — the initial setup bypasses the target’s reaction economy.
## <a name="_heading=h.wz719bgw0gtz"></a>**The Investigation-to-Strike Pipeline**
Social combat has a natural multi-round rhythm:

|Phase|Action|Skill|FP Pool|Effect|
| :- | :- | :- | :- | :- |
|**Investigate**|Gather raw data about target|Discovery / Lore / Medicine|Mental FP|Senses|
|**Appraise**|Refine data into weaponized tool|Valuation|Social FP|Weaken (strip Soak)|
|**Condition**|Apply debilitating social state|Streetwise|Social FP|Affliction (target Backgrounds)|
|**Strike**|Deliver killing blow to exposed target|Influence|Social FP|Damage (to Reputation)|

Players who skip Investigation and Appraisal fight against full Soak every hit. Players who invest in setup strip defenses first, then strike cleanly. The pipeline rewards preparation and punishes brute force — the social equivalent of positioning before attacking.

The cross-pillar cost structure creates natural party roles: the analyst (Mental investment, Discovery/Lore) gathers the dossier, the face (Social investment, Influence/Valuation) weaponizes it. Neither can optimally do both unless they’ve invested deeply across pillars.

-----
# <a name="_heading=h.daaj227tirtx"></a>**PART IV: SOCIAL TOOLS AS SPELL SENTENCE CONSTRUCTS**
## <a name="_heading=h.q7b807bk2bvy"></a>**Core Principle**
Information is mechanically built through the same dual-pool grammar as spells. A Social Tool (forged ledger, compromising photograph, sworn testimony, royal writ) is constructed with an Effect Pool and a Construction Pool, producing an Effective Social Level that determines its mechanical weight.
## <a name="_heading=h.v3tbnqds3zj4"></a>**The Effect Pool (What the Tool Does)**
Like a spell, a piece of leverage has a primary effect:

|Tool Intent|Effect|
| :- | :- |
|Impose a social condition|Affliction|
|Strip target’s defenses|Weaken|
|Deceive an audience|Illusion|
|Destroy target’s standing|Damage|
|Suppress an active social effect|Nullify|

The tool’s subtype targets a specific aspect of the target’s identity — Commerce (financial ruin), Passion (secret lover), Alliance (proof of treason), Faith (heretical text). If the subtype directly threatens a target’s Schema values, the impact is magnified.
## <a name="_heading=h.n35o4ynomg68"></a>**The Construction Pool (How the Tool Deploys)**

|Parameter|Options|
| :- | :- |
|**Target/Area**|Individual (whispered blackmail) vs Spread/Burst (distributed pamphlets, broadcast)|
|**Range**|Close (gala whisper) vs Visual Sight (Kan-tech broadcast)|
|**Duration**|Hours (stolen patrol route) to Permanent (deep faction secret)|
|**Subtractives**|Sense-Dependent (must read document), Limited (only affects members of a specific College)|

When a Social Tool’s Duration expires, its ESL drops to 0. Information has an expiration date.
## <a name="_heading=h.51j37r1qg9mg"></a>**Achieved ESL Through Valuation**
A Social Tool has a Base ESL determined by its quality and construction. Valuation determines the Achieved ESL — how effectively the tool is deployed in the moment.

The Achieved ESL determines the FP cost of the strike that uses it. A tool with Achieved ESL 5 makes the Influence strike cost (5 × 2) − 1 = 9 Social FP. The weight of what you’re saying determines the social stamina required to say it. A character with a low Social FP pool literally cannot deliver a high-ESL revelation — they don’t have the composure to swing that hammer.
## <a name="_heading=h.bu6wkpolg9zl"></a>**The Exposed Cooldown**
Once a Social Tool is deployed against a target, it gains “Exposed” status against that specific individual or crowd for the remainder of the scene. The same tool cannot be used to repeatedly strip Soak or apply conditions round after round.

After a tool is Exposed, the attacker must either deploy a different tool or switch to Influence (the hammer) to exploit the opening the first tool created.

Exposed is a SCENE-LEVEL tracking mechanic, not a discrete condition. It does not interact with Panacea or Nullify. It simply reflects that the shock value of a specific piece of leverage is spent once deployed.

-----
# <a name="_heading=h.fff64ayxkvul"></a>**PART V: CROWD COMBAT**
## <a name="_heading=h.zduwzudqf6zw"></a>**Core Principle**
A crowd is not a collection of NPCs. It is an environmental hazard that can be seized, weaponized, or pacified. When Performance or Influence targets a crowd, you are attacking the CROWD’s defenses to turn them into a weapon against your actual target. The crowd becomes a DoT that continuously damages the target’s Reputation until they address it.

This is the Systemic Catalyst applied at Social scale. The crowd IS the natural system being leveraged.
## <a name="_heading=h.zh3cado2722g"></a>**Crowd Statistics**
**Combined Defense:** (Average Guile + Average Composure of demographic) / 2 + local faction’s Mastery Level

**Attitude Modifiers:**

|Attitude|Defense Modifier|
| :- | :- |
|Hostile to your position|+5|
|Neutral / Uninformed|+0|
|Sympathetic / Aligned|-5|

**Crowd Tier (Size determines DoT potency):**

|Tier|Size|Social Damage Per Round|
| :- | :- | :- |
|1|Tavern, boardroom (10-30)|1 per round|
|2|Gala, guild hall (50-100)|3 per round|
|3|City square, stadium (500+)|5 per round|
## <a name="_heading=h.h4dv6hmw16q"></a>**The Attack**
Roll Performance/Influence/Streetwise + relevant attribute + Mastery + Tool ESL (if applicable) vs Crowd’s Combined Defense.

|Degree|Result|
| :- | :- |
|Critical Success|Crowd becomes weaponized mob. Severe condition AND Tier DoT applied. Target’s Social Deflection overridden.|
|Success|Crowd turns on target. Standard condition OR Tier DoT applied.|
|Success with Flaw|Crowd turns on target and applies DoT, but you incur a debt to the crowd or the mob causes collateral Background damage.|
|Failure|Crowd unamused, remains neutral. Action wasted.|
|Critical Failure|Crowd turns on YOU. DoT applied to your Reputation instead.|
## <a name="_heading=h.vgvydec1nzq6"></a>**Crowd-Applied Conditions**

|Condition|Social Expression|
| :- | :- |
|Bleeding/Poisoned equivalent|Public Scrutiny — ambient DoT. Bypasses target’s Dodge and Deflect; only Soak absorbs.|
|Deafened equivalent|Drowned Out — target suffers -5 to all social checks, constantly fighting the room’s noise.|
|Restrained equivalent|Mobbed — target suffers -2 to Social Defenses, cannot leave without triggering physical conflict.|
|Frightened equivalent|Ostracized — target’s only viable option is yield or flee entirely.|
## <a name="_heading=h.tgd18u8yzz50"></a>**Defensive/Buff Conditions (Crowd Supporting You)**

|Condition|Social Expression|
| :- | :- |
|Invisible equivalent|Echo Chamber — Advantage on your social attacks, Disadvantage on attacks against you.|
|Regeneration equivalent|Adulation — heal Social HP equal to Crowd Tier per round.|
## <a name="_heading=h.c3pte19kqpza"></a>**Clearing Crowd Conditions**
The target has three options:

1\.          **Tank it:** Let Soak absorb the DoT, ignore the crowd, attack you directly

2\.          **Flee:** Leave the area to break the crowd’s influence

3\.          **Pacify the crowd:** Spend their entire action rolling Performance or Influence against the Crowd’s Combined Defense to calm, redirect, or intimidate them into silence
## <a name="_heading=h.jmx3yy3zbuoz"></a>**Why the Target Can’t React to the Initial Attack**
Performance targeting a crowd attacks the CROWD’s defense, not the ultimate target’s. The target doesn’t get a reaction trigger until the crowd’s condition actually applies to them on a subsequent round. By then, the crowd is already turned. This is the power of indirect social warfare.
## <a name="_heading=h.ualmz6evrwkx"></a>**Institutional Applications**
**Unity Academy:** Their Thrallherd networks artificially lower Crowd Combined Defense in their territory, turning entire city blocks into pre-weaponized social infrastructure. The initiation teaches scope of influence through community building — proving yourself to move between social strata.

**Chiaroscuro Institute:** They use Illusion effects as Social Tools to trick crowds into perceiving fabricated events, instantly shifting Attitude Modifiers to Hostile against the target. The crowd doesn’t know it was manipulated.

**Sim-stim technology (Unity-produced):** Kan-tech broadcast equipment that compresses Scope transitions for crowd manipulation. A sim-stim broadcast operates at S3 but is composed of accumulated S0 performances transmitted through infrastructure, reaching audiences across a region simultaneously.

-----
# <a name="_heading=h.d6dmqog29qup"></a>**PART VI: THE METAPHYSICAL PILLAR (S0-S3)**
## <a name="_heading=h.mv5bpgpytod"></a>**What Metaphysical “Combat” IS**
The Metaphysical pillar is a laboratory, not a battlefield. Its four skills handle the identification, operation, perception, and survival of magical and planar phenomena. Combat applications exist but are secondary — these skills primarily function during investigation, downtime, crafting, and environmental navigation.

When Metaphysical combat DOES occur, it is slow, methodical, and consequence-heavy. Unweaving a ward takes minutes to hours, not a combat round. Operating a hostile Kan-tech device requires sustained concentration. Resisting planar mutation pressure is an endurance test, not a duel.
## <a name="_heading=h.jeb0mb2oo8ig"></a>**The Four Metaphysical Skills**
### <a name="_heading=h.sqbql1diwudm"></a>**Metaphysics (Spellcraft + Slow-Burn Unweaving)**
**Attack formula:** d20 + Intellect + Metaphysics + Mastery vs. spell/ward Pattern (ESL + caster’s Mastery)

**What it does:** The hard science of magical architecture. Identification of ongoing effects, reading ward structure, recognizing spell syntax during casting. When used offensively, slowly deconstructs magical phenomena by pulling apart their concordance structure. NOT a combat interrupt — requires focus and sustained time.

**Compatible effects (from matrix):** Damage, Affliction, Nullify (primary), Weaken, Protection, Healing, Panacea, Senses (primary), Create, Enhanced Trait

**S0 example:** Identifying a spell as it’s being cast by reading somatic/verbal syntax (Light, 1 Metaphysical FP) **S2 example:** Safely defusing a Kan-tech ward over ten minutes of careful unweaving (Heavy sustained, 5+ Metaphysical FP) **S3 example:** Analyzing and deconstructing an institutional ward network over days of methodical work (sustained S0 labor filling S3 clock)
### <a name="_heading=h.agwep1lynohv"></a>**Operate (Kan-tech Interface — The Mundane Variable)**
**Attack formula:** d20 + relevant attribute + Operate + Mastery vs. device’s activation TN

**What it does:** Use Magic Device applied to an industrialized magical setting. Activates Kan-tech terminals, triggers wands belonging to other colleges, boots ætheric engines, interfaces with any magical device regardless of prerequisites. The effect produced is determined by the DEVICE — Operate provides access, not capability.

**Compatible effects (from matrix):** 23 of 24 (everything except Feature). Operate IS the mundane Variable — any effect a device can produce, Operate can activate.

**S0 example:** Activating an unfamiliar wand (Standard, 3 Metaphysical FP) **S1 example:** Operating a Kan-tech vehicle’s ætheric engine while Conveyance handles steering (Standard, 3 Metaphysical FP) **S2 example:** Hijacking an enemy facility’s ward system through its control interface (Heavy sustained, 5-9 Metaphysical FP) **S3 example:** Managing a Forge ward communication network across a district (sustained S0 labor, S3 operational task)
### <a name="_heading=h.q173yyngs27x"></a>**Insight (Preternatural Wisdom Sense)**
**What it does:** The gut feeling. The hair standing up. Insight “pings” the metaphysical environment — sensing the weight of active buffs/debuffs/mutations, detecting wrongness in reality, feeling danger before it manifests. Does NOT provide analytical data (that’s Metaphysics interpreting what Insight detected).

**Compatible effects (from matrix):** Senses (primary), Protection (warning sense). Two effects total. Extremely narrow, extremely valuable.

**Soul Reading:** Requires a disconnected soul or a roundabout divinatory ritual (tarot, casting bones, reading entrails). Provides symbolic impressions of Schema values — “The Emperor inverted, shadowed by the Tower” — not raw numbers. Cannot directly soul-read a living person.

**Chiaroscuro Warning:** Using Insight to pierce hyper-real (>100% collapse) illusions is dangerous. Staring too deeply into a manufactured reality doesn’t dispel it — it invites the cognitive dissonance of the Great Lie directly into your brain, risking Mental and Metaphysical track damage.
### <a name="_heading=h.a7tep1eca2c8"></a>**Praxis (The Soul’s Immune System)**
**What it does:** The metaphysical muscle. Managing mutations, resisting planar warping, maintaining identity coherence under metaphysical stress. Not combat-first — this is the skill of SURVIVING in a world where the environment actively tries to rewrite you.

**Compatible effects (from matrix):** Damage, Affliction, Weaken, Protection (primary), Immunity, Immortality, Healing, Panacea, Enhanced Trait, Summon, Transform

**S0 example:** Suppressing an unwanted mutation manifestation in polite company (Light, 1 Metaphysical FP) **S1 example:** Anchoring your pattern against a localized planar bleed zone during exploration (Standard, 3 Metaphysical FP) **S2 example:** Acting as a human grounding wire for a damaged ley line while allies evacuate (Heavy sustained, 5-9 Metaphysical FP) **S3 example:** Enduring a prolonged exposure to high-Formation-Stage isotope fields during an extended expedition (sustained S0 resistance across days, fills S3 endurance clock)
## <a name="_heading=h.lihd1jmmg2vk"></a>**Metaphysical Reactions**
**Triggers:** - Isotope expression contacting your body - Planar bleed pressure exceeding Strain threshold - Spell effect targeting your Pattern - Nullify or Transform attempt on your active expressions

**Appropriate reactive effects:** - Protection (ontological fortitude through Praxis — asserting your pattern) - Senses (Insight ping — reflexively detecting the incoming threat) - Nullify (Metaphysics counter — identifying and dismissing the effect through theory)

**Cost:** Metaphysical FP at (ESL × 2) − 1. No tempo consumed.

-----
# <a name="_heading=h.uleo5kdgjakr"></a>**PART VII: INSTITUTIONAL SCALE (S4-S5)**
## <a name="_heading=h.cy9rwgcf6gq2"></a>**The Dual Nature of Institutions**
An institution is a large organism whose cell structure is visible. It uses BOTH the individual character sheet system AND the front/clock system from the Operating Philosophy:

**The character sheet** describes what the institution IS — aggregate HP pools across four pillars, condition tracks, Composite Defense, Soak, Threshold. When you attack an institution’s Reputation directly (a propaganda campaign), you’re targeting its Social HP pool.

**The front/clock system** describes what the institution DOES — operational clocks, methodology, representation chains, relationships. When you disrupt an institution’s operations (sabotaging their communication network), you’re advancing or retarding their operational clocks.
## <a name="_heading=h.a4lv4h83x7qs"></a>**Institutional Character Sheet**
An institution’s stats are derived from its leadership, infrastructure, and membership, scaled to its Scope:

|Stat|Derivation|
| :- | :- |
|HP Pools|Aggregate of leadership + institutional infrastructure + membership base|
|Composite Defense|Institutional Dodge (adaptability) + Deflection (structural resilience)|
|Soak|Institutional inertia — larger institutions absorb more damage before feeling it|
|Threshold|Scale-dependent — a guild’s threshold differs from a college’s|
|Condition Track|Same 5-step structure, mirrored at institutional scale|
## <a name="_heading=h.etvmu2pt6nw"></a>**Institutional Condition Tracks**
The same condition names apply but the EXPRESSION scales:

|Step|Individual Expression|Institutional Expression|
| :- | :- | :- |
|-1|Flustered|Minor operational disruption — delayed reports, confused messaging|
|-2|Humbled|Public setback — rival gains ground, internal morale dips|
|-3|Disgraced|Major institutional failure — public loss of confidence, membership questioning|
|-4|Rapt|Institutional paralysis — leadership unable to coordinate, factions forming internally|
|-5|Broken|Institutional collapse — dissolution, mass defection, restructuring required|
## <a name="_heading=h.kve78b9ca2ly"></a>**The Two Attack Surfaces**
**Attacking the Organism (HP/Track):**

Target the institution’s aggregate stats through sustained S0-S3 actions that cascade upward. A propaganda campaign filling an S3 clock deals Social damage to the institution’s Reputation pool. A sabotage campaign filling an S3 clock deals Physical damage to the institution’s infrastructure.

When institutional HP is depleted or the condition track reaches Incapacitated, the institution ceases to function as a coherent entity. It may reform, restructure, or be absorbed — but its current incarnation is done.

**Attacking the Operations (Clocks):**

Target specific operational clocks through the front system. Advancing an enemy institution’s “internal dissent” clock doesn’t damage their HP — it moves their political situation toward a threshold crossing that produces specific consequences.

Clock attacks and HP attacks are independent but complementary. A propaganda campaign that deals Social HP damage AND advances the “public distrust” clock is attacking both surfaces simultaneously.
## <a name="_heading=h.ia99p7wirh0g"></a>**How Individual Actions Cascade to Institutional Scale**
Per the nested clock architecture (Document 2), individual S0-S3 actions accumulate to fill S4-S5 institutional tempos. The five validated attack vectors (Stealth Accumulation, Parallel Relay Exploitation, Clock Starvation, Single Point of Failure, Analytical Poisoning) all operate at this interface.

The Systemic Catalyst talent allows individual characters to target institutional leverage points, multiplying their reach from personal to institutional scale. A party member using Discovery to find the structural keystone of an institution’s information network, then Artifice to sabotage it, then Performance to broadcast the resulting confusion — this is a multi-skill, multi-pillar, multi-Scope attack chain that cascades from S0 actions to S4-S5 institutional consequences.

-----
# <a name="_heading=h.iebhjv6y82gu"></a>**PART VIII: THE BACKGROUND SYSTEM**
## <a name="_heading=h.12891r923yom"></a>**Divine Umbrellas (XP Investment)**
XP purchases Reputation capacity in a Divine Aspect (Lex, Rhapsodia, Agora, etc.). The Umbrella’s rank determines how many Background dots can be slotted within that philosophical alignment. The XP investment is PERMANENT — the world cannot take away the fact that you are a person of consequence.

**Filling the Capacity:** Specific Backgrounds (Allies, Contacts, Influence, Rank, Resources, etc.) are slotted into the Umbrella through Schema alignment and narrative acquisition. The “payment” is philosophical loyalty, not additional XP.

**Fluidity:** Factions are not monolithic. Deepguard respects Lex. The Sovereign Exchange respects Lex. A local street gang respects Lex (the code). Your Umbrella is a passport — your Reputation travels with you and attaches to whoever in the new region recognizes your philosophical alignment.
## <a name="_heading=h.6kx8xwobcaxj"></a>**Cultural Dissonance (Replacing Binary Infamy)**
When a character enters a region whose Dominant Divine Value is antithetical to their Reputation Umbrella, they experience Cultural Dissonance — not criminal status, but social friction.

**The Social Tax:** Penalty to Influence and Valuation checks equal to Umbrella Rank when dealing with aligned institutional NPCs. Your etiquette confuses them.

**The Novelty Bonus:** Bonus to Performance and Streetwise checks equal to Umbrella Rank. You are the fascinating outsider. Counter-culture, bored elite, and marginalized populations are drawn to you.

**Background Warping:** Your Contacts don’t give you access to magistrates — they give you fascinated sociological scholars or underground art collectives. Your Influence doesn’t pass laws — it starts cultural trends in the lower wards.

**Escalation to True Infamy:** Only triggered if the character WEAPONIZES their Cultural Dissonance to actively attack the region’s dominant structure. Being the wrong alignment is Dissonance. Inciting a riot against the local Thrallherd network is Infamy.
## <a name="_heading=h.tk6wmaqcbc3d"></a>**Background Fallout System**
When Backgrounds are destroyed through social combat, the XP is NEVER dead.

**Phase 1 — Shock (1-3 sessions, GM discretion):** The destroyed Background dots enter the Pivot Pool. The character feels the mechanical sting of losing their safety net. The player marks the XP as safe but temporarily sequestered.

**Phase 2 — Grace Period (during Shock):** The GM drops narrative lifelines — factions offering aid with strings, opportunities arising from the fallout. The player is not expected to have an immediate answer. “I don’t know” is valid.

**Phase 3 — Reallocation Mandate (end of 3rd session):** XP must be released from the Pivot Pool. Three resolution methods: - **Proactive Pivot:** Player declares their plan — “I spent the last three sessions building a new network.” - **Taking the Lifeline:** Player accepts a GM-provided narrative hook. - **Default Severance:** XP automatically converts to Resources or Favors as a fail-safe.

**The Law of Equivalent Exchange:** No objective has only one door. For every obvious solution, the GM must prepare or permit at least three alternative vectors utilizing different Pillars or Backgrounds.

-----
# <a name="_heading=h.skhklbqjucyp"></a>**PART IX: INFORMATION INFRASTRUCTURE**
## <a name="_heading=h.n0f55mpzeayo"></a>**Dual Relay Systems**
Two parallel relay systems exist in Tessera, one magical and one technological. Both compress Scope transitions. Both are attack surfaces.

**Magical Relay:** - Senses effects for information gathering (Collection/Analysis/Prediction) - Mind Link effects for bidirectional communication - Forge ward networks for Scope compression - Palimpsest links for unlimited-range cross-territorial communication

**Technological Relay (Kan-tech):** - Radio networks (Forge-manufactured broadcast infrastructure) - Photography and cameras (S0 evidence preserved for deployment at higher Scopes) - Sim-stim technology (Unity-produced experiential broadcast — compressed sensory transmission) - Print media (pamphlets, newspapers, institutional publications) - Signal infrastructure (telegraph equivalent, semaphore networks, pneumatic tubes)
## <a name="_heading=h.qz3yotpho5o"></a>**Both Relay Systems Compress Scope**
A Forge ward transmitting a report from S0 to S4 compresses five transitions to one. A Kan-tech radio broadcast transmitting news from S0 to S3 compresses three transitions to one. The mechanical effect is identical — faster information flow with less fidelity loss per transition.
## <a name="_heading=h.wf6mmajldun5"></a>**Both Relay Systems Are Attack Surfaces**
Every infrastructure node is a point of vulnerability:

|Attack|Magical Infrastructure|Technological Infrastructure|
| :- | :- | :- |
|**Destroy the node**|Nullify the Forge ward|Sabotage the radio tower|
|**Corrupt the signal**|Illusion injected into Mind Link|False broadcast, doctored photographs|
|**Intercept the traffic**|Senses tapping the ward network|Wiretapping, signal interception|
|**Flood with noise**|Multiple conflicting Senses effects|Propaganda saturation, competing broadcasts|
|**Steal the infrastructure**|Operate hijacking the ward control|Physical seizure of broadcast equipment|
## <a name="_heading=h.vmhsri43l7z0"></a>**The Tet Offensive Vector in Tessera**
Kan-tech broadcast infrastructure exists in Tessera because Setting B IS a dieselpunk/Kan-tech industrial civilization. A faction exploiting broadcast infrastructure to transmit damaging S0 evidence directly to an S3-S5 audience — bypassing the target institution’s information relay chain — is using the Parallel Relay Exploitation vector through technological rather than magical means.

The infrastructure works for EVERYONE. The institution that built the broadcast network does not control what gets broadcast. A party with access to the physical equipment (Operate to activate, Performance to produce compelling content) can weaponize the institution’s own communication infrastructure against it.

This is why the Unity Academy produces sim-stim technology but guards its broadcast infrastructure jealously. They understand that the system they built to manage public perception can be turned against them by anyone with technical access and a compelling narrative.

*End of Document 3. This document integrates with Document 2’s combat engine architecture, uses canonical formulas from the Player’s Guide V4.1, and references the SRD v3’s 23+Variable effect list. The Skill × Effect Compatibility Matrix serves as the reference for which skills can produce which effects across all four pillars.*

### <a name="_heading=h.tchueug3jrsy"></a>**Compiled April 16, 2026**

## <a name="_heading=h.4f91e38zudih"></a>**PURPOSE**
This document catalogs every specific error, inconsistency, or outdated reference in the Document 1 (Unified Architecture) and Document 2 (Combat Engine, Parts 1-3) output files. Each entry identifies the error, cites the canonical source, and provides the correct value. A single consolidation pass will incorporate these corrections after Document 3 is complete.

The architectural decisions in these documents remain sound. These are formula-level, terminology-level, and mechanical-detail corrections — not structural redesigns.

# <a name="_heading=h.ybmx4lvdk19n"></a>**SECTION 1: HP POOL FORMULAS**
## <a name="_heading=h.dpm0wvu4xjyo"></a>**Error 1.1 — HP Formula (All Documents)**
**Where it appears:** Document 1 (Section: Resource Pools), Document 2 Part 1 (Part V: Unified FP System — HP reference), Document 2 Part 3 (Part IV: Damage Application)

**What was written:** Various formulas used, including (Primary Attr × 3) + (Secondary Attr × 3) + (Mastery × 5) in some places, and inconsistent references elsewhere. Document 1 referenced a formula from the outdated Character Creation v3 document.

**Canonical source:** Player’s Guide V4.1, Chapter 2

**Correct formulas:**

|Pillar|Pool Name|Formula|
| :- | :- | :- |
|Physical|Health (Vitality)|(Vigor × 3) + (Might × 3) + (Mastery × 5)|
|Mental|Resolve (Clarity)|(Wits × 3) + (Intellect × 3) + (Mastery × 5)|
|Social|Reputation (Poise)|(Presence × 3) + (Composure × 3) + (Mastery × 5)|
|Metaphysical|Pattern (Pattern Integrity)|(Integrity × 3) + (Anima × 3) + (Mastery × 5)|

**Verification:** At starting state (all attributes 1, Mastery 3): (1×3)+(1×3)+(3×5) = 21. Matches character profile.

# <a name="_heading=h.kb21uvk31iyw"></a>**SECTION 2: HP POOL NAMING**
## <a name="_heading=h.thp0204m98ec"></a>**Error 2.1 — Inconsistent Pool Names**
**Where it appears:** Document 1 (multiple sections), Document 2 Parts 1-3 (throughout)

**What was written:** Mixed usage of Vitality/Resolve/Poise/Pattern (Document 1 and early Document 2) vs Health/Resolve/Reputation/Pattern (character profile).

**Canonical source:** Player’s Guide V4.1 and Character Profile

**Correct names:**

|Pillar|HP Pool Name|Condition Track Name|
| :- | :- | :- |
|Physical|Health|Vitality|
|Mental|Resolve|Clarity|
|Social|Reputation|Poise|
|Metaphysical|Pattern|Pattern Integrity|

The HP pool and the condition track have DIFFERENT names for the same pillar. Health is the pool; Vitality is the track. Reputation is the pool; Poise is the track. Documents should use the correct name for the correct context.

# <a name="_heading=h.zcxnnao5b7ri"></a>**SECTION 3: DEFENSE SYSTEM**
## <a name="_heading=h.jbrsjflj2rzx"></a>**Error 3.1 — Defense TN Formula**
**Where it appears:** Document 2 Part 3 (Part II: Static Defense Mechanics)

**What was written:** “Defense TN = 10 + Pillar Defense Modifier + Mastery + Talent Bonuses + Protection Effects” — a base-10 system.

**Canonical source:** Player’s Guide V4.1, Chapter 2

**Correct formula:** Defense TN IS the Composite Defense score. No base 10.

**Composite Defense = Dodge Defense + Deflection Defense**

|Pillar|Dodge (Reflex)|Deflection (Fortitude)|
| :- | :- | :- |
|Physical|Finesse + Wits + (Mastery / 2)|Might + Armor Bonus + Shield Bonus + (Mastery / 2)|
|Mental|Acuity + Dominion + (Mastery / 2)|Intellect + Composure + (Mastery / 2)|
|Social|Guile + Wits + (Mastery / 2)|Presence + Intellect + (Mastery / 2)|
|Metaphysical|Anima + Acuity + (Mastery / 2)|Integrity + Vigor + (Mastery / 2)|

Attack check = d20 + Attribute + Skill/Talent + Modifiers vs. Composite Defense.
## <a name="_heading=h.f6midd104cc"></a>**Error 3.2 — Soak Formula**
**Where it appears:** Document 2 Part 3 (Part II: Static Defense Mechanics) — referenced but not specified correctly.

**Canonical source:** Player’s Guide V4.1

**Correct Soak formulas:**

|Pillar|Soak|
| :- | :- |
|Physical|((Vigor + Mastery) / 2) + Armor Soak|
|Mental|((Wits + Mastery) / 2) + Talent Bonuses|
|Social|((Composure + Mastery) / 2) + Status/Title Bonuses|
|Metaphysical|((Integrity + Mastery) / 2) + Faith/Pact Bonuses|
## <a name="_heading=h.qc7fe39hd229"></a>**Error 3.3 — Damage Threshold Formula**
**Where it appears:** Document 2 Part 3 (Part IV: Damage Application)

**What was written:** “Threshold = (Primary Attribute for that Pillar × 2) + Mastery + Threshold Bonus”

**Canonical source:** Player’s Guide V4.1

**Correct Threshold formulas:**

|Pillar|Damage Threshold|
| :- | :- |
|Physical|(Vigor + Mastery) × 2|
|Mental|(Wits + Mastery) × 2|
|Social|(Composure + Mastery) × 2|
|Metaphysical|(Integrity + Mastery) × 2|

At starting state (attribute 1, Mastery 3): (1+3)×2 = 8. Matches character profile.
## <a name="_heading=h.93mhdelskieb"></a>**Error 3.4 — The 4×4 Defense Matrix Labels**
**Where it appears:** Document 1 (Section: Defense Architecture), Document 2 Part 3 (Part II)

**What was written:** Document 1 established pillar-specific defense axis names (Evasion/Guard/Toughness/Vigor for Physical, Willpower/Control/Conviction/Courage for Mental, Grace/Dignity/Composure/Discretion for Social, Acuity/Integrity/Resilience/Essence for Metaphysical).

**Canonical source:** Player’s Guide V4.1 uses generic Dodge/Deflection/Soak/Threshold for all four pillars. The talent manual used a third naming scheme. The character profile uses Dodge/Deflect/Soak/Thresh.

**Resolution needed:** The pillar-specific defense axis names (Willpower/Control/Conviction/Courage etc.) are architecturally valid and provide richer mechanical identity. However, the canonical character profile uses the generic names. This is a DESIGN DECISION to be locked, not an error to be corrected — Alex should confirm whether to use the generic Dodge/Deflect/Soak/Thresh across all pillars or adopt pillar-specific names.

-----
# <a name="_heading=h.eojsrwmsd58a"></a>**SECTION 4: CONDITION TRACKS**
## <a name="_heading=h.32c677ejgtt6"></a>**Error 4.1 — Condition Track Penalty Values**
**Where it appears:** Document 2 Part 3 (Part IV: Damage Application and Condition Tracks)

**What was written:** Linear penalty ramp: -1 at step -1, -2 at step -2, etc. Also invented pillar-specific terminal states (Dying/Broken/Disgraced/Unmade).

**Canonical source:** Player’s Guide V4.1, Chapter 2

**Correct penalties:**

|Step|Penalty|Physical|Mental|Social|Metaphysical|
| :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- |
|0|—|Normal|Normal|Normal|Normal|
|-1|-1|Winded|Distracted|Flustered|Frayed|
|-2|-2|Reeling|Shaken|Humbled|Strained|
|-3|-5|Wounded|Frightened|Disgraced|Corrupted|
|-4|-10|Crippled|Befuddled|Rapt|Unraveling|
|-5|Incapacitated|Unconscious/Dying|Catatonic|Broken|Dissolving|

**Critical rules:** - Steps REPLACE within a track (not cumulative with themselves) - Steps across DIFFERENT tracks ARE cumulative - 0 HP on any pool = immediate drop to Incapacitated (bottom) - 0 Physical/Mental HP = Unconsciousness - 0 Social/Metaphysical HP = Emotional Collapse (confusion effect) - Metaphysical track steps produce temporary narrative detriments (not beneficial mutations) - External afflictions (Cursed, Poisoned, etc.) are Layer 3, separate from track steps
## <a name="_heading=h.3yzn1jedau0i"></a>**Error 4.2 — Track Recovery Rates**
**Where it appears:** Document 2 Part 3 (Part IV) — invented Scope-based recovery rates per step.

**Status:** The Player’s Guide does not specify per-step recovery rates. The rates I invented (S2 for -1, S3 for -2, etc.) are design proposals, not corrections of existing canon. They should be flagged as proposals pending confirmation rather than treated as established mechanics.

-----
# <a name="_heading=h.6c8naa3l37ed"></a>**SECTION 5: DEGREES OF SUCCESS**
## <a name="_heading=h.s1a1noh2rt0q"></a>**Error 5.1 — Four vs Five Degrees in Combat**
**Where it appears:** Document 2 Part 3 (Part III: The Critical System)

**What was written:** Four-state combat system: Critical Success / Hit / Miss / Critical Failure. “Success with Flaw” was explicitly dropped with the note “doesn’t translate cleanly to combat.”

**Canonical source:** Player’s Guide V4.1, Section 1.2 and 1.3

**Correct:** Five degrees of success apply to ALL checks including attack rolls:

|Result|Degree|Combat Application|
| :- | :- | :- |
|≥ TN+10 OR natural 20 that succeeds|Critical Success|Full damage + isotope critical expression + 1 FP refund|
|≥ TN|Success (Hit)|Normal damage|
|1-4 below TN|Success with Flaw|Hit lands but at a cost — overextension, weapon stress, self-exposure|
|5-9 below TN|Failure (Miss)|No damage|
|≤ TN-10 OR natural 1|Critical Failure|No damage + engaged actors gain bonus tempo|

**Design work needed:** Define what “Success with Flaw” means mechanically in combat. Suggestions from the Player’s Guide examples: “you pick the lock but your tools break; you hit the enemy but overextend.” Combat equivalents: - You deal damage but provoke a free reaction from the target - You deal damage but your weapon takes one step on its condition track - You deal damage but end the action in a disadvantageous position (grant Flat-Footed to next attacker) - You deal damage but the effort costs 1 additional FP from the relevant pool

This is a design decision, not a formula correction.

-----
# <a name="_heading=h.cn2o35bbs19z"></a>**SECTION 6: FP UNIFICATION**
## <a name="_heading=h.cvsxmay6khax"></a>**Note 6.1 — Stamina Formula Change**
**Where it appears:** Document 2 Part 1 (Part V: Unified FP System)

**What was written:** Physical FP = (Vigor + Tc) × Mastery. This REPLACES the canonical Stamina formula.

**Canonical Stamina formula (Player’s Guide):** (Vigor + Mastery) × 2

**Canonical ÆP formula (SRD v3):** (Dominion + Tₘ) × Mastery

**Status:** The FP unification was an intentional design decision approved by Alex. The Physical FP formula deliberately changes the Stamina calculation to parallel the ÆP structure. This is NOT an error — it is a locked design change. However, the following should be verified:

**Verification needed:** Does (Vigor + Tc) × Mastery produce appropriate starting values compared to the old (Vigor + Mastery) × 2?

| |Old Stamina|New Physical FP|
| :- | :- | :- |
|Starting (Vigor 1, Mastery 3, Tc=5)|(1+3)×2 = 8|(1+5)×3 = 18|
|Journeyman (Vigor 3, Mastery 5, Tc=10)|(3+5)×2 = 16|(3+10)×5 = 65|
|Master (Vigor 4, Mastery 8, Tc=15)|(4+8)×2 = 24|(4+15)×8 = 152|

The new formula produces significantly larger pools. This is intentional (pools naturally scale with character investment). But the universal cost formula (ESL × 2) − 1 and drip recharge rates need to be tuned against these larger pools during playtesting.
## <a name="_heading=h.pp4yywxm8yao"></a>**Note 6.2 — Stamina Regeneration Rate**
**Where it appears:** Document 2 Part 1 (Part V: Drip Recharge)

**What was written:** “Base rate is one FP per S2 interval per pool” with ×1/×2/×4 conditional multipliers.

**Canonical Stamina regeneration (Player’s Guide):** Mastery Rating × 2 per 10 minutes.

**Status:** The canonical Stamina regen produces specific numbers (Mastery 3 = 6 per 10 min, Mastery 5 = 10 per 10 min, Mastery 8 = 16 per 10 min). The “1 FP per interval” generic drip produces much lower numbers. These need reconciliation.

**Possible resolution:** The drip rate should be Mastery × 2 per S2 interval per pool under stressful conditions, doubled to Mastery × 4 under calm conditions, quadrupled to Mastery × 8 during sleep/rest. This preserves the ×1/×2/×4 structure while matching canonical Stamina regen as the ×1 baseline.

# <a name="_heading=h.bxqq0wul6y3d"></a>**SECTION 7: TERMINOLOGY CORRECTIONS**
## <a name="_heading=h.6bmxkvhc3df5"></a>**Error 7.1 — “Poise” as HP Pool Name**
**Where it appears:** Document 1, Document 2 Parts 1-3 (multiple references)

**What was written:** Social HP pool called “Poise” throughout.

**Correct:** Social HP pool is “Reputation.” The Social condition track is “Poise.” These are different things.
## <a name="_heading=h.dn82jrwmc6no"></a>**Error 7.2 — “Vitality” as HP Pool Name**
**Where it appears:** Document 1, some Document 2 references

**What was written:** Physical HP pool called “Vitality.”

**Correct:** Physical HP pool is “Health.” The Physical condition track is “Vitality.”
## <a name="_heading=h.qqkqyh7v0wbz"></a>**Error 7.3 — “Pattern” Ambiguity**
**Status:** “Pattern” is used for both the Metaphysical HP pool AND the condition track name (Pattern Integrity). The Player’s Guide distinguishes them as “Pattern Pool” and “Pattern Integrity (Track).” This should be consistently disambiguated in all documents.
## <a name="_heading=h.9ks05u8e50ob"></a>**Error 7.4 — “Composite Defense” vs “AC” vs “Combined Defense”**
**Where it appears:** Document 2 Part 3 (Part II)

**What was written:** References to “Defense TN” with a base-10 structure.

**Correct term:** Composite Defense. The sum of Dodge + Deflection for the relevant pillar. No base 10. The Player’s Guide explicitly defines this and uses it as the attack roll target.

|Petal|Pillar|Skills in This Petal|
| :- | :- | :- |
|Combat|Physical|Athletics, Skullduggery, Conveyance, Survival|
|Knowledge|Mental|Lore, Medicine, Artifice, Discovery|
|Social|Social|Streetwise, Performance, Influence, Valuation|
|Philosophy|Metaphysical|Metaphysics, Operate, Insight, Praxis|

Character Creation v3 uses a 9/7/5 spread across THREE petals (Combat/Knowledge/Philosophy) with Social absent. If Social is now a fourth petal, the creation spread needs updating to accommodate four groups.

The following canonical renames have been applied throughout this document. Source files (AC Unified Architecture, SRD v3 Combined, Character Profile, Book of Shadows v6) have not yet been updated to reflect these changes. When cross-referencing this document against source files, apply these mappings:

|**Old Term**|**New Term**|**Source**|
| :- | :- | :- |
|**Integrity (attribute)**|Moiety|CF-17|
|**Anima (attribute)**|Essence|OD-4, Session 10|

|**Ki Pool**|Dissolved into FP pool|OD-12, Session 11|


# **The Zero State**
Every entity begins existence at this baseline before any priorities, vocabulary, or XP are applied.

|**Trait**|**Starting Value**|
| :- | :- |
|**Mastery Level**|3 (Passing Knowledge). Purchasable to 5 during creation.|
|**Attributes**|All 12 attributes start at 1 dot.|
|**Skills**|All 16 skills start at 0 ranks.|
|**Backgrounds**|0 dots.|
|**XP Pool**|120 XP (liquid assets for customization in Step 7).|
|**Heritage Attributes**|Separate track. Applied in Step 2. Capped at +2.|
|**Magical Vocabulary**|Universal Grammar (all 0 CP components) free. Three derived pools in Step 5.|


# **Step 1: Psyche Schema**
**Vertebra 1 of the Narrative Engine.** The Schema says WHAT you value. Everything else determines HOW you pursue it.

**Inputs:** Player answers the Character Discovery Guide questionnaire (24 questions from the v2 Player Discovery Guide or cold-administration instrument).

**Process:** GM uses the Translation Guide to map narrative answers onto Schema dimensions: god-name weights, aspect values, Core Philosophy alignment. The player does not need to know the divine names or cosmological structure.

**Outputs:** Psyche Schema profile, college affinity recommendation, Legacy starting seed, FP pressure point identification (outliers, splits, breaks).

**Design Notes**

The Schema is not a personality test in the conventional sense. It is a diagnostic instrument that maps the character’s values onto the setting’s divine coordinate system. Two characters with identical Schema scores but different college affiliations produce completely different fronts because the college determines HOW the profile expresses. The Schema is stored in the character’s soul stone — a physical object in the setting that can be read, queried, stolen, damaged, or transferred.


# **Step 2: Heritage**
**Inputs:** Player choice of ancestry/species.

**Process:** Select Heritage qualities. Each quality is priced at magnitude through Spell Sentence scaling tables (the same grammar that governs spells, items, and mutations). Calculate Heritage rating. Apply Heritage attribute bonuses on a separate track.

**Outputs:** Heritage qualities, Heritage rating, Heritage attribute bonuses (separate track, capped at +2).

**Heritage Rating Formula**

Heritage Rating = (Highest Effective Quality EML + floor((Count − 1) / 4)) × 0.75

**Heritage Attribute Rules**

Heritage attributes function as a separate track from base attributes, similar to the Prodigy/Inept system. Each Heritage attribute bonus is capped at +2. Each ÆC attribute point equals a 3.5e modifier, not a score — a +2 Heritage bonus is equivalent to a +4 ability score bonus in legacy systems.

**PLACEHOLDER:** *Heritage State generation algorithm (Gap #3). The Punnett-square isotope-pair mapping exists conceptually but is not generator-ready. For the alpha build, use pre-built Heritage packages. Full procedural Heritage generation is a future workstream.*


# **Step 3: Attribute Allocation**
**Inputs:** Heritage bonuses from Step 2.

**Process:** Assign pillar priority. Distribute bonus dots across the four attribute groups using the 7/5/5/3 spread. All attributes begin at 1 before the spread is applied.

**Outputs:** 12 attribute scores (base + spread). Heritage bonuses tracked separately.

**The 7/5/5/3 Spread**

Assign priority to the four attribute groups: Physical, Mental, Social, Metaphysical. The primary group receives 7 additional dots. Two secondary groups each receive 5. The quaternary group receives 3. No attribute may exceed Rank 5 during this step.

|**Pillar**|**Attributes**|**Spread Applied To**|
| :- | :- | :- |
|**Physical**|Vigor / Might / Finesse|Distribute assigned dots freely among the three.|
|**Mental**|Wits / Intellect / Acuity|Distribute assigned dots freely among the three.|
|**Social**|Presence / Composure / Guile|Distribute assigned dots freely among the three.|
|**Metaphysical**|Moiety / Dominion / Essence|Distribute assigned dots freely among the three.|

**Constraints**

Creation cap: no attribute above Rank 5 during this step. Attributes are not constrained by Mastery. Heritage bonuses are applied on a separate track and do not count against the creation cap. Additional attribute dots may be purchased in Step 7 using XP (cost: New Rating × 4).


# **Step 4: Skill Allocation**
**Inputs:** Attribute scores from Step 3 (some skills key off specific attributes for checks).

**Process:** Assign pillar priority for skills. Distribute ranks within each pillar’s four skills.

**Outputs:** 16 skill ranks.

**The 9/7/5/3 Skill Spread**

Assign priority to the four skill pillars. The primary pillar receives 9 ranks to distribute. Secondary receives 7. Tertiary receives 5. Quaternary receives 3. Skill ranks are capped by current Mastery (3 at creation unless Mastery is purchased higher in Step 7).

**Skill Grid (16 Skills, 4 Per Pillar)**
|**Physical**|**Mental**|**Social**|**Metaphysical**|
| :- | :- | :- | :- |
|**Athletics**|Lore|Streetwise|Metaphysics|
|**Skullduggery**|Medicine|Performance|Operate|
|**Conveyance**|Artifice|Influence|Instinct|
|**Survival**|Discovery|Valuation|Praxis|

**Design Note**

Pillars are identity labels, not talent access gates. A character’s pillar priority describes who they are, not what they can do. Any talent tree can draw from any pillar’s FP pool. Skill priority and attribute priority do not need to match.


# **Step 5: Magical Vocabulary**
**Every living being in Tessera is composed of planar materials. There is no purely mundane baseline.** This step establishes every character’s starting magical vocabulary before any XP is spent.

**Inputs:** Attribute scores from Step 3 (specifically Intellect, Dominion, Acuity, Essence).

**Process:** Calculate three derived pools. Spend each pool on its designated category. Apply Universal Grammar (free).

**Outputs:** Starting Effect ratings, starting Isotope access, unlocked CP geometry components.

**A. Universal Grammar (Free)**

Every character inherently knows all 0 CP components: Individual, Ray, Emanation, Burst (Small) for shapes. Touch/Self and Close for ranges. Instantaneous and Concentration for durations. No cost, no roll, no purchase required.

**B. Shaping Pool (Effect Vocabulary)**

**Formula:** Intellect + Dominion

Spend these points to purchase starting ratings in Effects (the “verbs” of the Spell Sentence). One point buys one dot in one Effect. No individual Effect rating may exceed current Mastery. The logic: Intellect provides the formula, Dominion provides the command.

**C. Substance Pool (Isotope Vocabulary)**

**Formula:** Acuity + Essence

Spend these points to purchase starting access to Isotopes (the “adjectives” of the Spell Sentence). One point buys one dot in one Isotope. No individual Isotope rating may exceed current Mastery. The logic: Acuity perceives the specific frequency, Essence provides the connection to it.

**D. Grammar Points (CP Geometry)**

**Formula:** Intellect + Dominion

Spend these points to unlock Common-tier Construction Pool components (shapes, ranges, and durations costing 1–4 CP). Components costing 5+ CP (Dialect and Treasure tier) cannot be selected at creation and must be learned through play.

**Note on Pool Separation**

The Shaping Pool and Grammar Points share the same formula (Intellect + Dominion) but are separate pools. A character with Intellect 3 and Dominion 2 receives 5 points for Effects AND 5 points for geometry, not 5 total. The Substance Pool uses a different formula (Acuity + Essence) and is fully independent.


# **Step 6: Background**
**Inputs:** Player choice from 27 backgrounds (SRD v3 Combined).

**Process:** Select one background. Apply its mechanical benefits.

**Outputs:** Starting Clout, skill bonuses, narrative hooks, starting equipment package, college affiliation context.

**The Prince Rule (Social-Background Cap)**

A character cannot purchase a Background rating higher than their highest Social attribute (Presence, Guile, or Composure). This represents the social infrastructure required to maintain external resources. A character with Presence 4 can have Resources 4. A character with all Social attributes at 2 is limited to Background ratings of 2, regardless of available XP.

**College Affiliation**

College affiliation is social membership, not a mechanical gate. A character’s background and Schema affinity recommend a starting college, which provides institutional context, training methodology, and initial talent access patterns. College affiliation can change through play — migration, multi-college training, and personal development expand access beyond origin. The college determines HOW the Psyche Schema profile expresses.


# **Step 7: Talent & XP Spend**
**The 120 XP pool.** This is the customization layer that creates divergent character archetypes from identical base templates. All purchases draw from this single pool.

**Inputs:** 120 XP. All values from Steps 1–6.

**Process:** Spend XP on any combination of the options below. Free starting talents are applied before XP spending.

**Outputs:** Final Mastery, additional attributes/skills, talent list, expanded magical vocabulary, mutation investments, background ratings.

**XP Cost Table**
|**Trait**|**Cost Formula**|**Notes**|
| :- | :- | :- |
|**Mastery**|New Rating × 8|ML 4 = 32 XP. ML 5 = 40 XP (72 XP total from base 3 to 5).|
|**Attribute**|New Rating × 4|Creation cap: Rank 5. Heritage bonuses are separate.|
|**Skill (1st dot)**|3 XP (flat)|Capped by current Mastery.|
|**Skill (2nd+ dot)**|New Rating × 2|Capped by current Mastery.|
|**Talent**|BEL × 3|Prerequisite chaining discount applies.|
|**Effect (1st dot)**|6 XP (flat buy-in)|Rated 1–10. Capped by Mastery.|
|**Effect (advance)**|Current Rating × 4|Advancing from 2 to 3 costs 8 XP.|
|**Isotope (1st dot)**|6 XP (flat buy-in)|Rated 1–10. Capped by Mastery.|
|**Isotope (advance)**|Current Rating × 4|Compounds require constituent pures.|
|**Mutation**|BEL × 3|Same 120 XP budget. Not a separate economy.|
|**Background**|New Rating × 1|Capped by highest Social attribute (Prince Rule).|

**Free Starting Talents**

**PLACEHOLDER:** *Free starting talent count and selection rules are TBD. The alpha build will define a curated starter talent list. These represent the character’s basic training and style prior to play — a specific martial art, a magical tradition, or institutional methodology.*

**Mastery Advancement at Creation**

Mastery purchased during character creation has its benefits apply retroactively. It is considered to be purchased first, before any other XP expenditure. Advancing Mastery raises the cap on skills, Effects, and Isotopes, and increases all derived statistics. The “Hollow Master” archetype (72 XP on Mastery alone, leaving 48 for everything else) and the “Inept Prince” archetype (keep Mastery at 3, invest heavily in Backgrounds) are both valid and intentional build paths.

**PLACEHOLDER:** *Talent system rebuild (BEL assignments) is incomplete. Most talent trees do not yet have finalized BEL values. For the alpha build, reference the curated starter talent list.*

**PLACEHOLDER:** *Spells/rote construction at creation needs further specification. Characters use their Effect ratings, Isotope access, and Grammar Point unlocks from Step 5 plus any XP-purchased vocabulary to compose starting rotes.*


# **Step 8: Derived Statistics**
Calculated after all XP is spent. All formulas reference final attribute values and final Mastery.

**Inputs:** All final values from Steps 1–7.

**Process:** Calculate all pools, defenses, thresholds, and derived values.

**Outputs:** Complete mechanical profile.

**HP Pools (One Per Pillar)**
|**Pillar**|**Pool Name**|**Formula**|
| :- | :- | :- |
|**Physical**|Vitality|(Vigor × 3) + (Might × 3) + (Mastery × 5)|
|**Mental**|Resolve|(Wits × 3) + (Intellect × 3) + (Mastery × 5)|
|**Social**|Poise|(Presence × 3) + (Composure × 3) + (Mastery × 5)|
|**Metaphysical**|Pattern|(Moiety × 3) + (Essence × 3) + (Mastery × 5)|

**Fulcrum Point Pools (One Per Pillar)**
|**Pillar**|**Driving Attribute**|**Formula**|**T Counts**|
| :- | :- | :- | :- |
|**Physical**|Vigor|(Vigor + Tc) × Mastery|Combat pillar talents|
|**Mental**|Acuity|(Acuity + Tk) × Mastery|Knowledge pillar talents|
|**Social**|Guile|(Guile + Ts) × Mastery|Social pillar talents|
|**Metaphysical (ÆP)**|Dominion|(Dominion + Tₘ) × Mastery|Philosophy pillar talents|

Some specific talent trees may override the default pillar draw, calling FP from their invested tree’s pool rather than the pillar default. The universal FP action cost is (ESL × 2) − 1.

**Composite Defense (Per Pillar, No Base 10)**

Composite Defense = Dodge + Deflection. Attack checks roll d20 + Attribute + Skill/Talent + Modifiers against the target’s Composite Defense.
|**Pillar**|**Dodge (Evade)**|**Deflection (Deflect)**|
| :- | :- | :- |
|**Physical**|Finesse + Wits + (Mastery/2)|Might + Armor + Shield + (Mastery/2)|
|**Mental**|Acuity + Dominion + (Mastery/2)|Intellect + Composure + (Mastery/2)|
|**Social**|Guile + Wits + (Mastery/2)|Presence + Intellect + (Mastery/2)|
|**Metaphysical**|Essence + Acuity + (Mastery/2)|Moiety + Vigor + (Mastery/2)|

**Soak (Absorb) and Threshold**
|**Pillar**|**Soak Formula**|**Threshold Formula**|
| :- | :- | :- |
|**Physical**|((Vigor + Mastery)/2) + Armor Soak|(Vigor + Mastery) × 2|
|**Mental**|((Wits + Mastery)/2) + Talent Bonuses|(Wits + Mastery) × 2|
|**Social**|((Composure + Mastery)/2) + Status/Title|(Composure + Mastery) × 2|
|**Metaphysical**|((Moiety + Mastery)/2) + Faith/Pact|(Moiety + Mastery) × 2|

**Defense Names (16 Unique)**
|**Function**|**Physical**|**Mental**|**Social**|**Metaphysical**|
| :- | :- | :- | :- | :- |
|**Evade**|Evasion|Willpower|Grace|Elusivity|
|**Deflect**|Guard|Control|Dignity|Integrity|
|**Absorb**|Toughness|Conviction|Composure|Resilience|
|**Resist**|Vigor|Courage|Discretion|Coherence|

**Other Derived Values**
|**Value**|**Formula**|
| :- | :- |
|**Construction Pool**|(½ Effect Tier) + (½ Isotope Tier) + Mastery + Moiety|
|**Complexity Pool**|Same formula as Construction Pool|
|**Mutation Threshold**|Moiety × 5|
|**Tempo Pool**|Wits + Mastery|
|**ÆP Cost (per rote)**|(ESL × 2) − 1|
|**Anchor Cap**|ceil(Base EML / 2)|
|**Mastery Capacity Pool**|Mastery × 4 (base). Max: Mastery × 34 (with Expanded Consciousness).|


# **Step 9: Equipment**
**Inputs:** Background starting package from Step 6. Any remaining XP from Step 7.

**Process:** Select or purchase starting gear. Background provides a base equipment package.

**Outputs:** Equipment list with stat blocks.

**PLACEHOLDER:** *No equipment stat block catalogue exists. For the alpha build, use narrative equipment descriptions tied to background packages. Full equipment catalogue is a future workstream requiring an estimated 4–6 hours of creation.*


# **Step 10: Legacy Initialization**
**This is NOT a player choice step.** It is what the system sees when it reads everything the player has already decided. Identity crystallizes from pattern recognition, not forward-facing milestone achievement.

**Inputs:** The complete character sheet from Steps 1–9: Psyche Schema profile, Heritage, attributes, skills, college affiliation, background, talent selections, magical vocabulary, equipment.

**Process:** The GM reads the accumulated pattern of choices backward. The Palimpsest has already been recording this soul’s history. The starting Legacy state is derived from what the character’s pre-play existence reveals about who they are.

**Outputs:** Starting Legacy traits. Each trait provides a mechanical bonus in situations that align with the pattern and a mechanical penalty in situations that contradict it. Legacy modifies how Schema scores are weighted — not the scores themselves, but their significance.

**The Backward-Facing Read**

The Legacy System does not ask “what did you accomplish?” It asks “what pattern do your accumulated choices reveal about who you are?” A character who prioritized Physical attributes, took combat talents, chose a military background, and affiliated with Stormpeak has a pattern. That pattern crystallizes into Legacy traits that were not planned or assigned — they emerged from the accumulated shape of the character.

**What a Legacy Trait IS**

A permanent modifier reflecting accumulated pattern. It may provide a mechanical bonus in situations that align with the pattern, a mechanical penalty in situations that contradict it, or both. The trait is the character’s identity crystallized into a rule. Legacy traits are descriptive, not prescriptive. They describe who the character HAS BEEN, not who they SHOULD BE. The character can act against their Legacy — doing so creates friction (the trait’s penalty applies), which is itself a story moment.

**How Legacy Interacts with the Schema**

The Legacy trait modifies how the Schema is read. A character with a Legacy trait of “Protector” has their Constantine (Duty) score read with that specificity — the duty is now defined by the pattern of protective action. The Schema numbers do not change. Their weight and significance shift based on what the Legacy reveals.

**During Play**

After every significant cycle (typically every 3–5 sessions, or at the end of a campaign arc), the GM and player review the character’s accumulated actions. When the backward read reveals a consistent pattern, the GM proposes a new Legacy trait. The trait is discussed collaboratively. If both agree the pattern is genuine, the trait is added to the character sheet. The soul stone carries all Legacy data in-setting.


# **Appendix A: Condition Tracks**
Each pillar has a five-step condition track. When the corresponding HP pool reaches 0, subsequent damage drives the character down the track. Penalties are cumulative with the track position, not with each other.

|**Track**|**Step 1 (−1)**|**Step 2 (−2)**|**Step 3 (−5)**|**Step 4 (−10)**|**Step 5**|
| :- | :- | :- | :- | :- | :- |
|**Vitality (Physical)**|Winded|Reeling|Wounded|Crippled|Incapacitated|
|**Clarity (Mental)**|Distracted|Shaken|Frightened|Befuddled|Catatonic|
|**Poise (Social)**|Flustered|Humbled|Disgraced|Rapt|Broken|
|**Pattern Integrity (Meta.)**|Frayed|Strained|Corrupted|Unraveling|Dissolving|


# **Appendix B: Five-Degree Critical Table**
|**Degree**|**Attacker Gets**|**Target Gets**|
| :- | :- | :- |
|**Critical Success**|Full effect + isotope expression + 1 FP refund|Nothing extra|
|**Success**|Full effect|Nothing extra|
|**Success with Flaw**|Full effect|Free reaction trigger (costs FP to exploit)|
|**Failure**|Nothing|Nothing|
|**Critical Failure**|Nothing|All engaged actors gain 1 bonus tempo (free)|


# **Appendix C: Placeholder Steps**
The following elements are identified as incomplete and flagged for future workstreams. They do not block the alpha build — each has a defined workaround.

|**#**|**Placeholder**|**Workaround**|**Estimated Effort**|
| :- | :- | :- | :- |
|**1**|Heritage State generation algorithm (Gap #3)|Pre-built Heritage packages|Requires Punnett-square mapping formalization|
|**2**|Free starting talent count and selection rules|Curated alpha starter list|Depends on talent rebuild|

|**4**|Spells/rote construction at creation|Use Step 5 vocabulary + XP purchases|Needs worked examples|
|**5**|Equipment stat block catalogue|Narrative descriptions per background|4–6 hours estimated|


# **Appendix D: Casting Philosophies**
Every character chooses a casting philosophy at creation. This is a character identity choice, not a talent purchase.

|**Philosophy**|**Primary Attribute**|**Advantage**|**Weakness**|
| :- | :- | :- | :- |
|**Architect (Studied)**|Intellect|Recipe Optimization: FR reduction by ESL band (−4/−3/−2). Complexity Mastery: bonus to Complexity Pool = ⌊Dominion ÷ 2⌋. Intellect-based Metamagic efficiency.|Tactical rigidity. Rotes are fixed once designed.|
|**Improviser (Innate)**|Essence|On-the-fly modification: swap Additives or primary Isotope mid-cast. Essence-based ÆP reduction: per-rote Base ÆP reduced by (Essence − 3), minimum 0.|No Final Rank reduction. Less efficient foundations.|
|**Polymath**|Both|Maintains separate repertoires of optimized and flexible forms. Most versatile.|Requires investment in both paths. Rarest practitioners.|
