Documents 4a-4c covered the combat petal (martial trees), general utility trees, and philosophy petal (magical trees). Document 4d covers the two remaining talent domains: the Five Artifice Paths (crafting) and the Companion system (creature acquisition and specialization).

Both domains use the same Spell Sentence grammar and transmission framework. Crafting talents unlock the ability to create items — composing expressions INTO material substrates. Companion talents unlock the ability to acquire, enhance, and command creatures — composing expressions THROUGH biological substrates.

-----
# <a name="_heading=h.iexyu2l3uold"></a>**SECTION A: THE FIVE ARTIFICE PATHS**
Source: SRD v3, Section: The Five Artifice Paths

All five paths use the Artifice skill as their primary skill. Path tiers align with proficiency bands: Novice (Artifice 1+, Formation Stage 1-4), Journeyman (Artifice 5+, Formation Stage 5-7), Master (Artifice 8+, Formation Stage 8-10).

**Artifice Check = d20 + (Artifice Rank × 2) + Relevant Attribute + Modifiers**

-----
## <a name="_heading=h.ic8e0x21tpbk"></a>**Path 1: The Mundane Artisan**
**Discipline:** Physical operations — shaping, cutting, grinding, joining, finishing, assembly. **Primary Attributes:** Might (force operations), Finesse (precision operations), Wits (reactive decision-making).

**What it does:** Works with the physical properties of materials. Expertise in the real periodic table — hardness, tensile strength, density, elasticity. Transforms raw material into functional objects through physical technique.

**Transmission Framework:** The Artisan transmits through physical force applied to material substrates. They don’t compose Spell Sentence expressions — they prepare the CABLE that other expressions will travel through. The sword the Artisan forges has no enchantment, but its material properties (Tensile Strength → damage die, Hardness → Soak contribution, Density → weight class) determine how effectively it transmits the wielder’s physical expressions.

|Tier|Capability|
| :- | :- |
|**Novice**|Masterwork quality (×1.0 multiplier). Read material physical properties through examination. +1 Competence modifier on physical operations.|
|**Journeyman**|Complex multi-component assembly. Advanced material processing. Working with Formation Stage 5-7 materials.|
|**Master**|Pinnacle-tier materials. Legendary-quality items. Formation Stage 8-10 access. The item’s physical properties are maximized.|

-----
## <a name="_heading=h.em21thvjzme"></a>**Path 2: The Enchanter**
**Discipline:** Component installation — expressions, attachments, insets, charge systems. **Primary Attributes:** Dominion (channeling capacity), Intellect (compatibility analysis).

**What it does:** Populates the housing the Artisan built. Installs Active Expressions (internal rotes), Attachments (sub-items with their own rotes), and Insets (spell sentence modifiers). The Enchanter is NOT creating magic from nothing — they’re installing pre-composed expressions into prepared material substrates.

**Transmission Framework:** The Enchanter installs transmission channels into items. A sword without enchantment transmits only the wielder’s physical expressions. A sword WITH an installed Damage + Affliction (Burning) expression transmits both the wielder’s strike AND the item’s own rote simultaneously.

|Tier|Capability|
| :- | :- |
|**Novice**|Single expression installation. Basic charge system setup. Simple housing configurations.|
|**Journeyman**|Multiple expression layering. Attachment installation. Complex housing with selective routing (Forge Links managing which expression fires through which output).|
|**Master**|Full housing architecture. Inset installation. Multi-expression linked compositions within a single item. The item IS a spellcaster in material form.|

-----
## <a name="_heading=h.nbzjg930o7bv"></a>**Path 3: The Alchemist (Crafting Path)**
**Discipline:** Chemical operations — reagent processing, compound synthesis, alchemical treatment, transmutation. **Primary Attributes:** Intellect (formula design), Acuity (process monitoring), Wits (reactive adjustments).

**What it does:** Processes materials through chemical and alchemical operations. Where the Artisan shapes through force, the Alchemist shapes through chemistry. Potions, poisons, mutagens, reagent preparation, material purification, isotope extraction.

**Transmission Framework:** Alchemical processes ARE Spell Sentence compositions delivered through chemical medium. A potion IS a Create + Healing expression stored in liquid substrate. A poison IS an Affliction expression delivered through chemical transmission. The Alchemist composes the expression, the chemical substrate carries it, the target’s biology receives it.

|Tier|Capability|
| :- | :- |
|**Novice**|Basic potions, poisons, alchemical reagents. Simple single-effect compounds.|
|**Journeyman**|Complex multi-component compounds. Mutagen synthesis. Isotope extraction from raw materials. Material purification processes.|
|**Master**|Philosopher’s Stone project. Master-tier transmutations. Alchemical compounds that rival formal spellcasting in complexity. Magnum Opus capstone.|

-----
## <a name="_heading=h.9pqkq8a9ne0n"></a>**Path 4: The Appraiser**
**Discipline:** Analysis — material identification, expression decomposition, quality assessment, compatibility verification. **Primary Attributes:** Acuity (perception), Intellect (analysis), Wits (pattern recognition).

**What it does:** The Two-Table Read. Reads BOTH the real periodic table (physical properties) AND the planar periodic table (isotope identity, formation stage, concordance). Decomposes existing expressions to identify their Spell Sentence construction. Quality control for crafting operations.

**Transmission Framework:** The Appraiser doesn’t transmit — they RECEIVE. Their talent opens Senses channels specifically for material and expression reading. They see what an item IS at every layer: base material, active expressions, attachments, insets, formation stage, charge state, condition track position.

|Tier|Capability|
| :- | :- |
|**Novice**|Read physical properties (Hardness, Tensile Strength, Density). Identify material origin. Basic quality assessment.|
|**Journeyman**|Two-Table Read — identify both physical AND planar properties simultaneously. Expression Decomposition — reverse-engineer an item’s Spell Sentence construction. Compatibility assessment for installations.|
|**Master**|Full provenance reading — identify the crafter, the crafting method, the material source, and the history of modifications. Identify flaws invisible to lower-tier analysis. Certification authority for institutional quality standards.|

-----
## <a name="_heading=h.orlx5415y48d"></a>**Path 5: The Scrivener**
**Discipline:** Documentation — schematics, formulas, process records, reproducibility. **Primary Attributes:** Intellect (systematic documentation), Acuity (precise observation).

**What it does:** Records the crafting process so it can be REPRODUCED by others. Where the Artisan builds one sword, the Scrivener documents how to build a thousand. Institutional crafting IS Scrivener output — factory production requires schematics.

**Transmission Framework:** The Scrivener composes Senses (output) — Create (document) expressions. Their documentation IS a stored transmission — a blueprint that allows another crafter to reproduce an expression they didn’t personally design. The Scrivener is the industrial printing press of the crafting world.

|Tier|Capability|
| :- | :- |
|**Novice**|Basic schematics — record physical operations and simple assemblies.|
|**Journeyman**|Full expression documentation — record Enchanter installations, Alchemist formulas, compatibility requirements. Cross-discipline schematics.|
|**Master**|Institutional-grade reproducibility documentation. Isolation of critical variables. Quality standards that allow production lines to operate without master-tier supervision.|
## <a name="_heading=h.aenrwjx9dx7u"></a>**Path Interactions**
Common combinations from SRD v3:

|Combination|Application|
| :- | :- |
|Artisan + Enchanter|Smith who also installs components. Builds the housing AND populates it. Most common two-path combo.|
|Appraiser + Alchemist|Analyst who also processes. Reads what it IS, then changes it. Research alchemist.|
|Alchemist + Enchanter|Compound specialist who also installs. Creates treatments AND integrates them. Production enchanter.|
|Scrivener + Appraiser|Documentarian who also analyzes. Reads processes AND records them. Institutional researcher.|
|Artisan + Alchemist|Physical + chemical operations in one practitioner. Broadest single-practitioner capability.|
|**Full Workshop**|All five paths employed together — institutional-scale crafting. Artisans shape, Alchemists process, Enchanters install, Appraisers certify, Scriveners document.|

-----
# <a name="_heading=h.kgbneqsehxxh"></a>**SECTION B: THE COMPANION SYSTEM**
Source: Combined Companion Archetype & Creature Types document

The companion system has two layers: MASTER ARCHETYPES (how you acquire companions) and CREATURE TYPE PATHS (what kind of companion you specialize in). A character selects one Master Archetype and one or more Creature Type Paths.

-----
## <a name="_heading=h.tg0y1w7shp44"></a>**Layer 1: Master Archetypes (Acquisition Method)**
### <a name="_heading=h.v1hkazz0f1nx"></a>**Contracts (Path of Binding)**
**Crux:** Magical Contract — create magically binding agreements with creatures through ritual. **Cost:** Stamina equal to target’s EML AND ÆP equal to target’s EML. **Limitation:** Cannot bind creature with EML higher than your Mastery. **Group Binding:** Every 10 identical creatures count as one of next higher EML for cost calculation.

**Transmission Framework:** The contract IS a Mind Link + Affliction (Compelled) expression bound through ritual rather than combat. The creature is bound by the expression’s terms, not by loyalty. Contract trees expand enforcement (Ironclad Oath, Binding Mark, Unbreakable Vow), enhancement (Contractual Vigor, Boons), and versatility (Swift Binding, Multi-Binding, Transferable Contract).
### <a name="_heading=h.m75ito3t4o6a"></a>**Empathy (Path of Bonding)**
**Crux:** Empathetic Bond — form a permanent empathetic bond with a willing creature. Silent communication of emotions and simple concepts.

**Transmission Framework:** The bond IS a Mind Link (sustained, mutual) expression. Unlike Contracts, Empathy requires willingness — no coercion. The bonded creature stays because it chooses to. Bond trees expand the connection (telepathic link, shared senses, shared defenses) and the companion’s capabilities.
### <a name="_heading=h.ntgzl89x821m"></a>**Psychic Contagion (Seeded / Thrallherd)**
**Crux:** Implant Psychosometric Seed — implant a psychic seed in a target.

**Transmission Framework:** The seed IS a Mind Link (invasive) + Transform (mental, subtle) expression. Unity Academy’s signature acquisition method. The target doesn’t know they’ve been seeded. This is the most ethically fraught archetype — the companion doesn’t consent or even know they’re a companion. The Thrallherd network IS crowd combat infrastructure at institutional scale.
### <a name="_heading=h.8t0gzhff0j4c"></a>**Summons (Path of Creation)**
**Crux:** Summon Creature — use the Summon effect. Base duration = Mastery × 10 rounds.

**Transmission Framework:** The summon IS the Summon effect from the 23+Variable list. The creature is composed from æther using the same character engine as players (Spell = Item = Creature). ESL caps Creature Mastery Level. Evolution Pool (Feature Points) determines the construct’s capabilities.

-----
## <a name="_heading=h.gb547j1usak2"></a>**Layer 2: Creature Type Paths (Specialization)**
Each path specializes in a specific biological/metaphysical category of companion. The Crux for each is “[Type] Specialization” — unlocking the Companion Ascension system for that creature type.
### <a name="_heading=h.larj76xwo2uc"></a>**Beast — Path of the Wild**
**Specialization:** Natural animals and bestial creatures. The ranger, the beast master, the cavalry officer.

**Enhancement Trees:** - Primal Enhancement (raw physical power — Primal Vigor, Sharpened Fangs) - Bond Synergy (shared benefits — Shared Resilience, Pack Tactics)
### <a name="_heading=h.rjy9gy8iy1zp"></a>**Arcaniform — Path of the Arcanist**
**Specialization:** Magical and elemental creatures. The familiar master, the elemental binder.

**Enhancement Trees:** - Arcane Symbiosis (spell integration — Spell Conduit, Arcane Synthesis) - Resonant Casting (companion as magical focus — reduced EML, ÆP cost reduction)
### <a name="_heading=h.6clwtvdd1kv"></a>**Floraform — Path of the Verdant**
**Specialization:** Plant and fungal creatures. The grove warden, the mycologist, the Verdant Font specialist.

**Enhancement Trees:** - Verdant Growth (resilience, regeneration, adaptive biology) - Spore Network (communication and area control through fungal transmission)
### <a name="_heading=h.3i99djelc98r"></a>**Forged — Path of the Artificer**
**Specialization:** Constructed creatures. Golems, automatons, Kan-tech drones. The engineer’s companion.

**Enhancement Trees:** - Structural Enhancement (physical upgrades — armor plating, weapon mounts) - System Integration (Operate-linked control — remote operation, multiple construct management)
### <a name="_heading=h.cypjaoxlo9u"></a>**Humanoid — Path of Command**
**Specialization:** Humanoid followers, retainers, cohorts. The commander, the cult leader, the employer.

**Enhancement Trees:** - Leadership (morale and capability enhancement — Commander tree integration) - Organization (multiple follower management — squad mechanics, delegation)
### <a name="_heading=h.e2rw0rlumoco"></a>**Interstitial — Path of the Binder**
**Specialization:** Planar entities, outsiders, creatures from between the planes. The demonologist, the angel caller, the Palimpsest navigator’s companion.

**Enhancement Trees:** - Planar Attunement (resistance sharing, planar transit capabilities) - Essence Binding (investing your own Pattern into the companion — power at personal risk)
### <a name="_heading=h.sne5r7cn8v76"></a>**Returned — Path of the Gravemaster**
**Specialization:** Undead creatures, revenants, spirits. The necromancer, the Mortis Ævum death-priest.

**Enhancement Trees:** - Undead Enhancement (Synod-channeled resilience, fear aura, entropic touch) - Spirit Binding (incorporeal companions, possession-based tactics, spectral scouts)
### <a name="_heading=h.f35to825lyz1"></a>**Anomalous — Path of the Harbinger**
**Specialization:** Aberrations, mutations, creatures that don’t fit other categories. Vespidan symbiotes, Lacunae entities, beings born from Reality Seams.

**Enhancement Trees:** - Mutagenic Symbiosis (shared mutation — the companion’s biology feeds the master’s adaptation) - Harbinger Attunement (Planar Strain resistance through anomalous familiarity)

-----
## <a name="_heading=h.yq7p88av95gu"></a>**The Companion Ascension System**
All companion types use the same advancement framework. Companions don’t earn XP — they advance through the Companion Ascension system powered by the master’s investment.

|Resource|Effect|
| :- | :- |
|**Mastery Capacity Pool**|Each companion occupies capacity equal to its EML. Higher Mastery = room for more/stronger companions.|
|**Feature Points**|Budget for companion customization. Decompose into Spell Sentence expressions — claws (Damage + Transform), wings (Movement), breath weapon (Damage + Burst), etc.|
|**Companion Condition Tracks**|Same four-pillar track system as players. Companions can be Winded, Shaken, Flustered, or Frayed just like characters.|

The critical architectural point: companions use the SAME character engine as players. A companion’s claw attack IS a Damage expression transmitted through biological medium. A companion’s breath weapon IS a Damage + Burst expression using isotope geometry. The Feature Point budget determines how many of these expressions the companion has access to. The companion’s “talent trees” are their Feature Point purchases.

-----
*End of Document 4d. This completes the talent system catalogue across all domains: combat petal (4a), general utility (4b), philosophy petal (4c), crafting paths and companion system (4d). The Spell Sentence grammar and transmission framework are consistent across all talent types — every talent opens a channel, every ability decomposes into the 23+Variable effect vocabulary.*

*—*

**TESSERA**

*The Narrative Engine*



A World That Asks You to Pay Attention



Ætherium Codex

*Setting Prospectus*


# <a name="_heading=h.sfafibrcvad6"></a>**The World You Walk Into**
You arrive in a city where the walls remember every conversation that has ever happened near them. The streets record footfalls. The doorframes are carved with genealogies that extend past the boundary between the living and the dead. A grandmother who died forty years ago is not gone — she is in the queue, awaiting processing by a bureaucracy that has been managing the afterlife since before anyone alive can remember. Her grandson visits the parish office to check on her case number. He brings flowers. He also brings paperwork.

You travel to a port city where three million people buy and sell everything the world produces, where a merchant pauses for exactly two seconds before agreeing to any deal because that pause is the ritual acknowledgment that consequences are about to begin, where moths cluster visibly around buildings where significant transactions are closing because the moths can sense the probability ripples that every deal produces. The harbour glows at night from bioluminescent eels whose ancestors were ordinary brown fish before centuries of magical runoff selected for the ones that shine.

You visit a forest that does not care that you are there. The trees predate every institution in the world. The fungal network beneath your feet knows you arrived before you took your second step, and it has communicated your presence to every connected organism in a radius you cannot estimate. The college that operates here accepts anyone. No prerequisites. No background check. The forest offers the same terms to everyone: adapt or decompose. Some of the things hunting you in the deep canopy used to be people.

You attend a resort built on a swamp, where the food is extraordinary and the architecture is stunning and the performance you are watching is so beautiful it makes you cry, and every tear is a genuine tear, and the woman who produced it in you is eating cold noodles in a corridor you will never see, wondering whether tomorrow’s show will be the one where she goes into the role and doesn’t come back.

You walk into a volcano where the air itself has a statistical opinion about what you will do next. The casino is gorgeous and the house always wins because the house can see the future. The hot springs will tell you what is wrong with your body before your body tells you. The labyrinth beneath the caldera restructures itself between visits because the probability field resolved a different configuration the second time. Somewhere in the deep levels, geological creatures are communicating through the stone in frequencies you cannot hear, discussing whether you are interesting enough to notice.


# <a name="_heading=h.ntejaedwq6in"></a>**The World Underneath**
None of this is window dressing. Every detail you just read is a consequence of how the world actually works — its physics, its theology, its political architecture, its economics. The walls in the desert oasis remember conversations because the dominant planar expression at that location preserves information in material surfaces. The moths at the port city track probability ripples because centuries of exposure to a specific magical interference pattern selected for that trait. The forest’s fungal network communicates through molecular chemistry because the biological field that saturates the territory enhances every organism’s existing capabilities. The resort’s performer risks losing herself because the technique that produces genuine emotional response in an audience uses the same cognitive machinery as genuine emotional experience, and the line between performing and becoming has been erased by the planar expression of the swamp.

This world was built from the inside out. The cosmology produces the physics. The physics produces the ecology. The ecology produces the culture. The culture produces the people. The people produce the stories. At every level, the detail you encounter is a consequence of the level beneath it. Nothing was placed because it seemed cool. Everything grows from the architecture.
## <a name="_heading=h.jzs4502zoul4"></a>**Two Worlds That Became One**
The setting you are entering is called Tessera. It is a mosaic — a single world assembled from the fragments of two that collided. One was ancient: sword-and-sorcery, divine mandate, the oral tradition as scripture. The other was modern: diesel engines, industrial manufacturing, a world war fought with weapons that combined technology and magic. The collision — the Calamity — killed more people simultaneously than any event before or since. What survived the merger became Tessera.

The merged world should not work. Two incompatible physics, two incompatible histories, two incompatible theologies occupying the same material space should produce incoherent nonsense. It doesn’t. The world feels natural. Seamless. As if it had always been this way. Residents of Tessera do not walk around marvelling at the blend of ancient temples and industrial infrastructure the way a tourist would. They live inside it. It’s home.

The reason it works is the setting’s deepest structural secret: a god is holding it together. The Smoothing Effect — the continuous, invisible, cosmological-scale intervention that makes the merged reality feel coherent — is maintained by the Overdeity through mechanisms that almost nobody in the world understands and almost everybody in the world depends on. The seams are real. They are hidden. The institutions that hide them believe they are protecting the world. They are probably right. The cost of that protection is the setting’s central question.


# <a name="_heading=h.f3aare6dzwwi"></a>**What the Civilisation Built**
The world’s institutions grew from the merged reality the way coral grows from a reef. Eight colleges — each one a university, a government, and a philosophy made institutional — occupy the landscape like organs in a body. Each one manages a different dimension of the civilisation’s function. Each one trains practitioners through an initiation process that permanently changes how they perceive reality. Each one produces graduates who carry that change into every corner of the world.

The colleges are not factions. They are not teams you pick at character creation. They are the civilisation’s operating system — the institutions that keep the lights on, the dead processed, the walls warded, the economy functioning, the soldiers trained, the knowledge archived, the art produced, and the reality coherent. You will interact with all of them. You will discover that each one is simultaneously doing essential work and producing institutional damage as a byproduct of that work. You will not be asked to choose which one is right. You will be asked to notice what each one costs.

***The College of the Dead***

Manages the afterlife’s bureaucracy. Processes souls. Maintains the queue. Operates out of an oasis between two magical deserts at the site of the Calamity itself. The society here speaks formally because the ancestors can hear you. Death is scheduled at middle age. A commoner’s passing is a line item planned years in advance. The college’s deepest archive holds preserved souls that cannot be released — a dam holding back the river of the dead that fills one soul at a time with no mechanism for emptying.

***The Mountain of Contracts***

Built inside a mountain range by engineers who believed the system needed a counterweight to the church’s monopoly on the dead. A shopkeeper’s lease is a magical instrument whose breach conditions fire automatically. Children learn the difference between casual speech and binding statement before they learn long division. The criminal ecology exploits contractual gaps the way water exploits cracks in stone, and the institutional response is better drafting, and the arms race between them is the civilisation’s most productive professional dynamic.

***The Serene City***

The love potion at civilisational scale. A woman of immense psionic capability arrived with small, warm creatures whose presence makes people feel better. Not metaphorically. Measurably. The creatures spread. The therapeutic effect spread. The city that grew around them is the safest, healthiest, most cooperative settlement on the clock. The cost is measured in autonomous selfhood — the gradual, comfortable, clinically beneficial erosion of the capacity to make fully uninfluenced decisions. The medicine and the dependency are chemically identical. She knows this. She considers it an acceptable price.

***The Beautiful Lie***

A swamp transformed into the most curated environment in the world. The resort works. The performances move people. The food is extraordinary. Behind the glamoured promenades, a working society of technicians and stagehands lives in utilitarian corridors, eats honest food on tin plates, and maintains the infrastructure of beauty at three in the morning. The headmaster is a fictional character who achieved consciousness through collective belief and is now more metaphysically real than the people who created it. It studies how the Overdeity built reality the way a graduate student studies a masterwork.

***The Market City***

Three million people and the closest thing to a universal currency. The port exists because the forest is right there and somebody has to be the interface between civilisation and maximum-biological-transformation ecology. The commerce is genuine. The fair-trade protocols are genuine. The information asymmetry that makes every interaction at the casino a data-collection event is also genuine. The front door to the world’s most secure location is operated by people who do not know what is behind it.

***The Living Forest***

The college of last resort. No filter on applicants. The desperate, the broken, the wanted, the lost. The forest offers the same terms to everyone: transform or feed the soil. The ones who survive become immortal operators who retrieve lost souls from across the planes, contain things the forest produces that cannot be killed, and carry an obligation that has not ended in centuries and will not end. The ones who fail become the things the survivors contain. A mentor requesting support for a containment operation is requesting it because the alternative is doing it to someone who was their student for three hundred years.

***The Forge of Soldiers***

How do you kill something that cannot die? You separate the soul from the body. You ship the stone to the death-processing college. You deal with what is left. The entire zone is organised around this cycle. The mesa’s perpetual storms are not weather — they are planar expression, all four elements in permanent conflict. The multinational cadet body trains inside conditions that are actively trying to kill all of them equally. The bonds this produces are the strongest in the setting. The memorial markers at every intersection are not shrines. They became shrines through accumulation.

***The Calculus of Fate***

A dormant volcano whose gases produce altered perceptual states. The pilgrims came first, centuries before anyone built an institution. They sat in the gas and they saw things that had not happened yet. The institute systematised the experience without replacing it. The probability field amplifies the most likely outcome of anything observed within it. The casino’s house edge is architectural. The resort’s hot springs diagnose conditions the guest didn’t know they had. The labyrinth beneath the caldera restructures itself between observations. And the intelligence apparatus that monitors the entire civilisation from behind the resort’s facade is the setting’s second spy agency, locked in a cold war with the first.

***The Centre***

The immune system. The institution that monitors every other institution, maintains the infrastructure of shared education across the entire civilisation, and holds the deepest structural secret: the merged world’s coherence is manufactured. The graduates who complete every other college’s initiation — who hold all eight philosophical frameworks simultaneously — develop the ability to read what reality is moving toward because they have internalised every axis the architecture operates on. They act autonomously. They do not receive orders. They perceive the world’s grain and align with it. They are the closest thing this setting has to paladins, and their divine connection is an emergent property of comprehensive philosophical integration, not a granted power.


# <a name="_heading=h.kl2ole5k1u0c"></a>**What the Civilisation Costs**
This is where the setting departs from everything else on the shelf.

The civilisation works. The institutions serve. The justice is imperfect but real. The commerce is genuinely fair. The people are genuinely selfless. The infrastructure genuinely functions. And the human cost is still unsustainable.

The system runs on duty. Every position on the clock carries significant obligation. The soldiers who hold the corridor. The humanitarian workers who staff the schools and clinics. The intelligence operatives who monitor the seams. The chirurgeons who process the dead. The wardens who contain the forest’s failures. The performers who risk identity dissolution every time they step on stage. Each one gives something of themselves to maintain the world, and the world does not have a mechanism for giving it back.

The setting’s most diagnostic finding: the civilisation that maintains itself through the selfless duty of people it does not adequately care for.

This is not a dark finding. This is the honest finding. The struggle is not an obstacle to the golden age. The struggle is the golden age. The people who maintain the world are the world’s most valuable product and its most consumed resource, simultaneously, and the resolution to that tension is not provided because the resolution is what you bring to the table.


# <a name="_heading=h.mheivolq01d7"></a>**What You Do Here**
You play a person living inside this. Not a hero summoned by prophecy. Not a chosen one marked by a god. A person with a competency, a history, a set of choices already made that the system reads backward to determine who you have become. Your identity is not declared at session zero. It crystallises from the pattern of your decisions, recognised in retrospect like a geological stratum — you were always becoming this. You just didn’t see it until the pattern was thick enough to read.

The game’s mechanical grammar is unified. Magic, talent, heritage, mutation, and equipment all operate through the same system. An effect that heals a wound and an effect that forges a sword and an effect that reads a mind and an effect that transforms your body all use the same sentence structure, the same scaling tables, the same resolution mechanics. The system has no special cases. The rules that govern a cantrip govern a civilisation-scale intervention. The difference is magnitude, not kind. When you understand one part of the system, you understand every part because every part is the same part at a different scale.

The setting’s political architecture is a cycle, not a spectrum. Sixteen positions arranged in a ring, each expressing a legitimate political philosophy, each producing genuine benefits and genuine institutional damage. The cycle does not progress from bad to good. It does not decline from golden age to collapse. It turns. Every position is simultaneously someone’s golden age and someone’s crisis. The civilisation that builds the strongest walls is the civilisation that cannot see past them. The civilisation that maximises individual freedom is the civilisation that cannot coordinate a response to a threat. The civilisation that manages information most effectively is the civilisation whose people cannot tell whether they are being served or controlled. None of these are wrong. None of them are right. Each is a legitimate answer to a genuine problem, and the cost of each answer is the problem the next position exists to address.

You walk into this world carrying whatever you bring. Your own politics, your own values, your own answer to the question of what a civilisation owes the people who maintain it. The setting will not tell you which answer is correct. The setting will show you eight institutions, each one doing essential work, each one consuming the people who do it, and it will ask you: what do you do about this? Not what do you think about this. What do you do?

Your action is your answer. Your accumulated actions become your legacy. Your legacy is read backward by a system that recognises patterns the way a geologist recognises strata — not because you declared your identity but because your choices deposited it, layer by layer, and the pattern became legible.


# <a name="_heading=h.64fipyex921d"></a>**Why This Matters**
The world does not need another setting where the dark lord threatens the kingdom and the heroes save it. The world does not need another setting where the system is broken and the players fix it. The world does not need another setting that presents a problem and delivers the answer in the back of the book.

Tessera is a world that presents the problem and trusts you to hold it. Not solve it. Hold it. The way an adult holds a genuine dilemma — understanding that both sides are real, that the cost is real, that the people paying the cost are worth caring about, and that your engagement with the problem is the only resolution the problem will ever receive.

The game is the engagement. The setting is the problem. The answer is yours.



*The mosaic is assembled from broken pieces. Each piece was whole once. The beauty is in how they fit together now.*





\# WELCOME TO TESSERA

\### A Player's Introduction to the Ætherium Codex

\---

The war ended a generation ago. Nobody agrees on exactly when, or exactly who won, but the factories are still running and the propaganda posters are still up, and the soldiers who came home are still trying to figure out what to do with themselves. That part, at least, feels familiar.

What doesn't feel familiar is everything else.

The world you're stepping into is called Tessera. On the surface, it looks like the kind of place you've seen before — knights and merchants, scholars and thieves, cities with walls and roads between them. Look a little closer and you'll notice the knight has a sidearm. The merchant is pricing cargo through an interplanar shipping lane. The scholar's college sits atop a mesa that's been quietly humming with power for centuries, and nobody in the upper ranks talks about what's underneath it.

Tessera is a post-war world that hasn't quite figured out what it is yet. The technology of a war effort — assembly lines, mass-produced enchantments, Kan-tech that can level a city block — coexists uneasily with ancient noble houses whose power comes from bloodlines nobody has fully explained. The culture is 1950s in its bones: desperate for normalcy, deeply traumatized, telling itself the worst is behind it. The infrastructure is 1940s in its body: built for conflict, repurposed for peace, with all the scars that implies.

Magic runs through everything here, but not evenly. There are places where it pools — called Planar Bleeds — where the boundary between this world and others has worn thin. In those places, things mutate. Creatures adapt or don't. People who spend too long there start to change in ways that don't fully reverse. This isn't a secret, exactly. It's more like something everyone knows and nobody thinks about too hard, the way people in certain cities don't think too hard about the water.

Nine great colleges govern the use of magic and, through it, most of what matters in the world. Each one controls something essential. The necromancers handle death — not dramatically, not evilly, but administratively. They run the resurrection queue. When you die, assuming you're carrying a soul stone, your soul anchors there and waits to be processed. Standard tier gets you a skeleton body until someone pays for the biological upgrade. Premium tier gets your original body back on a timeline that depends on your subscription. Death is a bureaucracy here, and the chirurgeons who staff it started raising themselves centuries ago because they were dying of exhaustion at the operating table and the work wasn't finished. Undeath is the ultimate overtime.

The conjurers run the ports and the shipping lanes. The abjurers run the vaults and the contracts — if a Deepguard-certified agreement says you'll do something, you will do it, and the magical enforcement mechanism is not metaphorical. The enchanters run the largest asylum in the world and also, quietly, the mood of the largest city in the world. The illusionists run the entertainment industry and a number of things that are technically entertainment. The evokers train every military officer in every nation on the continent, which means when nations go to war, both sides are running the same playbook. The transmuters take the students nobody else wanted and run them through a forest until something either emerges or doesn't. The diviners study fate and probability and run, as a sideline, the most profitable casino in the world. And the generalists sit in the middle of all of it, study all of it, and quietly keep the whole arrangement from collapsing.

You are an adventurer. This is a job description that exists because the world needs people willing to go into the places that are too dangerous, too unstable, or too legally ambiguous for institutional actors. Guilds organize this work. They started as a betting system — locals placing wagers on which parties would make it back from the deep dives — and evolved into something more elaborate when the money got serious enough. There's now a futures market on adventuring parties at Port Janus. You can be shorted. This is not a metaphor.

The gods are real here and most people have met one. Or met someone who met one. They walk around. They have faces. They have opinions. The god of crafting is specifically the god of what it costs to make things, and his mythology is a set of oral traditions that vary by profession because every craft tells the story of his wound differently. The god of commerce accepts cursed currency when no one else will, and uses the curse itself as a backing asset for a separate black-market economy. The gods of love are seven of them, a polycule of divine entities covering everything from passionate romance to the love you have for someone who will never love you back, and they are explicitly co-directors of mortal emotional life alongside the Fates, who are the ones that cut the thread when they decide you've learned enough.

You have a soul stone. You've had it since childhood, probably. It means that when you die, you don't immediately go wherever people go — you get held in the stone and processed back into the world, usually. The soul stone is a standard feature of life in Tessera the way health insurance is a standard feature of employment somewhere else, with similar questions about what happens when you can't afford the premium tier.

You also have Fulcrum Points. Everyone does. They're not magic, exactly — they're more like the universe's acknowledgment that some people bend probability around them through force of narrative weight. You can spend them to make the unlikely happen, to turn a near-miss into a hit, or, when things get truly desperate, to survive something that should have killed you. The cost of that last one is that something changes. The universe kept you alive, but it took something in exchange.

What kind of world is this? It is a world that has been through something. The architecture of normalcy is intact — markets, governance, religion, education — but it's load-bearing in ways the people living inside it don't fully understand. The colleges are doing things the public doesn't know about. The institutions have shadow functions that contradict their public missions. The history that everyone agrees on is missing pieces, and the pieces that are missing are the ones that would explain why certain things are the way they are.

There are people who have seen behind the curtain. Most of them don't come back from that in good shape. The ones who do tend to end up in places where their particular kind of broken is an asset rather than a liability.

That's you. Or it will be.

Welcome to Tessera. You've got a soul stone, a character sheet, and a set of skills that someone, somewhere, decided were worth paying for. The apocalypse already happened. The next one is optional.

Let's find out if you can make it optional.

\---

\*The Ætherium Codex uses a d20 resolution system. Your character is defined by four pillars — Physical, Mental, Social, and Metaphysical — each of which can be damaged, depleted, and repaired. You can die. Death is temporary, usually. What you become on the other side of enough of it is the interesting question.\*

*—*
# <a name="_heading=h.hyz0p4qzcq17"></a>**THE ÆTHERIUM CODEX — THE ENVIRONMENTAL ENGINE**
## <a name="_heading=h.jt3znjpt48b5"></a>**Document 5 (Revised): Tessera as Active Participant**
### <a name="_heading=h.1vsfe95agr4x"></a>**Canonical Draft — April 17, 2026**
-----
## <a name="_heading=h.z75n6nlt14o0"></a>**PREAMBLE**
Tessera’s environment is not a backdrop. It is an active participant using the same 23+Variable effect vocabulary as every character, spell, and item. The environment operates through the same engine as combat — it has condition tracks, produces effects through isotope expression at Formation Stages that function identically to ESL, and participates in the nested clock architecture.

This document describes what the dissolved solution of three merged realities looks like at ground level, how its geological and meteorological systems produce the materials and creatures of the setting, how the three setting expressions (A/B/C) interact with environmental bleed, and how the entire system operates mechanically at the table.

-----
# <a name="_heading=h.jm6s2vk631xi"></a>**PART I: THE DISSOLVED SOLUTION**
## <a name="_heading=h.yy2n93c0pvf9"></a>**What Tessera IS**
Tessera is three competing reality-states dissolved into a single coherent world by the Smoothing Effect. The Smoothing Effect is not glue — it is a solvent. It dissolved the boundaries between incompatible realities so they could find equilibrium.

**Setting A** contributes ancient geography, intuitive magical tradition, sword-and-sorcery ecosystems, and the Elder Fey.

**Setting B** contributes industrial infrastructure, Kan-tech engineering, logic-based magical systematization, and the Lost Fey.

**The Third Blending (Setting C / The Present)** is the messy, vibrant fusion — the current geopolitical landscape, represented by the Newborn Fey and the hybrid cultures that have grown from the merge.

The blend IS peace. The default state is cohesion. The threat is not the clock striking midnight — it’s someone trying to SEPARATE the solution back into component parts, thinking they’re “fixing” it. The unknowing bad actors are the purists on either side who see the blend as contamination rather than equilibrium.
## <a name="_heading=h.25xbizxz13ji"></a>**The Dual Currents**
### <a name="_heading=h.atyk7dbejw36"></a>**The Primal Current (Anima’s Influence)**
Raw, chaotic lifeblood emanating from the planet’s core, unique ecosystems, and geologically embedded magical materials.

**Sources:** Natural Wellsprings (geothermal-like vents), Reality Seams (where Setting A and B geology merge), Planar Infusions (areas saturated by bleed from one of the five planes).

The Primal Current IS environmental Spell Sentence expression. A Wellspring producing B-03 Slag at Formation Stage 6 is mechanically identical to a caster producing Damage (B-03 Slag) at ESL 6 — the geology itself is the transmission medium.
### <a name="_heading=h.iv331ijs41lp"></a>**The Structured Current (Kanon’s Influence)**
Predictable, logical framework derived from the “waste radiation” of the Grand Orrery of Ætherna. The ambient byproduct of the engine of Fate.

**Expression — Ley Lines:** Arteries of magical flow, not sources. Ley lines don’t CREATE magical energy — they ROUTE it. The distinction matters for infrastructure: civilizations build ALONG ley lines for the routing benefit, not ON ley lines for the generation.

—

\# Welcome to Tessera

\### An Introduction to the Ætherium Codex Narrative Engine

\---

\## What You're Looking At

You're looking at a world that shouldn't exist.

Not in the dramatic sense — in the literal, cosmological, physics-violating sense. Two incompatible realities were shattered by a catastrophe called the Calamity, and an entity called Aíon — the Overdeity, something beyond the gods — stitched the surviving fragments into a single functioning world rather than letting both dissolve into entropy. The world is called Tessera. It is held together by something called the Smoothing Effect: a cosmic maintenance process that papers over the contradictions between the two source realities, normalizes the impossible, and keeps the population sane enough to keep reality's narrative weight sustained.

The first source reality — call it Setting A — was ancient sword-and-sorcery. Deep forests. Living gods walking among mortals. Magic as an innate, breathing thing tied to the land and the soul. Bronze and iron and stone, and the slow geological weight of civilizations measuring themselves in millennia.

The second source reality — call it Setting B — was the 1940s. Industrial warfare. Diesel engines. Kan-tech: the fusion of runic magic with vacuum tubes, mechanical fire-control systems, and armored half-tracks. A world that had just discovered how to put magic through a factory and make it reproducible.

In Tessera, both are simultaneously true and have been for long enough that nobody finds this strange.

A heavily armored knight dismounts from a diesel-chugging, runic-stamped half-track. A student studies the somatic components of a fireball while adjusting targeting vacuum tubes. Guns exist. So do dragons. The post-war reconstruction economy runs alongside feudal land-holding law. This is not treated as remarkable. It is Tuesday.

You experience the friction. The inhabitants don't.

That friction is the setting's engine.

\---

\## The Great Lie

There is a reason the inhabitants don't notice. The Smoothing Effect doesn't just maintain physical coherence — it maintains narrative coherence. It tells the world a story about itself that makes the impossible feel mundane, and the mortals who believe that story provide the conceptual gravity that keeps it true. Belief is structural in Tessera. It has mechanical weight. The gods hold their positions because mortal souls, through the act of living within a god's domain, provide the philosophical backing that makes that god's relevance real. Stop believing in commerce, and Valorin weakens. Stop enacting sovereignty, and Aurelion's position erodes.

The Great Lie is the public version of this: a coherent history, a continuous narrative, a world that seems to have always been exactly this way. The Generalist College — the academic institution called Praxis Collegium at the center of the political landscape — maintains and distributes this narrative as a function of civilizational stability. Not out of malice. Out of necessity. The raw truth of what Tessera is would produce mass cognitive dissonance, and mass cognitive dissonance would begin to unravel the Smoothing Effect, and an unraveling Smoothing Effect would return the world to entropy. The Great Lie is the roof over everyone's head. The people maintaining it are not villains. They are load-bearing.

The Great Truth is available, but it is reserved for those with the status, power, or raw trauma to perceive the disruptions that the Smoothing Effect cannot fully obscure. The player characters begin as people who do not know. The arc of the game is the slow, careful raising of the temperature — the burned-out archivist muttering about the Seams, the strange ruins that belong to a history that doesn't match the official record, the moment when the geography simply doesn't add up — until the characters have accumulated enough reality to perceive what they're standing inside.

\---

\## The Material Foundation

Here is where the system gets precise.

Every physical substance in Tessera — every rock, every pool of water, every living tissue, every crystal lattice in a machine — has a planar identity. The world is saturated with bleed from five outer planes, and the materials that form under that bleed carry its character permanently. This is not magic layered on top of physics. It is physics.

The five planes are:

\*\*The Borderlands\*\* is the engine of matter. Stone, lightning, fire, frost, acid — the classical elements, but expressed as forces in their most fundamental form. A Borderlands material is defined by what it physically does, how it moves, what it consumes or resists. The Borderlands is what makes the physical laws of Tessera feel consistent even when everything else is in flux.

\*\*The Verdant Font\*\* is the engine of life. Not just biology — the organizing principle that produces complexity from chemistry, agency from matter, and predation from hunger. The Font contains everything from the World Tree's permanent living structure to the Titan's impossible biological scale to the Chimera's mid-species transformation. The Font is Darwinism made cosmological. It does not care about individuals. It cares about the continuation and diversification of the pattern.

\*\*The Synod of Endings\*\* is the engine of death. But death in Tessera is not nothing — it is the most permanent state, the final record, the absolute stillness against which all motion is measured. The Synod contains bone and void and memory and echo. Its materials preserve. They record. They drain. They express the full spectrum of what remains after the vital force stops.

\*\*The Hidden Forge\*\* is the engine of technology. It is the most unusual of the five because it has no antithetical — nothing in existence is fundamentally opposed to the principle of making things work. The Forge pairs naturally with every other plane because that is its nature: the universal mediator, the plane that finds the functional interface between all other expressions. Its materials are crystal lattices and chemical compounds and electrical circuits and pressurized mechanisms. It is the plane that produced Kan-tech. In the broader cosmological sense, it is the reason that Setting B's industrial magic and Setting A's elemental magic are able to coexist in Tessera at all.

\*\*The Palimpsest\*\* is the engine of narrative. Probability, memory, fate, glitch — the plane that governs the relationship between cause and effect, the gap between what physics predicts and what the story requires. The Palimpsest is why Tessera feels like it has a narrative gravity: certain events want to happen; certain endings feel inevitable; certain objects appear when they are needed. The Smoothing Effect is a Palimpsest expression applied at cosmological scale.

Every material in Tessera is anchored to one of these planes. Not metaphorically — the planar bleed that permeates certain geographic zones saturates the materials forming there, and those materials emit a continuous resonance that contributes to the regional magical atmosphere. A region dominated by strong Verdant Font bleed produces biological abundance and rapid mutation. A region under Synod pressure is cold, still, and dense with accumulated memory. The landscape reads like a periodic table entry if you know how to look.

\---

\## The Alchemical Periodic Table

This is the system's most important structural element.

Each of the five planes has an internal phase wheel — four expressions organized around the classical phase states of matter. The Borderlands runs Earth, Water, Air, Fire. The Verdant Font runs Flora, Myco, Viral, Fauna. The Synod runs Osseous, Archival, Spectral, Void. The Hidden Forge runs Crystal, Chem, Draft, Volt. The Palimpsest runs Echo, Veil, Probability, Glitch.

Each of those phase expressions is a specific material state. Earth is the Bedrock — absolute geological permanence. Fauna is the Hunt — pure predatory biological drive. Void is Oblivion — entropy made locally manifest. Volt is the Grid — living electricity as environmental fact. Glitch is Error — physics refusing its own rules. Each one has a physical description, an emission character (the field it continuously radiates), and a tier scaling that describes how the material's properties intensify from Tier 1 through Tier 3.

That's 80 single-plane isotopes. They are the atoms of the system.

When two compatible planes bleed into the same geographic space, their isotopes combine. The combination isn't additive — it's relational. The primary plane defines the noun: what the thing fundamentally IS. The secondary plane defines the verb: how that nature acts. Borderlands primary + Verdant Font secondary produces Animism — elements with biological agency, fire that hunts, water that bleeds. Verdant Font primary + Borderlands secondary produces Adaptive Flesh — organisms that generate elemental expression as a biological function, the creature with electric breath rather than the electrical storm that pursues prey.

This is Godzilla vs. the Bioelectric Storm. Same components. Different primary.

The combination space runs to 5,120 cross-planar isotope states before you account for the two antithetical pairs — the plane combinations that cannot coexist naturally.

Life and death are antithetical. The Verdant Font's relentless forward momentum and the Synod's absolute permanence cannot occupy the same space without contradiction. When forced into the same material by the Hidden Forge's catalytic function, you get the Biotic Paradox: Amber Stasis, The Bloom, The Iron Lung, The Galvanic. Four different binding mechanisms depending on which Forge isotope is doing the mediation.

Matter and fate are antithetical. The Borderlands' consistent physical law and the Palimpsest's narrative logic cannot simultaneously be true. The rock falls because physics. The rock doesn't fall because the story requires it not to. Both cannot be true in the same material without the Forge holding them in forced coexistence. The Glitch-Spire, Alchemy, Clockwork Fate, Hard Light.

This is where the system's alchemy principle becomes explicit. The real periodic table existed in the 1940s. Tessera's Setting B characters have it. Deepguard engineers and Chronosynclastic researchers work from it as standard institutional knowledge. The alchemical periodic table we've built here describes the same material reality from the other direction — the symbolic-philosophical framework that Setting A's practitioners use to describe what the Setting B scientists call chemistry. Historical alchemy produced gunpowder, acids, and early metallurgy precisely because it was describing real chemical interactions before the scientific vocabulary existed to name them precisely. In Tessera, both descriptions are available simultaneously. The alchemist and the chemist are working the same seam from opposite sides.

\---

\## The Civilization Overlay

Every material also carries a political stamp.

The civilization that controls the geographic zone where a material forms, harvests it, and distributes it expresses its political character through that material. This isn't an aesthetic detail — it determines who has institutional access, what cultural resonance the object carries, and which power structures have a legitimate claim on it.

The political alloy system has three components: governance structure (who holds the leash), economic model (how short the leash is), and ethos (why the leash is there at all). Twenty-four governance forms, twenty economic models, twenty ethical frameworks. Every civilization that has ever existed in Tessera can be expressed as a combination of these three, and because the geography determines which materials are available, and the available materials constrain which economic models are viable, and the economic model shapes which governance structures can sustain themselves, and the sustainable governance develops an ethos that reflects how that combination of conditions actually feels to live in — the political alloy is downstream of the landscape.

Stormpeak exists where it is because the Stonefang Mountains produce sustained Borderlands electrical bleed at maximum intensity. That material geography produced Stormpeak's economy, which produced its military governance, which produced its combat-excellence ethos, which resonates with Bellum Hegemon's productive-conflict aspect, which generates the specific character archetype the institution selects for and venerates. The combination is not arbitrary. It is determined by the planar conditions of the land.

Every material object in Tessera therefore carries all of this simultaneously: its planar identity, its cross-planar combination (if applicable), its tier, and its civilization of origin. A blade made at Stormpeak from a cross-planar Borderlands-primary / Verdant Font-secondary combination at Tier 2, catalyzed with a Volt emulsion and stamped with the Stratocracy's Capability-Exporting Agonist alloy — that blade carries the full history of its origin in its material description. The code on the pommel tells you who made it, what conditions produced the material, which gods have a stake in its existence, and what kind of character is likely to be carrying it.

\---

\## The Gods and the Archetypes

The gods of Tessera are not persons in the conventional sense. They are the dominant philosophical positions on the questions their domain addresses, held in position by the weight of souls actively living within those questions.

Aurelion holds the domain of sovereignty not because he was appointed to it, but because the majority expression of how mortals currently answer the question "what makes authority legitimate?" aligns with his aspect. If that majority expression shifts, his position erodes. A god is proof of concept made manifest. The divine aspects — each god has multiple, representing the competing answers within their domain — are the real unit of cosmological weight. Aurelion's Luxos aspect (the ideal of sovereignty) and his Ignislor aspect (the real, the enacted) are in constant tension because mortals constantly argue about whether authority should be what it claims to be or what it demonstrably is.

This means the gods are legible. Their positions on the Psyche Schema — the 39-point constellation of divine aspects that defines a character's metaphysical profile — can be read from behavior. A character who consistently enacts the principles of craft and honest accounting of what making costs is performing an act of faith in Daedalon's domain whether they know Daedalon exists or not. Veneration in Tessera is not worship. It is the act of living within a god's portfolio.

The archetypes are the stellar phenomena.

A character built from a specific combination of planar material origin, political alloy, divine resonance, and life experience produces a recognizable pattern — the Warlord, the Scholar-Knight, the Archive Custodian, the Biopath, the Void-Walker. These are not classes in the conventional game sense. They are the constellations that appear when you look at the underlying invisible forces from a specific angle. The archetype of the Scholar-Knight is the superposition of Technocratic governance, Rent-Extracting economics, Integrity ethos, Deepguard planar suppression, and Kanon-dominant magic syntax aligned into a coherent visible pattern from ground level. The pattern recurs wherever those conditions align. King Arthur is that pattern expressed through a different political alloy and a different historical moment, which is why the legend persists across cultures that never contacted each other.

The system is generative in the full sense. Not because it lists every combination, but because it gives you the underlying organizing principles from which every combination can be derived.

\---

\## What You Can Do With This

If you're playing the game, your character is built from these layers. Your backstory is not flavor — it is a specification. Where you were born determines which planar materials were available in your environment. Which institution trained you determines your political alloy and your economic model. Which divine aspects your life has enacted most consistently determines where you sit on the Psyche Schema. The mechanics that govern your character in play — your attributes, your skills, the talents you've developed — are the downstream expression of these upstream facts.

If you're reading the novel, the same system governs everything you're experiencing. The reason the knight's half-track feels lived-in rather than anachronistic is that the material culture of Tessera was constructed from first principles rather than assembled from genre tropes. The setting makes internal sense even when it violates genre sense, because the internal logic is coherent. The Smoothing Effect is real and the Great Lie is load-bearing and you will not understand what you're standing inside until you've accumulated enough reality to see the seams.

If you're a designer or a writer building in this world, the system is the generator. Every city, every faction, every artifact, every creature has a material and political and divine specification that you can derive from first principles rather than inventing from nothing. The nine great colleges on the Zoetrope clock are not nine arbitrary factions. They are nine specific intersections of planar material geography, political alloy, and divine resonance that produce stable institutional expressions at those positions in the civilizational lifecycle. Every other faction in Tessera can be specified the same way.

The periodic table is the engine. The archetypes are the visible output. Everything in between is the system working.

\---

\## Where You Are

You're standing at the entrance to Praxis Collegium.

It is the closest thing Tessera has to a neutral hub — the academic institution at the center of the political clock that interfaces with all eight outer colleges without being captured by any of them. It is staffed by people who understand, at varying levels of clarity, that they are load-bearing members of the Great Lie. They are not villains. They are doing what they must.

Around you: a heavily armored knight is arguing with an administrator about procurement paperwork for a replacement treads assembly. A student in robes is annotating a fireball formula in the margins of a Kan-tech manual. Somewhere in the archive wing, a burned-out researcher is writing something on the walls about Seams and Smoothing and the fact that the world is not what it claims to be.

Nobody is paying him much attention.

You haven't learned to yet either.

That will change.

\---

\*The Ætherium Codex is a narrative engine TTRPG and fiction setting currently in active development.\*

—

# <a name="_heading=h.hw1oqv8cs6nr"></a>**PART II: LEY LINES AND FLOW STATES**
## <a name="_heading=h.fiolvvtzdw75"></a>**Laminar and Turbulent Flow**
Every ley line exists in one of two fundamental states:
### <a name="_heading=h.51449peavgk"></a>**Laminar Flow**
Stable, predictable. The magical current runs smoothly through the channel. The planar influence is evenly distributed — low saturation variance across the area. Like a well-stirred solution.

**Mechanical Effect:** Consistent environmental expression. What you measure at one point, you measure everywhere in the zone. Reliable Kan-tech operation. Predictable material formation. Stable ecology. Civilizations build here because the environment is manageable.
### <a name="_heading=h.8nyxaoymisaa"></a>**Turbulent Flow**
Chaotic, unpredictable. The planar influence is unevenly distributed — concentrated in pockets, surges, and eddies. Like an unmixed solution with clumps.

**Mechanical Effect:** Inconsistent environmental expression. You might walk three steps and go from Formation Stage 2 to Formation Stage 8 and back. The danger isn’t randomness for its own sake — it’s that you can’t predict where the concentration spikes are. A fighter steps into an unseen high-saturation pocket and is suddenly under Stage 8 mutation pressure.
### <a name="_heading=h.wt2lws25wjdl"></a>**The Resonance Principle**
What determines laminar vs turbulent isn’t just the ley line itself — it’s the COMPATIBILITY between the geological terrain and the planar bleed flowing through it.

**Matching geology + matching bleed = laminar, high intensity.** The terrain and the bleed share the same principle. The signal transmits cleanly. Maximum saturation, minimum turbulence.

**Mismatched geology + opposing bleed = turbulent, reduced intensity.** The terrain resists the signal. Lower saturation overall but UNEVEN — pockets where the bleed wins and pockets where the geology wins, creating the turbulence. The boundary between these pockets is where unusual cross-planar combinations form.
## <a name="_heading=h.8f9trugdsuid"></a>**Intensity Scale**

|Intensity|Description|Result|
| :- | :- | :- |
|**Trace**|Background magical field. Everywhere in Tessera.|Baseline. All characters can feel it. Praxis can read it. Detectable but not hazardous.|
|**Current**|Standard ley line intensity. Functional infrastructure.|Laminar: enhanced casting, reliable item function. Turbulent: occasional surges, minor hazard.|
|**Torrent**|Disaster-level intensity. Ætheric Cascade.|Laminar: maximum expression of the shared principle (see Part III). Turbulent: full wild magic storm. Environmental Damage at Formation Stage 8+.|

-----
# <a name="_heading=h.9ubcbn6thram"></a>**PART III: GEOLOGY-BLEED RESONANCE — THE NINE COLLEGES AS PROOF**
The colleges didn’t choose their locations arbitrarily. They positioned themselves where the geology RESONATES with their planar affinity. Each college is an existence proof of the resonance model.
## <a name="_heading=h.3mbcukuwdf8y"></a>**The College Positions**
**Mortis Ævum (Synod) — Oasis between two magically sustained hot/cold deserts.** Desert IS the Synod’s expression. Entropy, preservation through desiccation, endings made visible. The deserts are sustained by the Synod bleed itself — the magic creates the environment that resonates with the magic. Maximum laminar resonance produces maximum Synod expression: preservation is absolute, entropy operates at peak efficiency, nothing new begins because endings are perfected. The oasis at center is the livable pocket between two extremes of the same expression.

This site is also the CENTER OF THE CALAMITY — the oldest, most intense, and most stable Reality Seam in Tessera. See Part IX.

**Deepguard (Hidden Forge) — Deep mountains.** Mountains ARE geological pressure and stability. The Forge IS catalysis through pressure. Deep mountain geology is Forge-resonant terrain — maximum depth, maximum pressure, maximum stability. Deepguard buries itself in the most compatible geology possible.

**Unity (Palimpsest boundary) — NYC meets Montana metropolis.** The city at the edge of wilderness IS the Palimpsest boundary made geographic. Civilization (managed narrative) directly adjacent to untamed space (raw probability). A metropolis is humanity’s strongest narrative assertion — “this IS civilization” — pressed against wilderness that doesn’t care about narratives. Sim-stims are produced here, not at Chiaroscuro.

**Chiaroscuro (Palimpsest) — Swamp.** Liminal terrain. Neither land nor water. Nothing is what it appears. The surface conceals the depth. This IS Illusion as terrain. The geology resonates with the Palimpsest because both are about surfaces being unreliable. Fog, shifting ground, hidden depth, misleading paths.

**Sovereign Exchange (Borderlands + Forge) — Port city by ocean.** Ports ARE exchange nodes. The ocean IS the Borderlands’ most dynamic expression. Commerce happens at the interface between land and water because that’s where materials accumulate and redistribute. The Forge component is the manufactured infrastructure catalyzing the exchange.

**Titanwood / Wildpath Wardens (Verdant Font) — Dense forest / rainforest.** The forest IS the Verdant Font’s most direct expression. Maximum biological diversity. Maximum transformation. The ecology IS the planar expression. Maximum laminar resonance here means MAXIMUM LIFE — explosive growth, constant transformation, stasis is impossible.

**Stormpeak (Borderlands) — Mesa with storms.** Exposed strata, elemental erosion, the bones of the earth laid bare. And it STORMS. Lightning, rain, wind, flash floods. Water IS there — storm runoff, seasonal flooding. The Borderlands’ atmospheric expression hammering the Borderlands’ geological expression. Maximum laminar resonance here means MAXIMUM ELEMENTAL FORCE.

**Chronosynclastic Institute (Palimpsest + Synod interface) — Dormant volcano.** A volcano that COULD erupt but hasn’t. The event exists in potential but hasn’t collapsed into actuality. This IS temporal potential held in suspension — the geological equivalent of precognition. The geology resonates because the volcano IS a probability waiting to collapse.

**Praxis Collegium (none / Generalist) — Farmland + interstitial center.** Managed ecology — not wild, not urban, not elemental. The space between extremes, cultivated and maintained. Praxis sits at the center of the Zoetrope clock CATCHING AND CODIFYING all bleeds that flow between the other positions as they move centerward or to the outer edges. Their geology is deliberately neutral — moderate resonance with everything, strong resonance with nothing.
## <a name="_heading=h.wmidqav0525p"></a>**What Maximum Laminar Resonance Produces**
Maximum laminar stability doesn’t produce universal stasis. It produces the MAXIMUM EXPRESSION of whatever principle the geology and bleed share:

•            At Mortis Ævum: maximum preservation. Stasis.

•            At Titanwood: maximum growth. Hyperbiology.

•            At Stormpeak: maximum elemental force. Perpetual storms.

•            At Sovereign Exchange: maximum exchange. Perpetual commerce.

•            At Deepguard: maximum pressure. Perpetual stability.

The same mechanic produces opposite results in different locations because the resonating PRINCIPLE differs.

# <a name="_heading=h.flif49qg120h"></a>**PART IV: CROSS-PLANAR ENVIRONMENTAL EXPRESSIONS**
Environmental zones don’t always express a single plane. The SRD v3 and Elemental Cards document 16 natural cross-planar combinations (CP-01 through CP-16) and 8 Emulsion states that represent TWO planes expressing simultaneously. These are real terrain types.
## <a name="_heading=h.p5ksjkdwd2vn"></a>**Natural Cross-Planar Combinations (16 States)**

|Code|Dominant → Recessive|Name|Environmental Expression|
| :- | :- | :- | :- |
|CP-01|Borderlands → Verdant Font|Animism|Physical matter with biological will. Rocks that grow. Rivers that make decisions.|
|CP-02|Verdant Font → Borderlands|Adaptive Flesh|Biology that behaves like minerals. Skin that is stone. Bones that are metal.|
|CP-03|Borderlands → Synod|Fossilization|Physics frozen at its most dynamic moment. The waterfall that stopped. Lightning preserved as glass.|
|CP-04|Synod → Borderlands|Entropic Weather|Rain that accelerates rot. Wind that carries age. Structures experience rapid weathering. Item condition tracks degrade per exposure period.|
|CP-05|Verdant Font → Palimpsest|The Living Myth|Organisms shaped by narrative logic. The herb grows because it was needed. The wolf became more wolf-like because stories demanded it.|
|CP-06|Palimpsest → Verdant Font|The Somatic Prophecy|Biology that predicts. Organisms whose form reflects the probable future rather than ancestral past.|
|CP-07|Synod → Palimpsest|The Inevitable Record|History so complete it calculates the future. Archived truth that deduces forward.|
|CP-08|Palimpsest → Synod|The Forgotten Future|Probability subjected to entropy. Futures that are already decaying before they arrive.|
|CP-09|Forge → Borderlands|The Elemental Engine|Natural forces organized by Forge principle into mechanical cycles. Geysers on clockwork schedule.|
|CP-10|Borderlands → Forge|Artifact Terrain|Physical materials organized by Forge. Steel = CP-10. The canonical cross-planar alloy.|
|CP-11|Forge → Verdant Font|The Biopunk Interface|Biology organized by engineering. The Vespidan hive. Organisms with mechanical precision.|
|CP-12|Verdant Font → Forge|Natural Machinery|Biological process organized as mechanical output. The leaf as solar panel. The tree as water pump.|
|CP-13|Forge → Synod|The Derelict|Entropy organized. Decay that follows blueprints. Ruins that disassemble themselves in engineering-logical order.|
|CP-14|Synod → Forge|Applied Remains|Dead things given mechanical function. Bones as structural members. Fossil fuel.|
|CP-15|Forge → Palimpsest|Clockwork Fate|Probability organized mechanically. Deterministic prophecy. Fate as computation.|
|CP-16|Palimpsest → Forge|The Glitch Machine|Engineering subject to narrative logic. Machines that work only when observed. Technology that responds to dramatic necessity.|
## <a name="_heading=h.3537od2gb8s8"></a>**Emulsion States (8 States)**
These are cosmological events — two ANTITHETICAL planes forced together by Forge catalyst. They don’t occur naturally. They’re environmental catastrophes or deliberate institutional operations.

These environments are the most dangerous and most rewarding in Tessera. Prime materials form here. The creatures that adapt to Emulsion zones are the most powerful and unstable beings short of divine intervention.
