—

**THE PSYCHE SCHEMA**

A Complete Guide to Character Architecture

Ætherium Codex — System Reference


The Psyche Schema is a diagnostic instrument that maps a character’s philosophical convictions, emotional patterns, and cosmic alignment. It is not a personality quiz. It is a genuine measurement of who this person is — what they believe, how they love, and how reality itself reads them.

The Schema has four sections. Each section measures a different kind of data using a different structure. Understanding those structures is the key to reading the Schema correctly.

|**Section**|**Measures**|**Structure**|**Data Type**|
| :- | :- | :- | :- |
|**Core Philosophy**|What you believe|11 paired orientations|Band + dominant aspect mode|
|**Selûnae (Fate)**|How you relate to destiny|3 paired dimensions|Band + perspective + face|
|**Heart’s Compass (Love)**|How you connect emotionally|9 concepts|Band + face + trending|
|**Composite Scores**|How reality reads you|2 single values|Threshold (Aíon) / Silent clock (Lacuna)|


# **PART I: THE 10-BAND OVERLAY**
Every Schema dimension is measured on a 1–100 scale. The 10-band overlay converts that number into a meaningful label and a die. The band is what matters at the table. The 1–100 number tracks incremental change between sessions.

|**Band**|**Range**|**Label**|**Die**|**What It Means**|
| :- | :- | :- | :- | :- |
|1|1–10|**Absent**|—|Burned away, never developed, or actively suppressed. A scar or seed, not a zero.|
|2|11–20|**Vestigial**|d4|A trace. Present in the architecture but not functional.|
|3|21–30|**Weak**|d4|Present but easily overridden by stronger dimensions.|
|4|31–40|**Developing**|d6|Growing or recovering. Influences decisions when nothing stronger competes.|
|5|41–50|**Moderate**|d6|Functional baseline. Influences behavior regularly but doesn’t dominate.|
|6|51–60|**Established**|d8|Others describe the character using this dimension. Reliably present.|
|7|61–70|**Strong**|d8|A defining feature. Drives behavior even under pressure.|
|8|71–80|**Dominant**|d10|Shapes most decisions. Hard to act against.|
|9|81–90|**Driving**|d10|Near-total identification. Threatening it threatens the person.|
|10|91–100|**Defining**|d12|The character IS this dimension. The load-bearing wall of their identity.|

**Die Assignment:** Five distinct dice assigned at even bands. Odd bands share the die of the band below but carry a different narrative label. A character at Band 7 (Strong, d8) is on the cusp of Band 8 (Dominant, d10). The next significant experience might push them over.

**Mechanical Use:** When a dimension is directly relevant to a scene, the band’s die can be added as a bonus or complication. The die IS the dimension’s weight in the character’s psychology.

**Change:** Change operates at the 1–100 resolution. Upward pressure (reinforcing experiences) increases the score; downward pressure (eroding experiences) decreases it. Change only becomes mechanically visible when it crosses a band threshold.


# **PART II: CORE PHILOSOPHY**
Eleven paired orientations mapped to eleven deities. Each pair measures where the character sits on a philosophical spectrum. Both aspects are scored independently (1–100 each). They are NOT opposing ends of a single slider — a character can score high on both, low on both, or asymmetrically.

Each deity has its own internal modes — different ways the dominant aspect can express. The engine reads not just which aspect dominates but WHICH MODE of that aspect is currently active.

## **AURELION — Sovereignty**
*The gap between the leader you aspire to be and the leader you actually are under pressure.*

**Luxos (The Ideal):** The gilded mask — the impossible, perfect image of benevolent leadership that must be projected. Not the truth, but the necessary performance.

**Ignislor (The Real):** The burden of the crown — the venting of pressure from maintaining the mask. Not evil, but stressed, flawed, and fallible.

**Internal Modes:** Ignislor has four distinct failure modes: The Tyrant (wounded pride → overwhelming force), The Deaf King (burnout → unilateral decisions), The Permissive Father (excessive empathy → failure to set boundaries), The Understandable Egoist (personal insult → disproportionate response).

## **MOROS — The Life-Death Cycle**
*The relationship between growth and ending. Not good vs evil — two inseparable parts of a single process.*

**Silas (Development):** The entire process of living: growth, struggle, learning, memory, purposeful existence on the journey from birth to death.

**Dolor (Decay):** The entire process of dying: entropy, decline, the slow fading of memory, the final inevitable end.

**Internal Modes:** The Paradoxical Truth (esoteric inversion): The Verdant Font is the True Plane of Death (perpetual consumption). The Silent Athenaeum is the True Plane of Life (eternal preservation). Three sects: Followers of Silas (Developmental), Cults of Dolor (Entropic), True Priesthood (Balancers who understand both).

## **DAEDALON — Crafting**
*Two philosophies of creation. Whether the careful process or the passionate result matters more.*

**The Artificer (Process):** The Measured Hand — meticulous planning, safety, foresight of consequences. The master planner who never rushes.

**The Crucible (Result):** The Unbound Flame — passionate inspiration, necessary sacrifice, the belief that breakthrough demands risk.

**Internal Modes:** Three afterlife destinations by philosophy: Unbound Flame → Borderlands (chaotic creation). Measured Hand → Concordance (perfect order). Truly Balanced → Daedalon’s personal realm (working alongside the god).

## **NOMOS — Justice**
*Whether justice lives in the intent behind the law or in its exact wording.*

**Themis (The Spirit):** Compassion, flexibility, the potential for grace to rise from murky circumstances. The curved visor and the lotus.

**Lex (The Letter):** Unbending rule, unbreakable security, binding consequences. The straight visor and the chain link.

**Internal Modes:** The eternal debate: Is a flawed but flexible system (Themis) better than a secure but rigid one (Lex)? Cadenza (Freedom) argues Themis is a lie to maintain control. Cacophony (Anarchy) argues Lex is the ultimate tyranny.

## **BELLUM HEGEMON — Conflict**
*Is this struggle necessary, and is the outcome worth the price?*

**Kaelen (Productive Conflict):** The Strategist — disciplined, justified conflict. The sharp debate that forges better law. Strategy and discipline studied at Stormpeak.

**Bellax (The True Cost):** Bellax manifests as a CHILD — a victim, not a warrior. The true spirit of war: the slaughter of innocents, sacrifice, the suffering of those who didn’t choose the fight. A high Bellax score measures what you INFLICT on innocents, not what you endure.

**Internal Modes:** The symbol is intentionally ambiguous: the crumbling tower can be an enemy’s destruction raining on innocents below, OR your own society crumbling from within under the strain of war.

## **VALORIN — Commerce**
*Whether wealth creation serves communities or extracts from them.*

**Mercatus (Fair Trade):** The Broker — opportunity, mutually beneficial contracts, prosperity through fair trade. Resides in the Concordance (orderly, regulated markets).

**Credo (Predatory):** The Monopolist — exploitation, crippling debt, hostile takeovers. Not simple evil: tolerated for brutal efficiency. Resides in the Borderlands (unregulated frontiers).

**Internal Modes:** Cursed Coinage mechanic with Thalassus. “Flirting with the Fates” — Valorin courts each Selûnae goddess differently through both aspects, creating mythological drama with the god of Perilous Labor.

## **AGORA — Civilization**
*The duality of civilization itself: bastion of shared achievement vs suffocating conformity.*

**The Architect (Collective):** Community, safety, shared knowledge, culture, art, and progress through cooperation. The warmth of the hearth and the strength of the city wall.

**The Tyranny (System):** Tyranny is NOT active malice. It is a state a society falls into through improper maintenance and care — smog from unregulated factories, the coldness of bureaucracy, loss of self in a faceless crowd. High Tyranny is a warning sign, not a diagnosis.

**Internal Modes:** The Urban Faithful: “Urban Druids” and “Urban Rangers” — agents of Nature operating within Civilization. Activists, vigilantes, the Batman archetype hunting predators of the concrete jungle.

## **VERDENA — Nature**
*NOT nature vs civilization. Nature vs pollution and neglect. Fiora IS agriculture.*

**Fiora (The Cultivator):** Nurture — agriculture, animal husbandry, the symbiotic partnership between civilization and the natural world. Four seasonal aspects through the Primal Totem.

**Gorn (The Untamed Heart):** Nature — the savage, primal truth. Patron of orcs, minotaurs, and those who embrace wild nature and reject “taming.” Self-sufficiency and honor through strength.

**Internal Modes:** Verdena is not fundamentally opposed to technology — a waterwheel is acceptable; a great dam that chokes a river is anathema. The conflict is with Agora’s Tyranny aspect (pollution), not Agora’s Architect aspect (cities).

## **RHAPSODIA — Freedom & Chaos**
*Freedom as a creative force vs freedom as a destructive force. Gender-fluid deity (Varis as masculine aspect).*

**Jocan (Skillful Expression):** The Spark — cleverness, opportunity, fortune. The rebel who topples a tyrant with a perfectly executed plan. No system is truly unbreakable.

**Malix (Unrestrained Will):** The Glitch — deceit, chaos, misfortune. The spanner in the works. All systems are illusions; only chaos from unfettered will is truth.

**Internal Modes:** The Freedom Paradox: Followers who escape human systems are reborn into nature’s systems. The pacifist reborn as a wolf. The anarchist reborn as an ant. “The only thing worse than a system you can fight against is a system so absolute you don’t even know you’re in it.”

## **VESTUS — Knowledge**
*The difference between information and wisdom. Data with context vs data without.*

**Veritas (Contextualized Truth):** Lore — wisdom, discovery, truth WITH context. The story behind the facts. The patron of scholars who seek knowledge to understand and build.

**Gnosis (Raw Data):** Raw Data — hubris, forbidden secrets, the madness from contextless information. Knowledge pursued at any cost. The patron of those who hoard dangerous truths.

**Internal Modes:** Vestus is a tragic outsider — an echo of Setting B’s destroyed concept of faith. He and Headmaster Kyril are two halves of a single whole in a double blind. His church has two tiers: public Archivists (Veritas) and secret Eternal Scholars (Gnosis, undead who pursue raw data).

## **KANON & ANIMA — Magic**
*TWO SEPARATE GODS, not one. Magic as studied system vs magic as innate creative force.*

**Kanon (The Science):** The Architect of Order — formulas, rituals, theory. Two internal aspects: Theoria (Pure Theory, the elegant “why”) and Praxis (Proven Formula, the reliable “how”).

**Anima (The Art):** The Wellspring of Chaos — instinct, emotion, creativity. Two internal aspects: Epiphanes (Divine Inspiration, the “Eureka!”) and Pathos (Raw Passion, the emotional fuel).

**Internal Modes:** THE GREAT INVERSION: Kanon is from Setting B (the destroyed world). Structured magic — the foundation of the Colleges — may be repeating the ideology that destroyed a previous reality. Anima is native to Setting A. Her wild magic is how power is SUPPOSED to function. Every high-Kanon character is unconsciously aligned with a destroyed world’s methodology.


# **PART III: THE SELËNAE (FATE)**
Three goddesses who govern the cycles of fate. Each goddess has two personas — a primary perspective (with respect) and an inverted perspective (with agency or scorn). These are NOT independent scores. They are two views of the SAME dimension. The character leans toward one perspective or holds both in tension.

Each persona has three faces: Balanced (healthy expression), Unrestrained (virtue pushed to toxicity), and Trauma-Response (self-protective inversion born from hurt).

## **KISMET / SABINA — The Fate of Potential**
**Dimension: Potential**
### **Kismet (The Maiden) (Primary Perspective)**
**Balanced:** Healthy independence, courage to choose a path, boundless potential of self-discovery.

**Unrestrained:** Reckless Indecision — so in love with every possibility that they never commit. Life becomes unfinished projects and unrealized dreams.

**Trauma:** Fearful Limitation — a past choice led to disaster, so they cut off all but the “safest” thread. Stifling, predictable existence devoid of growth.
### **Sabina (The Wench) (Inverted Perspective)**
**Balanced:** The Freeholder/Trailblazer — master of their own destiny, forging new paths without permission, creating possibilities for others to follow.

**Unrestrained:** Conquest Without Respect — seizing potential through force of will without regard for others, bulldozing new paths at others’ expense.

**Trauma:** The Scorned Outcast — rejected by every system, they’ve decided all paths are rigged and all potential is a trap.

## **RHIANNON / LIVIA — The Fate of Connection**
**Dimension: Connection**
### **Rhiannon (The Mother) (Primary Perspective)**
**Balanced:** Nurturing, protection, the fierce bond of family and community. Making a house into a home.

**Unrestrained:** Suffocating Enmeshment — the bonds of community become a smothering web that chokes individuality. Tradition becomes dogma.

**Trauma:** Paranoid Isolation — after devastating loss, they sever all connections. Xenophobic protection of the “in-group” against all outsiders.
### **Livia (The Strumpet) (Inverted Perspective)**
**Balanced:** The Diplomat — a vast network of connections cultivated for mutual benefit. “Promiscuity” as powerful, strategic alliance-weaving.

**Unrestrained:** The Sycophant — seeks security by pleasing the powerful. No core principles, shifting loyalty to whoever holds the most power.

**Trauma:** The Betrayer — convinced all connections are lies. Weaves into others’ trust only to cut the threads at the most profitable moment.

## **VESPERA / THESSA — The Fate of Consequence**
**Dimension: Consequence**
### **Vespera (The Crone) (Primary Perspective)**
**Balanced:** Wise judge of actions and consequences. The grace and acceptance to know when a cycle is complete. The patron of historians and counselors.

**Unrestrained:** Paralyzing Fatalism — so aware of every potential negative consequence that they advise against all action. Stagnation through wisdom’s excess.

**Trauma:** Merciless Finality — convinced all choices lead to pain, they cut threads prematurely and without compassion, “sparing” others through swift, brutal endings.
### **Thessa (The Hag) (Inverted Perspective)**
**Balanced:** The Oracle — the speaker of hard truths polite society shuns. Wisdom from outside the world’s comforting lies. Potent, unsettling, real.

**Unrestrained:** The Puppeteer — uses knowledge of cause and effect to manipulate others for cruel amusement. Orchestrating tragic consequences to affirm their own power.

**Trauma:** The Vengeful Witch — scorned and wronged, they use secret knowledge for disproportionate, cruel revenge. Bitter curses as the only justice available to the powerless.

**How to Score:** The character sheet records: Dimension + Band + Perspective Lean + Active Face. Example: “Potential: Band 7 (d8) — Wench (Trailblazer, stable)” tells you this character actively forges their own destiny at a Strong level, currently expressing the healthy version.


# **PART IV: THE HEART’S COMPASS (LOVE)**
Nine gods of the Chorus of the Heart, each governing a form of love. Every concept has three faces: a Balanced Ideal, an Unrestrained Extreme (the virtue pushed to toxicity), and a Trauma-Response Extreme (born from hurt or loss). The band measures intensity. The face measures expression quality.

*A character at Constantine Band 8, Foundation is fundamentally different from Constantine Band 8, Contract — same intensity, same die, completely different person. The face IS the diagnostic.*

### **ISOLDE — Passionate Love (Eros)**
**Balanced:** The Spark — passion as creative, beautiful force. Embracing vulnerability, desire, and the fire of romance.

**Unrestrained:** Innocence — reckless, all-consuming passion that suffocates the partner and starves other forms of love. A cage made of emotional intensity.

**Trauma:** The Cage — possession born from betrayal. Passionate love is too precious to be left unsecured. Protecting what you love at any cost, even caging it.

### **SILAS — Affectionate Love (Philia)**
**Balanced:** The Kindred — friendship built on mutual trust, shared joy, open support. The powerful bond of allies who choose each other.

**Unrestrained:** The Floozy — tries to be everyone’s friend but has no deep loyalty. Openness so extreme that all relationships are shallow.

**Trauma:** The Oathbound — exclusionary loyalty born from being harmed by outsiders. Only the sworn circle can be trusted. Defending your own regardless of moral cost.

### **AETHEL — Familial Love (Storge)**
**Balanced:** The Hearth — the family as unconditional bond that nurtures individuals and helps them grow.

**Unrestrained:** The Bloodline — stifling obligation where family traditions and legacy are more important than any individual member. Personal desires sacrificed for the name.

**Trauma:** Familial Betrayal — born from a deep internal division. Breaking away from a toxic family to find or create a new, healthier one.

### **ESELLE — Selfless Love (Agape)**
**Balanced:** The Fountain — selfless, universal love for all beings. Giving freely without expectation for the good of the community.

**Unrestrained:** The Martyr — self-destructive giving. “The Giving Tree.” Self-care is selfish; the only way to prove worth is to give until nothing is left.

**Trauma:** The Abandoned — lost faith in the community’s ability to provide and protect. “Why should my taxes go to schools when I don’t have kids?”

### **CORIN — Playful Love (Ludus)**
**Balanced:** The Dance — flirtation and joyful, casual connection. Attraction as a source of fun and happiness, free from heavy commitment.

**Unrestrained:** The Diva — moves from one new thing to the next like a moth to flame. Captures attention but never forms lasting connection.

**Trauma:** The Mask — emotional detachment born from a deeply painful past relationship. Love is a game you control. Never let anyone close enough to truly hurt you.

### **CONSTANTINE — Enduring Love (Pragma)**
**Balanced:** The Foundation — love as stable partnership built on shared goals, duty, and commitment over a lifetime. The quiet love of weathered storms.

**Unrestrained:** The Fling — intensity without temporal boundaries. Burns through relationships quickly, leaving them feeling used when powerful emotions fade.

**Trauma:** The Contract — passionless transaction born from chaotic, failed relationships. Emotions are unreliable; true security is a clear transactional understanding of duties.

### **KENNA — Self-Love (Philautia)**
**Balanced:** The Pillar — healthy self-respect and self-care. Understanding and valuing yourself before offering healthy love to others.

**Unrestrained:** The Egoist — extreme self-absorption, vanity, narcissism. Own perspective is the most important one. The Emperor’s New Clothes.

**Trauma:** The Fortress — absolute selfishness born from being hurt or abandoned by everyone. The world is hostile; you can only rely on yourself. Build the walls.

### **ZENIEL — Hospitality (Xenia)**
**Balanced:** The Shared Cup — hospitality as sacred duty. The host offers safety without exploitation; the guest honors the host’s rules. Every stranger is potential kin.

**Unrestrained:** Predatory Obligation — a host who ensnares guests with false welcome, using obligation as a leash. The guest “owes” endlessly for the privilege of entry.

**Trauma:** Withholding Welcome — scarred by betrayal, the host never opens their door again. All strangers are threats. Every offer hides a trap.

### **VEYRA — Unrequited Devotion**
**Balanced:** Devotion as Inspiration — loving the unreachable motivates creation, faith, or moral strength. A safe love that provides grounding without demanding reciprocity.

**Unrestrained:** Obsessive Idolization — mistaking the phantom for reality. Replacing real bonds with an illusionary beloved. Parasocial corruption erasing the self.

**Trauma:** Denial of Love’s Reality — refusing reciprocal bonds altogether. Convinced all love is false or doomed. Nihilistic yearning: longing persists, but belief in love is gone.

**How to Score:** The character sheet records: Concept + Band (Die) + Face Name + Trending. Example: “Constantine: Band 8 (d10) — Foundation (trending Contract)” tells you this character has deep commitment currently expressed as genuine partnership, but the emotional content is slowly draining — they’re drifting toward passionless duty.

**The Personal Clock:** The personal clock IS the face drift. Balanced → Unrestrained drift means success/excess is consuming the virtue. Balanced → Trauma drift means hurt/loss is inverting it into self-protection. Mastery is holding Balanced under pressure. Slag is crystallizing at an extreme.


# **PART V: COMPOSITE SCORES**
## **AÏON (Cosmic Stability)**
Aíon is the Overdeity who built and maintains reality. He is not a dual-aspect god — his duality is purely functional. He operates at cosmic scale, indifferent to individual problems, focused on “movers and shakers” and long-arc threats to reality’s stability.

The Aíon composite score is a threshold measure, not a linear scale. Below the threshold, the character is invisible to cosmic attention — a local actor in a local story. Above the threshold, the character is either a potential instrument of stability (high positive) or a potential threat to it (high negative). The score drives Fulcrum Point drip recharge.

The Nexus Spire embodies his philosophy at urban scale: a self-regulating city that facilitates purpose but is indifferent to personal affairs, intervening only to maintain stability.
## **THE LACUNA (Cosmic Instability)**
The Lacunæ is not a god. Not an entity. Not something that can be worshipped. It is a meta-physical force — purposeless destruction, existential doubt, the universal tendency toward dissolution. “L’appel du vide” — the call of the void.

The Lacuna composite score is a silent clock. It increases without the character’s awareness through despair, self-sabotage, creative block, and purposeless destruction. Characters don’t KNOW their Lacuna score is rising. It manifests as: an artist’s creative block, a ruler’s paralyzing self-doubt, a mage’s sudden loss of faith in their power, the sudden inexplicable urge to destroy something beautiful for no reason.

The Lacuna is not the source of motivated villainy. It is the source of the ABSENCE that pulls things apart. High Lacuna doesn’t make you a villain. It makes you hollow — and the hollowness is what lets other forces fill the space.

Two cosmic forces. One builds. One dissolves. The composite scores measure which force has more purchase on this character’s soul. Every character in Tessera exists in the tension between them.


# **PART VI: HOW TO READ A SCHEMA**
Reading a Schema is not scoring a test. It is identifying pressure points — the places where the character is most vulnerable to change, most likely to generate story, and most revealing of who they actually are.

## **What to Look For**
**Outlier Scores:** Any dimension at Band 9–10 (Driving/Defining) or Band 1–2 (Absent/Vestigial). Extreme highs are load-bearing convictions — threatening them threatens the person. Extreme lows are absences or scars — forcing engagement with them produces disproportionate reactions.

**Asymmetric Splits:** Any paired dimension where the gap exceeds 4 bands (approximately 40+ points). An asymmetric split is an emotional wound disguised as a philosophical position. The suppressed side is a front waiting to happen.

**Pattern Breaks:** Scores that don’t match the expected profile for the character’s role or presented identity. Pattern breaks indicate hidden pressures the character may not be aware of. These are the richest adventure generators.

**Face Drift:** Any Heart’s Compass or Selûnae dimension drifting from Balanced toward Unrestrained or Trauma. A drifting face IS a personal clock and generates its own front.

## **The Three Discriminators**
Calibration against real historical figures identified three Core Philosophy dimensions that function as a moral compass:

**1. Tyranny Level:** Every institutional villain in calibration scored Band 7+ Tyranny. Every hero scored Band 5 or below. But Tyranny is passive drift (improper maintenance), not active malice. High Tyranny means the character has stopped asking whether the system serves the people or the people serve the system.

**2. Themis/Lex Orientation:** Villains lean Lex (letter over spirit). Heroes lean Themis (spirit over letter). The orientation determines whether the individual serves the system’s purpose or its mechanics.

**3. Veritas/Gnosis Orientation:** Villains lean Gnosis (raw data without context). Heroes lean Veritas (contextualized truth). The information orientation determines whether analytical capability produces wisdom or paranoia.


# **PART VII: SCHEMA NOTATION QUICK REFERENCE**

## **Core Philosophy (11 pairs)**
Format: [God]: [Aspect A] Band [X] / [Aspect B] Band [Y] — [Active Mode]

*Example: Aurelion: Luxos Band 4 / Ignislor Band 9 — Deaf King*

## **Selûnae (3 dimensions)**
Format: [Dimension]: Band [X] (Die) — [Perspective] ([Face], [Trending])

*Example: Connection: Band 7 (d8) — Mother (Balanced, stable)*

## **Heart’s Compass (9 concepts)**
Format: [Concept]: Band [X] (Die) — [Face Name] ([Trending])

*Example: Constantine: Band 10 (d12) — The Foundation (trending Contract)*

## **Composites**
Format: Aíon: [Score] (Band [X]) | Lacuna: [Score/Unknown]

*Example: Aíon: 88 (Band 9) | Lacuna: Unknown (silent clock)*

## <a name="_heading=h.hpk0yqczhu0"></a>**Open Design Items**
**⚠** Selûnae: Structural review pending. Current three-dimension model functional but face taxonomy for each persona needs testing.

**⚠** Lacuna Variable: No derivation formula exists. The composite score’s mechanical role is deferred.

**⚠** Schema Template v2: Outdated. The character creation output has the correct format. Template needs updating to match.

**PSYCHE SCHEMA**

System Reference — Version 4

Document 1A — Vertebra 1: Schema

*The profiling instrument of the Ætherium Codex unified architecture*

**WHAT THE SCHEMA IS**

The Psyche Schema is the profiling instrument at Vertebra 1 of the Codex’s seven-vertebra spine. It answers three questions about any entity: who are they, how do they process events, and what do they seek. Every subsequent vertebra reads the Schema’s output. Pressure Points (Vertebra 2) are extracted from Schema anomalies. Fronts (Vertebra 3) are built from Schema-driven motivations. Clocks (Vertebra 4) tick at rates determined by Schema-derived methodology. The Schema is not a character sheet supplement. It is the foundation the entire architecture builds from.

The Schema applies to everything that has internal complexity. Player characters, NPCs, institutions, territories, and gods all have Schemas or Schema-equivalents (see Document 1B: Profiling Companions Guide). Everything uses the same profiling architecture because everything interacts with the same spine. The rules do not change between scales. The content changes. The instrument is universal.

**The Schema as Narrative Energy Source**

The Schema generates Fulcrum Points — the metacurrency for spine manipulation. Internal tensions (outlier scores, asymmetric splits between paired dimensions, pattern breaks) are pressure points. Each pressure point generates one Fulcrum Point per session. A flat, simple profile generates minimal narrative energy. A complex, contradictory profile generates enormous narrative energy. Character depth IS mechanical power in the Codex. Not because deeper characters have higher stats. Because their internal contradictions produce more Fulcrum Points.

**The Schema as a Genuine Instrument**

The Psyche Schema is a measurement tool, not a personality quiz. It does not sort characters into types. It maps the internal landscape of values, tensions, and emotional architecture with enough resolution to predict behavior under pressure and identify where the interesting story lives. The scores are coordinates in a dimensional space defined by the gods’ portfolios. The gods are the axis labels. The character is the point plotted in that space.

**THE THREE SECTIONS**

The Schema is organized into three sections, each capturing a different category of data. The sections are not hierarchical. They are three lenses on one identity, each revealing information the others cannot.

|**I. Core Philosophy**|11 paired dimensions mapping the character’s worldview. Each pair is anchored to a god’s dual-aspect portfolio. Scored 1-100 per aspect. These are the character’s philosophical convictions — how they believe the world works and how they believe it should work. The most stable section. Changes slowly through accumulated experience.|
| :- | :- |
|**II. The Selûnae**|3 paired dimensions mapping the character’s relationship to fate, time, and consequence. Scored 1-100 per aspect. These are the character’s temporal orientation — how they relate to their own past, present trajectory, and future. More fluid than Core Philosophy. Face drift between paired aspects IS the character’s personal clock.|
|**III. Heart’s Compass**|9 dimensions mapping the character’s emotional architecture. Each dimension has three faces: Balanced (healthy expression), Unrestrained (excess), and Trauma (wounded expression). The face the character currently operates from determines how the dimension manifests. The most volatile section. Can shift within a single session through emotionally significant events.|

Sections I and II are derivable from the character’s philosophical position and institutional role. A GM generating an NPC can produce reasonable scores for these sections from a brief character concept. Section III (Heart’s Compass) requires knowledge of the character’s personal emotional life and is the section that demands the most authorial investment. The GM shortcut (see Section 9) acknowledges this asymmetry.

**SECTION I: CORE PHILOSOPHY**

Eleven paired dimensions. Each pair is anchored to a god’s dual-aspect portfolio. The two aspects are not opposites — they are two faces of the same divine principle. A character’s score on each aspect indicates how strongly that face of the principle manifests in their worldview.

|**God**|**Aspect A**|**Aspect B**|**The Tension**|
| :- | :- | :- | :- |
|**Aurelion**|Luxos (The Ideal)|Ignislor (The Real)|Aspiration versus pragmatism. How much does the character strive for perfection versus accept reality as it is?|
|**Moros**|Silas (The Struggle)|Dolor (The Stasis)|Engagement versus acceptance. Does the character fight against suffering or endure it? Is struggle the point or the obstacle?|
|**Daedalon**|Artificer (Process)|Crucible (Result)|Method versus outcome. Does the character value how something was made or what it produces? Craftsmanship versus product.|
|**Nomos**|Themis (The Spirit)|Lex (The Letter)|Intent versus rule. Does the character follow the purpose behind the law or the text of the law? Spirit versus letter.|
|**Bellum Hegemon**|Kaelen (Productive)|Bellax (Suffering)|Necessary conflict versus destructive conflict. When the character encounters violence, is it a tool for change or a source of trauma?|
|**Valorin**|Mercatus (Fair Trade)|Credo (Predatory)|Exchange versus extraction. Does the character create value through reciprocity or concentrate it through leverage?|
|**Agora**|Architect (Collective)|Tyranny (System)|Community versus control. Does the character build institutions that serve people or systems that perpetuate themselves?|
|**Verdena**|Fiora (The Garden)|Gorn (The Wild)|Cultivation versus nature. Does the character shape the natural world or submit to its terms? Order imposed on biology versus biology expressed freely.|
|**Rhapsodia**|Jocan (Skillful)|Malix (Unrestrained)|Discipline versus freedom in expression. Does the character channel creativity through craft or release it without constraint?|
|**Vestus**|Veritas (Lore/Context)|Gnosis (Raw Data)|Wisdom versus knowledge. Does the character seek understanding with context or information without interpretation?|
|**Kanon / Anima**|Kanon (The Science)|Anima (The Art)|System versus intuition. Does the character understand the world through analysis or through felt experience? Two separate gods in permanent tension.|

Each aspect is scored 1-100 independently. The two aspects of a pair are NOT inversely linked — a character can score 80 on both Luxos and Ignislor (a pragmatic idealist) or 20 on both (someone who neither aspires nor accepts). The relationship between the two scores in each pair is where the diagnostic information lives. High-high means the character holds both faces in tension. High-low means one face dominates. Low-low means the divine principle is not a significant part of the character’s identity.

**Positional Divinity as Coordinate System**

The gods’ portfolios are not flavor text. They are the dimensional axes of the Schema’s measurement space. Each score is the character’s coordinate on that god’s axis. The full Schema plots the character in a 22-dimensional space (11 pairs × 2 aspects) anchored by divine principles. This is the Positional Divinity system expressed as character architecture — the gods define the space, the character occupies a position within it, and the position determines which divine aspects resonate with the character’s soul.

Veneration occurs through performing a role within a god’s portfolio, not through formal worship. A character with Kaelen 85 venerates Bellum Hegemon’s productive-conflict aspect every time they engage in purposeful struggle. The character’s scores are simultaneously a personality profile AND a map of which divine aspects they embody through their actions.

**SECTION II: THE SELÛNAE**

Three paired dimensions mapping the character’s relationship to fate, time, and consequence. The Selûnae read fate backwards — identity crystallizes from the pattern that already exists, not from hitting milestones. The Legacy System (Vertebra 7) is the mechanical expression of this principle.

|**Pair**|**Face A**|**Face B**|**The Tension**|
| :- | :- | :- | :- |
|**Potential / Conquest**|Maiden (Inevitable Potential)|Wench (Chosen Conquest)|What comes to the character unbidden versus what they seized deliberately. Passive reception of fate versus active claim on the future.|
|**Connection / Flux**|Mother (Enduring Connection)|Strumpet (Shifting Connection)|Permanent bonds versus temporary alliances. Roots versus movement. Loyalty sustained across time versus adaptation to changing circumstances.|
|**Wisdom / Weight**|Crone (Understood Consequence)|Hag (Unending Consequence)|Consequences the character has integrated versus consequences the character cannot escape. Lessons learned versus burdens carried.|

**Face Drift as Personal Clock**

The Selûnae scores are not static. They drift over the course of a campaign as the character accumulates experience. The direction and rate of drift IS the character’s personal clock. A character drifting from Maiden toward Wench is claiming more agency. A character drifting from Crone toward Hag is accumulating burdens faster than they can integrate them. A character whose Selûnae scores are stable has reached equilibrium with their fate. A character whose scores are in rapid drift is in crisis.

Face drift is the only Schema section that changes without explicit player or GM intervention. The drift happens naturally as the Legacy System (Vertebra 7) reads the character’s accumulated choices and adjusts the temporal orientation accordingly. The player does not update their Selûnae scores — the GM updates them based on the pattern the Legacy System reveals.

**SECTION III: HEART’S COMPASS**

Nine dimensions mapping the character’s emotional architecture. Each dimension has three faces: Balanced (healthy expression), Unrestrained (excess that becomes its own problem), and Trauma (wounded expression that distorts the dimension). The face the character currently operates from determines how the dimension manifests in behavior.

|**Dimension**|**Balanced**|**Unrestrained**|**Trauma**|
| :- | :- | :- | :- |
|**Isolde (Passion)**|Deep feeling channeled productively|Consuming obsession that burns relationships|Fear of feeling; emotional fortress built after being burned|
|**Silas (Friendship)**|Genuine reciprocal bonds|Codependence; identity defined by others|Inability to trust after betrayal; isolation as defense|
|**Aethel (Family)**|Healthy familial connection|Enmeshment; family as cage|Broken family bonds; orphan’s wound; inherited dysfunction|
|**Eselle (Selflessness)**|Generous service to others|Martyrdom; giving until empty|Resentment from unreturned sacrifice; weaponized generosity|
|**Corin (Playfulness)**|Joy, humor, lightness|Irresponsibility; avoiding seriousness|Inability to play; weight has crushed all lightness|
|**Constantine (Duty)**|Reliable commitment to obligations|Slavery to obligation; cannot refuse|Duty imposed without consent; obligation as trauma|
|**Kenna (Self-Love)**|Healthy self-regard|Narcissism; self above all others|Self-loathing; inability to value oneself|
|**Zeniel (Hospitality)**|Welcoming generosity to strangers|Inability to refuse entry; boundaries dissolved|Xenophobia; all outsiders are threats|
|**Veyra (Unrequited)**|Acceptance of asymmetric connection|Fixation on what cannot be had|Emotional wound from persistent rejection; the love that defined them by its absence|

Each dimension is scored 1-100 for intensity. The face (Balanced/Unrestrained/Trauma) is noted separately. A character with Isolde 90 Balanced expresses deep passion productively. A character with Isolde 90 Trauma has been burned so badly that the intensity manifests as an emotional fortress. Same score, different face, radically different behavior. The score measures how much energy the dimension carries. The face determines how that energy expresses.

Face transitions are the Heart’s Compass’s equivalent of Selûnae drift. A character whose Silas (Friendship) transitions from Balanced to Trauma during the campaign has experienced a betrayal significant enough to restructure their emotional architecture. The transition is a dramatic event — it should emerge from play, not be assigned arbitrarily.

**THE 10-BAND OVERLAY**

Every Schema score maps to a die type through a 10-band system. The die type represents the character’s mechanical expression of that dimension — how reliably and how powerfully the dimension manifests in resolution.

|**Score Range**|**Die**|**Expression Level**|**Mechanical Effect**|
| :- | :- | :- | :- |
|**1-10**|**None**|Absent. This dimension is not part of the character’s operating identity.|No mechanical expression. Cannot invoke this dimension.|
|**11-20**|**d4**|Minimal. Present but underdeveloped or actively suppressed.|Unreliable when invoked. Low floor, low ceiling.|
|**21-40**|**d6**|Developing. The dimension is part of the character but not dominant.|Functional but inconsistent. Average range.|
|**41-60**|**d8**|Established. A meaningful part of the character’s identity.|Reliable expression. Solid mechanical contribution.|
|**61-80**|**d10**|Dominant. This dimension is a defining feature of the character.|Strong expression. Frequently the decisive factor.|
|**81-100**|**d12**|Load-bearing. The character’s identity would collapse without this dimension.|Maximum expression. The character’s strongest mechanical channel.|

The 10-band overlay connects the Schema’s narrative content (who you are) to the game system’s mechanical resolution (what dice you roll). A character invoking their Kaelen (Productive Conflict) dimension during a social confrontation rolls the die assigned to their Kaelen score. A character with Kaelen 85 rolls d12. A character with Kaelen 30 rolls d6. The Schema IS the mechanical character in this system — not a separate narrative layer bolted on.

**THE COMPANION ELEMENTS**

The Schema’s three sections profile the character’s internal landscape. Five companion elements provide the external context that shapes how the internal landscape expresses.

**College Affiliation**

Which college shaped the character determines their methodology, their persistent test, their failure state, and their operational vocabulary. Two characters with identical Schema scores but different college affiliations produce completely different fronts because the college determines HOW the profile expresses. The Schema says what you value. The college says how you pursue it.

College affiliation also determines the character’s planar alignment (which of the five planes their methodology channels), their operational tempo (how quickly they act), and their institutional vocabulary (how they describe what they do). See the College Initiations Canon for the full initiation, persistent test, and failure state architecture of each college.

**Archetype**

The mechanical chassis the profile operates through. The Schema is the personality. The Archetype is the mechanical toolkit. A high-Kaelen Wildpath character playing through a martial Archetype produces a different set of mechanical expressions than the same Schema through a caster Archetype. The Archetype constrains which mechanical expressions of the Schema are available at Vertebra 6 (Adventures / Observation Collapse).

**Legacy System**

The backward-facing identity that accumulates at Vertebra 7 (Feedback Loop). At Vertebra 1, the Legacy is the character’s starting state — what the Palimpsest has already recorded about this soul. The Legacy system does not read forward from milestones. It reads BACKWARD from accumulated pattern. The character’s identity at session 1 is the Legacy reading everything that came before. Over a campaign, the Legacy accumulates new pattern from the character’s choices, which in turn modifies how the Schema is read — not the scores themselves, but their weight and significance.

**Soul Stone**

The physical container of the Schema data. In-setting, the soul stone carries the god-name weights, aspect values, and full experiential record. The Chirurgeons’ query reads it. The Legacy system is stored in it. The soul stone is the MEDIUM in which the Schema exists. This matters because it means the Schema is a THING in the world — it can be read, queried, stolen, damaged, or transferred. The profile has a physical instantiation that can be interacted with through the game’s mechanics.

**Positional Divinity**

The Schema’s coordinate system (described in Section I) is simultaneously a map of which divine aspects the character embodies. The character’s scores determine which gods’ faces resonate with their soul, which in turn determines which divine portfolio the character’s actions support. This is not worship. It is structural resonance. The character venerates a god by being the kind of person whose actions fall within that god’s portfolio.

**THE ACTIVE PROPERTIES**

Three properties transform the Schema from a static profile into a generative instrument that reaches into the spine and shapes play.

**Goffman: The Two-Faced Profile**

Every profiled entity has a front stage (what they present to the world) and a back stage (what they actually think, feel, and plan). The gap between front stage and back stage IS data. The Schema’s surface scores are the front stage. The outliers, asymmetric splits, and pattern breaks are where the back stage leaks through.

At Vertebra 2 (Pressure Points), the Goffman gap is a primary source of extractable pressure. When you identify that an NPC’s presented behavior contradicts their Schema scores, that contradiction IS a pressure point. The NPC Assessment Card’s Contradictions Log is literally a Goffman gap tracker — recording the observable discrepancies between what the NPC presents and what their profile predicts.

For player characters, the Goffman gap is chosen by the player. They decide what their character presents versus what they actually feel. For GM-generated NPCs, the gap is derived from the Schema — the front stage is what the NPC’s institutional role demands, and the back stage is what the Schema scores reveal about who they actually are underneath the role.

**Beliefs and Instincts**

Beliefs are Schema elements stated as active convictions: “I will protect the innocent.” “The law exists to serve power, not justice.” “No one should die alone.” Each belief is anchored to one or more Schema dimensions and represents the character’s commitment to acting on that dimension’s values. Beliefs CREATE mandatory engagement — certain seeds at Vertebra 5 (Symptoms) automatically trigger observation collapse at Vertebra 6 (Adventures) because the participant’s belief demands response. The character CANNOT walk away from situations that challenge their beliefs. This is the bonding mechanism between Vertebra 1 (who you are) and Vertebra 6 (how you engage).

Instincts are automatic behavioral rules: “I always check for exits.” “I never turn my back on someone I don’t trust.” “I share food before eating.” Instincts are Seeds that collapse automatically without deliberation. They produce consistent observable behavior that other characters and the audience can track. Instincts are the leitmotifs of the expression layer — the recurring behaviors that encode the character’s identity in the group’s collective memory.

Players write 2-3 beliefs and 2-3 instincts during character creation. Beliefs can change through play when the character’s convictions are tested and found wanting. Instincts are deeper — they change only through transformative experiences (initiations, traumatic events, Legacy system crystallization).

**Narrative Identity Type (McAdams)**

How the character processes events — the interpretive template they apply to experience. Four types:

|**Redemption**|Bad events are processed as setups for eventual positive outcomes. The character finds meaning in suffering by believing it leads somewhere better. The suffering was worth it because of what came after.|
| :- | :- |
|**Contamination**|Good events are processed as precursors to loss. The character anticipates that positive experiences will be corrupted or destroyed. Joy is temporary because pain follows.|
|**Agency**|Events are processed through the lens of personal control. The character believes their choices determine outcomes. Success is earned. Failure is the character’s responsibility.|
|**Communion**|Events are processed through relationships. The character’s experience is defined by connection to others. Success is shared. Loss is relational.|

The Narrative Identity type is not a Schema score. It is a processing template that determines how the character INTERPRETS the events the spine produces. The same clock advancement produces different character reactions depending on which type processes it. A Redemption character whose clock crosses the intervention threshold sees it as the darkest moment before the turning point. A Contamination character sees it as confirmation that the positive trajectory was always going to fail.

The type is derived from the character’s backstory and their current Heart’s Compass face distribution. Characters with predominantly Balanced faces tend toward Redemption or Agency. Characters with predominantly Trauma faces tend toward Contamination. Characters with high Silas/Aethel/Zeniel scores tend toward Communion. The type can shift through play as the character’s Heart’s Compass faces change.

**FULCRUM POINT GENERATION**

The Schema generates Fulcrum Points from its internal tensions. The generation mechanic connects Vertebra 1 (Schema) to Vertebra 4 (Clocks) where Fulcrum Points are spent. See Document 4B (Fulcrum Points System) for the complete spend mechanic, distance model, and bidirectional response architecture.

**Pressure Point Identification**

Three types of pressure points generate Fulcrum Points:

**Outlier Scores:** Any single aspect score above 80 or below 20 in any section. Extreme scores represent convictions so deep they are load-bearing — the character’s identity would collapse without them. Convictions this deep create pressure because reality constantly tests them. Each outlier generates 1 FP.

**Asymmetric Splits:** Any paired dimension where the gap between the two aspects exceeds 50 points. A 75-point gap between Veritas 90 and Gnosis 15 is not a preference — it is an emotional wound disguised as a philosophical position. The world will inevitably present situations where the suppressed aspect is the correct response, and the character cannot access it. Each asymmetric split generates 1 FP.

**Pattern Breaks:** Scores that contradict the expected profile for the character’s college, role, or presented identity. An immortal warden with a moderate-high Veyra (Unrequited) score breaks the pattern. Pattern breaks indicate hidden pressures the character themselves may not be aware of. Each pattern break generates 1 FP.

**Generation Formula**

Fulcrum Points per session = (count of outlier scores) + (count of asymmetric splits) + (count of pattern breaks).

A Tier 0 Everyman with a mostly centered Schema might generate 1-2 FP. A headmaster with massive outliers, enormous splits, and multiple pattern breaks might generate 12-15 FP. The system rewards character complexity with narrative energy. This is the same formula applied to NPCs, institutions, territories, and gods — the GM’s Fulcrum Point budget for any entity is generated by the same instrument from the same source.

**THE PLAYER QUESTIONNAIRE**

For player characters, the Schema is generated through a structured intake process. The player answers questions about their character’s values, history, and emotional life. The GM derives scores from the answers. The derivation uses the questions as anchors — the specific answers map to specific dimensional ranges.

**Intake Sections**

|**1. The Concept**|Name, alias, core concept in one sentence. What is this character about?|
| :- | :- |
|**2. The Philosophy**|Questions mapping to each Core Philosophy pair. “When rules conflict with justice, which do you follow?” (Nomos: Themis vs. Lex). “Is struggle the point of living or the obstacle to living?” (Moros: Silas vs. Dolor). One question per divine pair.|
|**3. The Timeline**|Questions mapping to the Selûnae pairs. “What came to you without seeking it?” (Maiden). “What did you take by force?” (Wench). “What bond has lasted longest?” (Mother). “What relationship did you leave behind?” (Strumpet). “What lesson cost you the most?” (Crone). “What consequence do you still carry?” (Hag).|
|**4. The Heart**|Questions mapping to the Heart’s Compass dimensions. “Who do you love and how?” (Isolde). “Who would you die for?” (Eselle). “When did you last laugh so hard you couldn’t breathe?” (Corin). “What obligation do you carry that you didn’t choose?” (Constantine). For each: which face is active (Balanced, Unrestrained, Trauma)?|
|**5. The Toolkit**|Defining moment, signature skill, greatest weakness, method of operation. These are not scored — they contextualize the scores. The defining moment often reveals the Heart’s Compass face transitions. The weakness often maps to the lowest-scoring dimension.|

The GM reviews the answers and assigns scores using the 10-band overlay as a guide. A strong, specific answer about a dimension suggests a high score (61-100). A vague or uncertain answer suggests a mid-range score (41-60). A deflection or avoidance suggests either a very low score (the dimension is absent) or a Trauma face (the dimension is present but painful). The questionnaire produces an initial Schema that the first few sessions of play will refine.

After the questionnaire, the player writes 2-3 Beliefs (active convictions anchored to Schema dimensions) and 2-3 Instincts (automatic behavioral rules). These are the Schema’s active components that reach into the spine during play.

**THE GM SHORTCUT**

The full questionnaire is player-facing. GMs generating NPCs need a compressed procedure that produces a functional Schema from minimal inputs. The shortcut acknowledges that GM-generated scores are authored estimates rather than derived measurements — the precision is approximate, but the diagnostic function (pressure point identification) still works.

**The Five-Question NPC Generator**

|**1. College and Position**|Which college trained them? What role do they occupy? These two facts constrain the Core Philosophy scores — a Stormpeak officer will have high Bellum Hegemon scores, and the specific balance between Kaelen and Bellax depends on whether they’re trending toward mastery or failure.|
| :- | :- |
|**2. The Want**|What do they want right now? The want maps to the highest Core Philosophy scores. A character who wants justice has high Nomos. A character who wants knowledge has high Vestus. The specific face (Themis vs. Lex, Veritas vs. Gnosis) reveals their methodology.|
|**3. The Fear**|What are they afraid of? The fear maps to the lowest scores or the Trauma faces in the Heart’s Compass. A character afraid of abandonment has Silas or Aethel in Trauma face. A character afraid of losing control has Rhapsodia tension.|
|**4. The Contradiction**|What’s one thing about them that doesn’t match their role? This is the deliberate pattern break — the score that generates the mystery front. The military commander with a secret artistic passion. The healer who enjoys the power that proximity to death provides.|
|**5. Clock Position**|Where are they on their college’s personal clock? Early (still learning), middle (competent but tested), late (approaching mastery or failure). Which direction are they trending? This determines which Selûnae face is dominant.|

From these five answers, the GM assigns approximate scores to the 3-5 most relevant Core Philosophy dimensions, the dominant Selûnae pair, and the 2-3 most active Heart’s Compass dimensions. Everything else is left at 50 (neutral). The pressure points are identifiable from the extremes and the contradiction. The NPC is functional for engine derivation.

Section III (Heart’s Compass) is the section that requires the most creative authorship for GM-generated NPCs. The engine acknowledges this: Sections I and II are derivable from position and philosophy. Section III requires the GM to make character choices that the engine cannot generate. The five-question shortcut minimizes this burden by asking for only one Heart’s Compass data point (the fear) and one deliberate pattern break (the contradiction).

**THE COMPOSITE SCORES**

Two composite scores sit outside the three sections. They are derived from the full Schema but are not dimensions themselves — they are emergent properties of the overall profile.

**Aíon (Cosmic Stability)**

A measure of how well the character’s identity integrates into the cosmic architecture. High Aíon stability indicates a profile that is coherent, internally consistent, and aligned with the setting’s structural logic. The character makes sense within the world. Their actions are predictable from their profile. The spine models them accurately.

The Aíon score is relevant to the Smoothing Effect — characters with high cosmic stability encounter less resistance from the setting’s coherence-maintenance architecture. Their actions are already within the parameters the Smoothing Effect permits. Characters with low cosmic stability encounter more friction because their existence is itself a discontinuity the Smoothing Effect wants to dampen.

**The Lacuna (Cosmic Instability)**

A measure of how much the character’s identity disrupts the cosmic architecture. High Lacuna instability indicates a profile that contains contradictions the setting’s structural logic cannot fully resolve. The character is a discontinuity — a point where the Smoothing Effect cannot fully suppress the gap between the two merged settings, or between what the character’s profile predicts and what they actually do.

The relationship between Aíon and Lacuna is not inversely proportional. A character can have high stability AND high instability simultaneously — they are deeply integrated into the cosmic architecture AND they are a source of disruption within it. These characters are the most narratively interesting because they are simultaneously essential to the system and threatening to it.

The derivation formula for both composite scores is pending calibration and will be finalized in the game system’s mathematical framework. The conceptual definitions are established. The specific numerical derivation requires playtesting.

**STORY STACK VALIDATION**

Every completed Schema should be tested against the Story Stack’s four layers to ensure the profile is rich enough to sustain play across all dimensions of the game.

|**Fantasy**|Who does the player want to BE through this character? The Schema should support the player’s fantasy — the version of themselves they want to explore. If the player wants to be a cunning strategist, the Schema should have high Kanon and Vestus scores. If the fantasy is a passionate defender, the Schema needs high Bellum Hegemon and Constantine.|
| :- | :- |
|**Actions**|What does the player want to DO? The Schema’s active components (Beliefs and Instincts) should generate engagement opportunities. If there are no Beliefs that force interaction with the world, the character will be passive. Every Schema should produce at least 2-3 situations per session where a Belief demands response.|
|**Economy**|How does the character interact with the game’s systems? The 10-band overlay should produce a distinctive mechanical signature. Characters whose dice pools are all d8 (everything at 41-60) are mechanically bland. Characters with d12s and d4s (strong outliers) have mechanical texture that creates meaningful choices.|
|**World**|How does the character relate to the setting? College affiliation, Positional Divinity resonance, and the Selûnae’s temporal orientation should connect the character to the setting’s cosmology. A character with no connection to any college, no divine resonance, and no temporal orientation is floating free of the world rather than embedded in it.|

If a Schema fails one or more layers, the solution is not to add scores. It is to add specificity. A character who fails the Actions layer needs sharper Beliefs. A character who fails the World layer needs clearer college affiliation or divine resonance. A character who fails the Fantasy layer needs a conversation between the player and the GM about what the player actually wants to experience.

**EXPRESSION GUIDANCE: PERSISTENCE ENCODING**

The Schema’s strongest expression bond is Persistence Encoding — the techniques that anchor a profiled entity in the group’s collective memory across sessions. Without encoding, the profile is forgotten between encounters. With encoding, the character becomes a recognizable presence that the group tracks and anticipates.

**Leitmotifs**

A recurring verbal phrase, physical action, or behavioral pattern associated with the character. The phrase or action appears whenever the character is present and the associated Schema dimension is active. Over time, the leitmotif carries the character’s full emotional weight in a few words or gestures.

**Design principle:** One per character maximum. More than one dilutes the signal. The leitmotif should be derived from the character’s highest or most distinctive Schema dimension.

**Behavioral Signatures**

The NPC personality technique: one behavior (repeated physical action when not addressed), one opinion (editorial perspective nobody asked for), one refusal (what they won’t do). Each maps to a Schema dimension. The behavior maps to the dominant Core Philosophy face. The opinion maps to the strongest Belief. The refusal maps to the lowest score or a Trauma-face dimension.

**Third-Person Narration**

Report instead of perform. “The Docent goes quiet and then says the symbol is familiar but won’t elaborate” communicates mystery without performing it. The emotional cue is the narrator’s contribution. The audience’s imagination provides the performance. Each participant generates a different character in their imagination from the same cue. The underspecification is a feature — Schema Theory (Bartlett) says the audience’s schema fills the gap with personally meaningful content.

**KNOWN LIMITATIONS AND DIAGNOSTIC FINDINGS**

From proof-of-concept testing (Kyril full derivation, Colonel reverse derivation):

|**False Precision**|GM-authored NPC scores feel authoritative but are actually estimates. Why 85 and not 80? There is no derivation formula for GM-generated scores. The precision is approximate. The diagnostic function (pressure point identification) still works because it reads for RELATIVE extremes, not absolute values. An outlier is an outlier whether the score is 82 or 88.|
| :- | :- |
|**Heart’s Compass Authorship**|Sections I-II are derivable from position and philosophy. Section III requires the GM to make character choices the engine cannot generate. The GM shortcut minimizes this burden but does not eliminate it.|
|**External Pressure Gap**|The engine’s derivation handles NPC-internal pressure (what the Schema reveals) but not NPC-external pressure (what the environment does TO the person regardless of Schema). An additional derivation step is needed: “What pressures does this POSITION generate independent of who occupies it?”|
|**Creative Interpretation Step**|The mapping from pressure point to front requires creative interpretation informed by setting knowledge. This step needs to be named explicitly in the procedure rather than hidden.|
|**Clock Parameterization**|Schema derivation produces fronts with potential clocks, not parameterized clocks. Specific clock values (segments, thresholds, terminal states) need play context. The Schema is a prep tool. The clock template is a play tool.|

These are edge-case limitations, not architecture failures. The core pipeline — Schema to pressure points to fronts — holds up. The gaps are at the inputs (how to generate scores without a full questionnaire) and the outputs (how to instantiate clocks when the NPC enters active play). The middle of the pipeline works.

*Document 1B (Profiling Companions Guide) extends this instrument to players, tables, institutions, territories, and gods. The architecture is identical. The content changes. The instrument is universal.*

**DOCUMENT 1A — ADDENDUM**

Diagnostic Findings: Corrected Resolutions

*Supersedes Section 15 of Document 1A (Psyche Schema System Reference v4)*

The original diagnostic findings in Section 15 were identified during proof-of-concept testing under the pre-unified architecture. The unified spine, the universal character sheet principle, and the regulatory systems resolve or reframe each finding. This addendum replaces Section 15 with corrected resolutions.

**1. FALSE PRECISION**

**Original finding:** GM-authored NPC scores feel authoritative but are estimates. Why 85 and not 80? There is no derivation formula for GM-generated scores.

**Status:** Resolved.

The system never reads individual scores as absolute values. It reads three relative properties: outlier status (above 80 or below 20), asymmetric splits (gap exceeding 50 points between paired aspects), and pattern breaks (scores contradicting the expected profile). All three are relative readings. A score of 82 and a score of 88 produce identical diagnostic output — both are outliers, both generate 1 Fulcrum Point, both signal a load-bearing conviction.

The 10-band overlay compounds this resolution. The GM does not need the exact number. They need the correct band. Any score within 81-100 maps to d12 and produces the same mechanical expression. The difference between 82 and 88 is invisible to the resolution system.

**Corrected Procedure**

For GM-generated NPCs, assign bands rather than specific numbers. The six bands are: Absent (1-10, no die), Minimal (11-20, d4), Developing (21-40, d6), Established (41-60, d8), Dominant (61-80, d10), Load-bearing (81-100, d12). Band assignment is honest about its resolution. It does not claim decimal precision it cannot support.

Specific numerical scores are a player-facing refinement for characters who complete the full questionnaire. The questionnaire’s structured intake produces answers specific enough to justify placement within a band. NPCs generated through the five-question shortcut produce band assignments. Both produce the same diagnostic output — the pressure point identification reads bands, not points.

**2. HEART’S COMPASS AUTHORSHIP**

**Original finding:** Sections I-II are derivable from position and philosophy. Section III requires GM authorship. This was framed as a gap in the engine.

**Status:** Reframed as correct design.

Schema Theory (Bartlett), one of the spine’s regulatory systems, establishes that participants co-complete underspecified elements with personally meaningful content. The GM is a participant in the spine. The Heart’s Compass is the designated entry point for the GM’s creative investment. The “authorship burden” is the same mechanism as the player’s contribution at Vertebra 6 (Adventures) — it is the participation that makes the output meaningful rather than mechanical.

A fully derivable NPC would be a mechanical output with no soul. The Heart’s Compass is where the GM invests enough personal creative energy that the NPC stops being a structural position and starts being a person. The Corin 35 score derived from “military people have dark humor” IS the GM’s schema filling the gap with their knowledge of military fiction. That is not a failure of the engine. That is the engine working as designed — the underspecification invited the GM’s contribution, and the GM contributed from their own experience.

**Corrected Framing**

Section III is not a derivation gap. It is the designated entry point for GM authorship. The procedure should acknowledge this explicitly:

*Sections I and II are derivable from position and philosophy. Section III is where you make the character a person. The engine provides the structure. You provide the heart. This is not a limitation. This is your contribution to the campfire.*

The five-question shortcut handles this correctly by asking for the fear (one Heart’s Compass data point) and the contradiction (one pattern break). Those two inputs are sufficient for the GM’s schema to fill the remaining Heart’s Compass dimensions through natural co-completion during play. The NPC does not need a complete Heart’s Compass at generation. They need enough for the first encounter. Play reveals the rest.

**3. EXTERNAL PRESSURE GAP**

**Original finding:** The derivation handles NPC-internal pressure (what the Schema reveals) but not NPC-external pressure (what the environment does TO the person regardless of their Schema). This was identified as a missing step in the derivation procedure.

**Status:** Resolved by the universal character sheet principle.

The unified architecture establishes that everything uses the same character sheet. The POSITION has its own pressure profile. A headmaster position generates specific pressures regardless of who occupies it — institutional maintenance demands, inter-college diplomacy requirements, faculty management obligations, resource allocation decisions. These are not derived from the person’s Schema. They are derived from the position’s Schema.

**Corrected Procedure**

Add one step to the NPC-to-Adventure derivation procedure. After reading the NPC’s personal Schema for internal pressure points, read the POSITION’s pressure profile for external pressure points. Two Schema readings — one for the person, one for the role — producing two sets of fronts that interact.

**Internal fronts:** Internal fronts are driven by the NPC’s personal tensions. They exist because of who the person is. Kyril’s Vestus Containment front (from the Veyra pattern break) and The Aíon Silence front (from the Constantine/Mother tension) are internal fronts.

**External fronts:** External fronts are driven by the position’s structural demands. They exist because of what the role requires regardless of who occupies it. Kyril’s Institutional Maintenance front and The Political Leverage Problem are external fronts. Any headmaster of Mortis Ævum would face these pressures.

**The intersection:** The intersection between internal and external fronts is where the richest story lives. How does this specific person’s internal tensions interact with this specific position’s structural demands? Kyril’s personal Vestus containment pulls attention away from the position’s institutional maintenance demand. That interaction — internal front competing with external front for the NPC’s finite attention — is the pressure that produces observable symptoms the party can detect.

This also retroactively explains the Colonel proof-of-concept’s Front 4 (Succession Question). That front was invented by the GM because the personal Schema could not generate it. With the corrected procedure, Front 4 would have been derived from the positional reading — “what does the senior officer position generate?” produces “other officers see this person as a leadership candidate” as a structural demand of the role, not as a property of the person occupying it.

**4. CREATIVE INTERPRETATION STEP**

**Original finding:** The mapping from pressure point to front requires creative interpretation informed by setting knowledge. This step was hidden in the derivation procedure.

**Status:** Named and acknowledged. The step is now explicit.

The step is named: Contextual Interpretation. It sits between pressure point identification (analysis) and front mapping (construction). The procedure should mark the transition clearly:

*Steps 1-2 are analysis. Step 3 is interpretation. You are now contributing, not calculating.*

**The Three Sub-Operations**

**1. Operational Context:** For each pressure point, ask: where in this NPC’s operational life does this tension manifest? The answer requires knowing the NPC’s college methodology, their position’s daily demands, and the current state of the setting around them. Kyril’s Veritas/Gnosis split manifests as the Vestus Containment front because of the specific setting fact that Vestus IS the Gnosis aspect made divine. A different NPC with the same split in a different setting context would produce a completely different front.

**2. Personal Shaping:** For each external pressure point (from the positional reading), ask: how does this NPC’s personal Schema shape their response to the positional demand? The headmaster position demands institutional maintenance. Kyril’s Constantine 95 means he handles this demand through absolute duty. A different headmaster with Constantine 40 would delegate, producing a completely different institutional culture.

**3. Intersection Check:** Check whether any internal and external fronts interact. Do the personal tensions amplify or dampen the positional demands? Where does the NPC’s attention divide between personal and positional fronts? The allocation of finite attention between competing fronts IS the dramatic engine. An NPC whose internal fronts consume all attention neglects their external obligations. An NPC whose external obligations consume all attention suppresses their internal tensions until they erupt.

The creative interpretation step is the same mechanism operating at every level of the campfire — the engine provides the framework, the GM interprets it through their own experience and setting knowledge. The interpretation is the GM’s contribution. It cannot and should not be automated. The procedure’s job is to make the invitation explicit so the GM knows when they are calculating and when they are creating.

**5. CLOCK PARAMETERIZATION TIMING**

**Original finding:** Schema derivation produces fronts with potential clocks, not parameterized clocks. Specific clock values (segments, thresholds, terminal states) need play context. This was framed as a limitation of the derivation procedure.

**Status:** Confirmed as correct architecture.

The spine has distinct prep-phase and play-phase operations. Vertebrae 1-3 (Schema, Pressure Points, Fronts) are prep-phase — they establish what exists before play begins. Vertebrae 4-7 (Clocks, Symptoms, Adventures, Feedback Loop) are play-phase — they operate during and between sessions. The boundary between Fronts and Clocks is the boundary between prep and play.

**Prep-Phase Output**

The Schema derivation produces the front’s clock POTENTIAL — a list of what this front could be tracking, what the tick triggers might be, what the terminal states would look like. This is the menu. The GM creates these during prep without committing to specific mechanical values.

A well-prepped front has 2-4 potential clocks described in narrative terms: “This front could track how aggressively Vestus’s agents probe the archives, or how quickly the containment protocols are being circumvented, or how much of Kyril’s attention is being diverted from institutional maintenance.” Each potential clock is one sentence. No segments. No thresholds. No terminal state mechanics. Just what it would track.

**Play-Phase Output**

When a front enters active play — when the party engages with the area of the setting where the front operates — the GM instantiates specific clocks from the potential menu. The clock template’s nine fields are filled in at this point using the play context to set appropriate values.

Segments are set based on how long the situation should run at the table. A 4-segment clock resolves in one session. A 6-segment clock runs across two or three sessions. An 8-segment clock is a multi-session arc. The GM calibrates to desired pacing, not to abstract correctness.

Tick triggers are set based on what the party is doing. An event-triggered clock ticks when specific party actions occur. A session-triggered clock ticks automatically between sessions, creating pressure regardless of party behavior. A hybrid uses both. The trigger type is chosen based on whether the GM wants the clock to respond to the party (event) or pressure the party (session).

The intervention threshold is set based on dramatic structure. Place it where the transition from “preventable” to “mitigable” produces the most dramatic decision point. Usually halfway to two-thirds through the segments.

**The Advantage of Separation**

The GM can prep extensively — many fronts, many potential clocks — without overcommitting to specifics that play might invalidate. A front whose potential clocks include “tracking Vestus’s probe escalation” becomes an instantiated clock with specific segments only when the party enters the City of Folly and the front becomes relevant. If the party never goes to the City of Folly, the clock potential enriched the GM’s understanding of the setting without requiring mechanical parameterization.

Prep that does not reach the table is not wasted. It is density that informs the GM’s improvisation, colors NPC behavior, and creates the sense of a world in motion beyond the party’s immediate field of view. But density does not need mechanical specifics until it reaches the table. The separation between prep-phase potential and play-phase parameterization is the engine’s efficiency mechanism.

**RESOLUTION SUMMARY**

|**Finding**|**Status**|**Resolution**|
| :- | :- | :- |
|**False Precision**|**Resolved**|The system reads bands, not points. GM-generated NPCs use band assignment (Absent through Load-bearing). Diagnostic reads are relative, not absolute. A score of 82 and 88 are mechanically identical.|
|**Heart’s Compass**|**Reframed**|Not a gap. The designated entry point for GM creative contribution. The engine provides structure. The GM provides the heart. Underspecification invites co-completion through play.|
|**External Pressure**|**Resolved**|Read the position’s Schema alongside the person’s. Two readings, two front sets. The intersection between internal and external fronts is where the richest story lives.|
|**Creative Interp.**|**Named**|The Contextual Interpretation step is now explicit between analysis and front mapping. Three sub-operations: operational context, personal shaping, intersection check.|
|**Clock Timing**|**Confirmed**|Correct architecture, not a limitation. Prep produces potential (narrative descriptions). Play produces parameterization (mechanical values). The boundary between Fronts and Clocks is the boundary between prep and play.|

*The core pipeline — Schema to pressure points to fronts — was sound before these corrections. The corrections refine the edges: how scores are assigned (bands not points), where authorship enters (Heart’s Compass by design), how external pressure is captured (positional Schema reading), where creative judgment is required (named and explicit), and when clocks become mechanical (play-phase, not prep-phase). The architecture holds. The procedure is now complete.*

**PROFILING COMPANIONS GUIDE**

Extending the Schema to Every Entity

Document 1B — Vertebra 1: Schema (Companion)

*The universal character sheet applied beyond individual characters*

**THE PRINCIPLE**

Everything uses the same character sheet. The same profiling instrument. The same pressure extraction. The same Fulcrum Point generation. The same spine. Player characters, NPCs, institutions, territories, and gods are all entities with internal complexity that generates narrative energy proportional to their contradictions.

This document does not define new instruments. It applies the Psyche Schema (Document 1A) to entity types other than individual characters. The architecture is identical at every scale. The content changes. The instrument is universal. The perception filter determines which lens the operator views it through.

Each entity type maps onto the Schema differently — an institution does not have a Heart’s Compass in the same way a person does. But each entity type produces the same diagnostic outputs: outlier values, asymmetric splits, and pattern breaks that generate Fulcrum Points and drive the derivation procedure at Vertebra 2 (Pressure Points) and Vertebra 3 (Fronts).

**PROFILING PLAYERS**

The player is not their character. The player is a participant in the campfire with their own engagement patterns, preferences, and needs. Profiling the player tells the GM what kind of fuel to add to the fire so this participant stays engaged.

**Player Types (Robin Laws)**

Six engagement profiles describing what each player seeks from the game. Most players are composites of two or three types with one dominant. The profile is read from observed behavior across 3-6 sessions, not from asking the player directly. What they engage with reveals their type more reliably than what they say they want.

|**Butt-Kicker**|Wants decisive physical action. Engages most during combat, chases, and confrontations. Disengages during extended planning, investigation, or social negotiation. Feed with: situations where hitting things is the obvious AND correct solution. Create encounter designs that let them shine physically.|
| :- | :- |
