|**Tactician**|Wants puzzles, plans, and optimization. Engages most when analyzing options, designing approaches, and executing strategies. Disengages during roleplay-heavy scenes or railroaded outcomes. Feed with: tactical information, multiple valid approaches, and respect for their plans.|
|**Method Actor**|Wants character moments and emotional scenes. Engages most during NPC interactions, moral dilemmas, and situations that test their character’s personality. Disengages during mechanical combat or content that doesn’t touch their character concept. Feed with: situations that press on their character’s beliefs and instincts.|
|**Storyteller**|Wants dramatic narrative. Engages most during plot revelations, climactic moments, and scenes with thematic weight. Disengages during routine encounters or mechanics-heavy sessions. Feed with: consequences that matter, revelations that recontextualize, and dramatic structure.|
|**Power Gamer**|Wants mechanical mastery and character growth. Engages most during character advancement, loot acquisition, and situations that reward system knowledge. Disengages during scenes where mechanical skill is irrelevant. Feed with: mechanical challenges that reward optimization and character investment.|
|**Casual Player**|Wants to participate without pressure. Engages most when included in the group’s activity without being forced to lead. Disengages when put on the spot or required to drive the session. Feed with: a role in the group’s plans without the burden of inventing the plans.|

**Player Profiling as Pressure Points**

Each player’s type IS a pressure point in the table’s collective Schema. A table with three Butt-Kickers and one Method Actor has an asymmetric split — the Method Actor’s needs are suppressed by the dominant engagement mode. That suppression generates pressure that, if unaddressed, produces disengagement (the Method Actor stops attending) or conflict (the Method Actor disrupts combat-heavy sessions seeking character moments).

The GM’s job is to feed all types every session. Not equally — proportionally. The dominant type gets the most fuel. The minority types get enough to sustain engagement. A session that feeds only one type is a session that loses everyone else. The player profile determines the fuel mix, not the session structure.

**Fulcrum Point Analog**

Players do not generate Fulcrum Points. Their characters do. But the player’s type determines HOW they spend. A Tactician spends FP on Distance 0 (boosting tactical outcomes). A Method Actor spends FP on Distance 1-2 (revealing pressure points or placing character-relevant seeds). A Storyteller spends FP on Distance 2-3 (manipulating clocks and relationship edges for dramatic effect). The player’s type shapes the spend pattern, not the generation.

**PROFILING TABLES**

The table is a group with its own creative agenda, distinct from the individuals composing it. GNS Theory (Ron Edwards) profiles the GROUP’s collective optimization target.

**GNS Theory: The Three Creative Agendas**

|**Gamism**|The table optimizes for challenge and triumph. Encounters are tests of player skill. Character builds matter. Tactical decisions matter. The satisfaction comes from overcoming difficulty through competent play. The system’s mechanical depth is the product.|
| :- | :- |
|**Narrativism**|The table optimizes for meaningful choice and thematic weight. Moral dilemmas matter. Character arcs matter. The satisfaction comes from making decisions that reveal what the characters (and by extension the players) believe. The story’s emotional impact is the product.|
|**Simulationism**|The table optimizes for internal consistency and world logic. The setting’s rules must be coherent. Consequences must follow logically. The satisfaction comes from a world that behaves predictably according to its own established physics. The setting’s believability is the product.|

Most tables are composites with one dominant agenda. The dominant agenda determines which regulatory systems the table prioritizes:

|**Gamist tables**|Weight the Information Economy heavily — information is a tactical resource. The Smoothing Effect is an obstacle to overcome. Transportation is secondary to mechanical engagement.|
| :- | :- |
|**Narrativist tables**|Weight Transportation heavily — the fictional dream must be protected. The Smoothing Effect supports verisimilitude. Information Economy serves dramatic pacing rather than tactical advantage.|
|**Simulationist tables**|Weight Schema Theory (Bartlett) heavily — internal consistency is paramount. The Smoothing Effect IS the physics. Information Economy serves world logic rather than dramatic or tactical needs.|

**Table Profile as Meta-Schema**

The table’s GNS profile is a meta-Schema that governs how the spine’s regulatory systems are prioritized. It does not determine WHAT the spine produces — the same fronts, clocks, and symptoms operate regardless of creative agenda. It determines HOW the table RECEIVES those outputs. A Gamist table encountering the same symptom as a Narrativist table will investigate it differently, engage with it through different mechanics, and judge the session’s success by different criteria.

The GM reads the table’s GNS profile the same way they read an NPC’s Schema — through observed behavior, not through asking. Which sessions produced the most engagement? Which moments did the table remember afterward? Which moments fell flat? The engagement pattern reveals the creative agenda more reliably than any conversation about preferences.

**PROFILING INSTITUTIONS**

An institution has a Schema. Not metaphorically. The institution’s operational philosophy produces outlier values, asymmetric splits, and pattern breaks that generate Fulcrum Points and drive fronts exactly as a character’s Schema does.

**The Institutional Schema**

An institution’s Schema is constructed from three sources:

**Founding Philosophy:** The institution’s official charter, values statement, or founding philosophy maps onto the Core Philosophy dimensions. A college founded on abjuration principles (Deepguard) has high Nomos scores. Its Themis/Lex balance reveals whether the institution prioritizes the spirit or the letter of its mandate. A mercantile guild (Sovereign Exchange) has high Valorin scores. Its Mercatus/Credo balance reveals whether the institution prioritizes fair exchange or predatory extraction.

**Operational Reality:** How the institution actually behaves — distinct from what it claims to value. The gap between charter and practice IS the institutional Goffman gap. Every institution has a front stage (the public mission) and a back stage (the covert operations, the institutional self-interest, the compromises that keep it functioning). Praxis Collegium’s front stage is humanitarian coordination. Its back stage is comprehensive surveillance disguised as interrelated jobs.

**Internal Factions:** Which of the institution’s internal factions are in tension. A monolithic institution with total internal alignment is rare and generates minimal FP. An institution riven by competing philosophies — the Sovereign Exchange’s Mercatus faction against its Credo faction — generates enormous institutional FP from the irreconcilable tension at its core.

**Reading the Institutional Schema**

The same three diagnostic reads apply:

**Outliers:** Institutional values that are extreme produce outliers. Stormpeak’s Bellum Hegemon scores are load-bearing — the institution would cease to exist without its martial identity. That extremity generates FP because the world constantly presents situations where martial identity is insufficient (diplomatic crises, civilian governance, peacetime malaise).

**Asymmetric Splits:** The gap between the institution’s two strongest values. Mortis Ævum’s tension between its overt medical mission and its covert truth-keeping function produces an asymmetric split — the institution simultaneously serves life AND manages the architecture of death. The split generates FP because every operational decision must balance both mandates.

**Pattern Breaks:** Behaviors that contradict the institutional charter. A humanitarian institution conducting surveillance (Praxis). A commercial institution enforcing justice (Sovereign Exchange under certain headmasters). Each break generates FP because the contradiction demands energy to maintain.

**Institutional Fulcrum Points**

The institution’s FP pool is generated by its Schema’s pressure points, the same formula as individual characters. The FP are spent by whoever is OPERATING the institution at the moment — the headmaster, the faction taking its turn, the front’s driving NPC. When Kyril takes a faction turn action on behalf of Mortis Ævum, he draws from BOTH his personal FP pool (generated by his personal Schema) AND the institutional FP pool (generated by the college’s institutional tensions). He is powerful because he is a complex person running a complex institution. Both generate energy. Both fuel his actions.

A simple guild with a straightforward mission and no internal contradictions generates minimal institutional FP. It can take basic faction turn actions but cannot do anything surprising. An institution riven by internal politics, competing factions, and ideological splits generates enormous institutional FP. The internal chaos is mechanically dangerous because it is narratively energetic.

**The Nine Colleges as Institutional Profiles**

Each college’s initiation, persistent test, master state, and failure state (see College Initiations Canon) constitute its institutional Schema. The initiation defines the founding philosophy. The persistent test defines the operational reality. The master state defines the ideal expression. The failure state defines the institutional back stage — what happens when the institution’s process fails and produces slag instead of masters. The gap between master and slag IS the institutional Goffman gap.

**PROFILING TERRITORIES**

A territory has a Schema-equivalent constructed from three systems: its Zoetrope position, its isotope profile, and its institutional landscape.

**Zoetrope Position as Core Philosophy**

The Political Zoetrope’s 16-position civilizational lifecycle IS the territory’s Core Philosophy equivalent. Each position describes the territory’s current governance philosophy, economic structure, and social contract. The directional pressures pulling the territory toward adjacent positions are the territorial equivalent of paired-dimension tension.

A territory at Zoetrope Position 8 (commercial ascendancy) has high Valorin scores by definition — the society’s organizing principle is trade. The Mercatus/Credo balance at Position 8 depends on whether the commercial ascendancy is producing shared prosperity (Mercatus-dominant) or concentrated wealth (Credo-dominant). The transition pressure toward Position 9 or back toward Position 7 IS the territory’s asymmetric split — the forces pulling the society in competing directions.

**Isotope Profile as Resource Landscape**

The 104 isotopes across five planes distributed across the territory’s geography determine what resources exist, who can access them, and which types of action the territory supports. A territory rich in Verdant Font isotopes supports biological operations (Wildpath methodology). A territory rich in Palimpsest isotopes supports information operations (Chronosynclastic methodology). The isotope distribution constrains what fronts CAN do by determining what materials they have access to.

Isotope distribution asymmetries ARE territorial pressure points. A territory with abundant Borderlands isotopes but depleted Verdant Font isotopes has a resource split — physical infrastructure is abundant but biological resources are scarce. That scarcity generates fronts (who controls the scarce resource?) and clocks (how quickly is the scarcity worsening?).

**Institutional Landscape as Operational Context**

Which colleges, guilds, and governments operate in the territory determines the institutional dimension of the territorial Schema. A territory with three competing colleges has a richer institutional Schema (more pressure points, more FP) than one dominated by a single institution. The inter-institutional tensions within the territory are the territorial equivalent of the Heart’s Compass — the emotional architecture is replaced by the relational architecture of competing institutional interests.

**Territorial Fulcrum Points**

A stable territory at a comfortable Zoetrope position with no major factional conflicts generates few FP. Events are predictable. The spine models this territory easily. A territory in Zoetrope transition with competing institutional interests and asymmetric isotope distribution generates many FP. Surprising things happen constantly because the territory’s internal tensions create too many possible states for the spine to predict.

Territorial FP are spent by whatever entity is interacting with the territory at that moment. The GM introduces the territory’s unpredictability through its FP — more FP means more unexpected events, more complications, more emergent situations. When the party enters a high-FP territory, the GM has a larger budget for injecting surprise. When they enter a low-FP territory, events unfold predictably. The territory’s complexity IS its narrative energy.

**PROFILING GODS**

The gods have Schemas. The god documents (Aurelion, Moros, Daedalon, Nomos, Bellum Hegemon, Valorin, Agora, Verdena, Rhapsodia, Vestus, Kanon/Anima, the Selûnae, Thalassus, the Chorus, Aíon) each define a divine Schema through the god’s dual-aspect portfolio, institutional expressions, and internal tensions.

**Divine Schema Architecture**

A god’s Schema is defined by its paired aspects. Each god owns one dimension of the Core Philosophy. The god’s relationship to its own two faces IS its Schema:

**Harmonious Faces:** A god whose two faces are in harmony generates minimal FP. The god is stable. Its portfolio is coherent. Its mortal agents operate without internal conflict from the divine source.

**Tense Faces:** A god whose two faces are in tension generates enormous FP. Valorin’s Mercatus (fair trade) versus Credo (predatory extraction) are irreconcilable. The god IS the tension. Bellum Hegemon’s Kaelen (productive conflict) versus Bellax (suffering) are in permanent competition. Kanon versus Anima are two separate gods in structural opposition. Each tension generates divine FP that flows through the god’s mortal agents and institutional expressions.

**Divine Fulcrum Points**

Divine FP flow DOWNWARD through the institutional hierarchy. The god’s internal tension generates FP. Those FP are available to the god’s mortal agents (Saints, Legends, clergy, initiated practitioners) through their divine resonance. A character whose Schema scores align strongly with a tense god has access to more divine FP than a character aligned with a harmonious god — not because the tense god is more powerful, but because its internal contradictions generate more narrative energy that its resonant mortals can channel.

This means the gods who are most interesting narratively are also most mechanically potent through their mortal agents. The dramatic gods — the ones whose portfolios contain irreconcilable tensions — produce more FP than the stable gods. This is correct by design. The campfire burns brightest with fuel that contains internal energy.

**Aíon as Special Case**

Aíon’s FP generation is theoretically infinite because Aíon’s Schema encompasses the tension between TWO MERGED SETTINGS. The contradiction at the foundation of reality itself. The Smoothing Effect is Aíon continuously spending FP to maintain coherence — infinite narrative energy allocated to preventing the world from tearing apart at the seams.

When a participant spends FP to override the Smoothing Effect (Distance ∞), they are momentarily outspending Aíon’s continuous maintenance expenditure at one specific point. The Smoothing Effect reasserts because Aíon’s generation is functionally infinite. The participant punched through momentarily, and the maintenance resumes. The deferred consequence of overriding Smoothing is Aíon’s architecture noticing the discontinuity and responding.

**THE UNIVERSAL APPLICATION**

Every entity type produces the same diagnostic outputs through the same instrument:

|**Output**|**Character**|**Institution**|**Territory**|**God**|
| :- | :- | :- | :- | :- |
|**Outliers**|Score >80 or <20|Extreme institutional values|Dominant Zoetrope position pressure|Dominant aspect face|
|**Splits**|Paired gap >50 pts|Charter vs. practice gap|Resource distribution asymmetry|Irreconcilable aspect tension|
|**Breaks**|Score contradicts expected profile|Behavior contradicts charter|Institution contradicts position|Mortal expression contradicts portfolio|
|**FP per cycle**|Count of above|Count of above|Count of above|Count of above|
|**Front source**|Personal tensions|Institutional tensions|Factional competition|Aspect tension flowing to mortals|
|**Clock source**|Personal persistent test|Institutional operations|Zoetrope transition pressure|Generational aspect cycling|

The spine does not change between scales. The content that feeds into each vertebra changes. A character’s outlier score and an institution’s extreme charter value enter the same pressure extraction procedure at Vertebra 2. A territory’s resource asymmetry and a god’s aspect tension enter the same front derivation at Vertebra 3. The instrument is universal. The entities are different. The architecture is identical.

**Stacking: The Composite Entity**

An NPC operating within an institution within a territory under a god’s resonance is a COMPOSITE entity. Their available FP is the sum of all applicable pools:

Personal FP (from their individual Schema) + Positional FP (from the role’s structural demands, per Document 1A Addendum) + Institutional FP (from the college’s internal tensions) + Territorial FP (from the region’s instability) + Divine FP (from the resonant god’s aspect tension, channeled through alignment).

This stacking is why headmasters are the most narratively potent entities in the setting short of the gods themselves. A headmaster is a complex person (personal FP) in a demanding role (positional FP) running a contradictory institution (institutional FP) within a politically charged territory (territorial FP) resonating with a tense god (divine FP). Every layer compounds. Every layer generates energy. The composite entity’s narrative potency is the sum of all layers’ internal tensions.

Adventurers participate in the same stacking. A Stormpeak-trained fighter operating in the Titanwood theater under Bellum Hegemon’s resonance has personal FP, the soldier role’s positional FP, Stormpeak’s institutional FP (accessed through their commission), the Titanwood’s territorial FP (high — the territory is in permanent instability), and Bellum Hegemon’s divine FP (moderate to high depending on the Kaelen/Bellax tension in their personal Schema alignment). The adventurer is not separate from the system. They are a participant in the same architecture that governs everything else.

**PRACTICAL APPLICATION**

The GM does not need to calculate all stacking layers for every entity in every scene. The stacking model describes the architecture. At the table, the GM uses a simplified approach:

|**Minor NPCs**|Personal FP only. Use band assignment from the five-question shortcut (Document 1A). Typical range: 1-3 FP. Do not track. Assume they have just enough to surprise the party once.|
| :- | :- |
|**Notable NPCs**|Personal + Positional FP. Track on the NPC Assessment Card. Typical range: 4-8 FP. These NPCs can make Distance 0-2 spends. They matter to the story.|
|**Major NPCs**|Full stack applicable. Personal + Positional + Institutional. Track on the front sheet. Typical range: 8-15+ FP. These NPCs can make Distance 2-3 spends. They shape the setting.|
|**Territories**|The GM uses the territory’s FP budget to determine how much unpredictability to inject when the party is in that region. High-FP territories produce more oracle-driven events, more unexpected complications, more emergent situations. Low-FP territories are stable and predictable.|
|**Institutional Actions**|When a college takes a faction turn action, the institutional FP budget determines how much narrative energy the action has. A high-FP institution can produce surprising, ambitious, far-reaching faction turn results. A low-FP institution takes predictable, conservative actions.|
|**Divine Influence**|Divine FP flowing through resonant mortals is the mechanical expression of divine favor. The GM does not track divine FP explicitly. When a character invokes divine resonance (acting within the god’s portfolio), the GM considers whether the god’s aspect tension supports a dramatic outcome and adjudicates accordingly.|

The stacking model exists so the GM understands WHY certain entities are more narratively potent than others. The practical application simplifies the model to three tiers of NPC tracking (minor, notable, major) and qualitative assessment for territories, institutions, and divine influence. The architecture is rigorous. The table experience is fluid.

*This completes the Vertebra 1 documentation. Document 2A (Pressure Extraction Procedures) builds directly from the Schema outputs described here — the same diagnostic reads (outliers, splits, breaks) applied through the Ten Questions and stored on the NPC Assessment Card.*

**PRESSURE EXTRACTION**

Procedures for Reading Systems Under Stress

Document 2A — Vertebra 2: Pressure Points

*The extraction methods, diagnostic tools, and storage procedures for the spine’s second vertebra*

**WHAT PRESSURE EXTRACTION IS**

Vertebra 2 reads Vertebra 1’s output for where the energy concentrates. The Schema (Document 1A) maps the internal landscape. Pressure extraction identifies the specific locations within that landscape where the terrain is unstable — the points where stress accumulates, contradictions surface, and narrative energy is generated.

Every pressure point identified at this vertebra becomes a potential front at Vertebra 3, a potential clock at Vertebra 4, a potential seed at Vertebra 5, and a potential adventure at Vertebra 6. The quality of the spine’s output depends entirely on the quality of extraction at this step. Shallow extraction produces shallow stories. Thorough extraction produces stories that surprise even the GM who built them.

Two instruments operate here: the Ten Questions (real-time extraction during play) and the Schema Diagnostic Reads (prep-phase extraction from completed profiles). Both feed into the NPC Assessment Card (persistent storage). Together they form a complete extraction apparatus — one tool for the moment, one for preparation, one for memory.

**THE TEN QUESTIONS**

Same ten questions. Every situation. Every entity. Whether you are a player reading a room, a GM preparing a session, or a novelist deconstructing a scene. The questions are the real-time extraction method for identifying pressure in any system you are currently observing.

|**#**|**Question**|**What It Extracts**|**Pressure Type**|
| :- | :- | :- | :- |
|**1**|**What does everyone here want?**|Not what they say. What they actually want. Every entity at the table has an objective even if they have not articulated it. The gap between stated and actual want IS a pressure point.|*Motivational*|
|**2**|**Who has the most to lose?**|The entity with the most at stake acts the most unpredictably. Their vulnerability is both a pressure point and a liability. The highest-stakes participant shapes the situation’s dynamics disproportionately.|*Motivational*|
|**3**|**What’s the exit?**|Before engaging, know how you leave. Conversation, combat, negotiation — the exit determines the shape of the engagement. An entity without an exit is cornered, and cornered entities are dangerous.|*Positional*|
|**4**|**What do I know for certain versus what am I assuming?**|Separate confirmed from inferred. Act on confirmed. Test inferred. The boundary between knowledge and assumption IS a pressure point because every assumption is a potential failure point.|*Informational*|
|**5**|**What resource is being spent and by whom?**|Time, spell slots, goodwill, patience, money, attention. Someone is always spending something. Identify the currency in play. Resource depletion generates urgency. The party spending the fastest is the party under the most pressure.|*Resource*|
|**6**|**What happens if I do nothing?**|The default outcome. The Default Timeline for this specific moment. Sometimes the optimal action is letting the situation resolve itself while everyone else exhausts their options. The default outcome IS the pressure’s natural trajectory.|*Positional*|
|**7**|**Who is performing and who is being genuine?**|The performer has an audience in mind. The genuine actor has an objective. Different leverage for each. The gap between performance and genuineness IS the Goffman gap — the front stage/back stage divide made observable in real time.|*Motivational*|
|**8**|**What’s the second-order consequence?**|Not what happens next — what does THAT cause? Every action produces a cascade. The second-order consequence is where the unexpected lives. Identifying it before it arrives converts surprise into preparation.|*Resource*|
|**9**|**What don’t I know that I should?**|The intelligence gap. Name it before it names you. If you cannot identify what you are missing, you are not ready to commit. The gap itself IS a pressure point because operating without critical information is operating under risk.|*Informational*|
|**10**|**Is this my problem?**|Not every situation requires engagement. Knowing when to step back is as tactical as knowing when to engage. The relevance question filters which pressure points are worth investing in and which will resolve without intervention.|*Relevance*|

**The Five Pressure Types**

The ten questions cluster into five pressure categories. Each category reveals a different kind of instability in the system:

|**Motivational (Q1, Q2, Q7)**|What entities want, what they stand to lose, and whether they are showing their true objectives. Motivational pressure drives fronts — a motivated actor pursuing a hidden objective through a performed exterior IS a front in miniature.|
| :- | :- |
|**Positional (Q3, Q6)**|Where entities are in the situation’s topology and what happens if they do nothing. Positional pressure reveals the default trajectory and the exit options. Entities with no exit generate the most volatile pressure.|
|**Informational (Q4, Q9)**|What is known versus assumed, and what gaps exist. Informational pressure drives investigation — every identified gap is a lead, and every unidentified gap is a risk.|
|**Resource (Q5, Q8)**|What is being spent and what cascades from the expenditure. Resource pressure generates urgency and reveals timeline constraints. When resources run out, the situation changes state.|
|**Relevance (Q10)**|Whether this pressure belongs to the observer at all. The filter that prevents engagement with every pressure point. Some situations resolve themselves. Some situations are other entities’ problems. The relevance check prevents overextension.|

**Using the Questions at the Table**

The ten questions are not a checklist to work through sequentially. They are a diagnostic reflex. When a scene begins and the player or GM senses uncertainty about what is happening, the questions identify which specific uncertainty is generating the discomfort. Usually one or two questions produce the critical insight. The rest confirm what is already visible.

**For players:** When the table stalls: run the ten questions internally. The question whose answer you cannot produce is the question that identifies the stall’s source. If you cannot identify what everyone wants (Q1), the motivational landscape is unclear — the situation lacks defined actors. If you cannot identify what happens if you do nothing (Q6), the default trajectory is unclear — the situation lacks urgency. If you cannot identify the gap (Q9), the informational landscape is opaque — the situation lacks investigable leads.

**For GMs:** The ten questions generate the raw material for the NPC Assessment Card. Each NPC encountered during play should produce answers to at least Q1 (want), Q7 (performing or genuine), and Q4 (what’s confirmed versus assumed about them). These three answers populate the Card’s core fields.

**THE SCHEMA DIAGNOSTIC READS**

The prep-phase extraction method. Applied to completed or partially completed Schemas before play begins. Three reads, each identifying a different type of pressure point.

**Read 1: Outlier Detection**

**Procedure:** Scan every Schema score for values above 80 or below 20 (or Load-bearing / Absent bands for GM-generated NPCs). Each outlier is a pressure point.

**Why outliers generate pressure:** High outliers (80+) are load-bearing convictions. The entity’s identity would collapse without this value. Reality constantly tests load-bearing convictions because the world does not accommodate extremes without friction. A character with Constantine 95 (Duty) is under permanent pressure because every situation presents competing claims on their attention that duty alone cannot resolve.

Low outliers (below 20) are absent capacities. The entity cannot access this dimension when the situation demands it. A character with Corin 5 (Playfulness) has no mechanism for levity, stress relief, or perspective shift through humor. The absence is structural brittleness — the entity functions, but the missing dimension is a fault line that sufficient stress will expose.

**Read 2: Asymmetric Split Identification**

**Procedure:** Scan every paired dimension for gaps exceeding 50 points between the two aspects. Each split is a pressure point.

**Why splits generate pressure:** A split this large is not a preference. It is an emotional wound disguised as a philosophical position. Kyril’s Veritas 90 / Gnosis 15 (a 75-point gap) does not mean he merely prefers contextual knowledge over raw data. It means his relationship to decontextualized knowledge is so aversive that it distorts his judgment. He cannot engage with Gnosis-aligned situations rationally because the suppressed aspect is too far below access threshold.

The world will inevitably present situations where the suppressed aspect is the correct response. When it does, the entity either fails (cannot access the needed capacity) or breaks (the suppressed aspect erupts uncontrolled). Both outcomes generate story. The asymmetric split is the most reliable front generator in the Schema because it guarantees that the world and the entity will eventually collide on the dimension the entity cannot handle.

**Read 3: Pattern Break Recognition**

**Procedure:** Compare each score against the expected profile for the entity’s college, role, and presented identity. Any score that contradicts the expected profile is a pattern break. Each break is a pressure point.

**Why breaks generate pressure:** Pattern breaks indicate hidden pressures the entity themselves may not be aware of. Kyril’s Veyra 55 (Unrequited) breaks the expected profile of an immortal warden whose emotional range has compressed to institutional service. An entity in his position should not have a moderate-high unrequited connection score. The fact that he does means something is happening beneath the surface that the rest of the profile cannot explain.

Pattern breaks generate the richest fronts because they produce mystery fronts — situations where the entity’s own behavior surprises them. The entity acts in ways their conscious self-model cannot account for. These are the adventures that lead toward the deepest revelations.

**THE DUAL READING**

Every NPC occupies a role. The role has its own pressure profile independent of who fills it. Pressure extraction requires TWO readings: the personal Schema (who the entity is) and the positional profile (what the role demands).

**The Personal Reading**

The three diagnostic reads above applied to the entity’s individual Schema. This produces internal pressure points — tensions that exist because of who the person IS, regardless of what role they occupy. Kyril’s Vestus Containment front and Aíon Silence front are internal. They would follow him into any role.

**The Positional Reading**

Ask: what pressures does this POSITION generate independent of who occupies it? A headmaster position demands institutional maintenance, inter-college diplomacy, faculty management, resource allocation, and succession planning regardless of the headmaster’s personal Schema. A military officer position demands tactical decisions, personnel management, morale maintenance, and chain-of-command navigation regardless of the officer’s personality.

The positional reading produces external pressure points — tensions that exist because of the ROLE, not the person. Kyril’s Institutional Maintenance front and Political Leverage Problem are external. Any headmaster of Mortis Ævum would face these pressures.

**The Intersection**

The richest story lives where internal and external fronts compete for the entity’s finite attention. How does this specific person’s internal tensions interact with this specific role’s structural demands?

Kyril’s personal Vestus containment (internal front) pulls attention away from institutional maintenance (external front). The institutional decay that results is not caused by Kyril’s incompetence — it is caused by a complex person whose internal pressures divert attention from positional demands. The party detects the institutional decay (a symptom at Vertebra 5) and investigates. The investigation leads to the front. The front leads to the intersection. The intersection reveals both who Kyril is AND what the position demands of him. Both are necessary to understand the situation.

**THE CONTEXTUAL INTERPRETATION STEP**

Between pressure point identification (analysis) and front mapping (construction at Vertebra 3), a creative interpretation step is required. This step is explicitly named because it represents the transition from calculation to contribution.

*Steps 1-2 of the derivation are analysis. This step is interpretation. You are now contributing, not calculating.*

**The Three Sub-Operations**

**1. Operational Context:** For each pressure point, ask: where in this entity’s operational life does this tension manifest? The answer requires knowing the entity’s college methodology, their position’s daily demands, and the current state of the setting around them. The same pressure point in different operational contexts produces completely different fronts. A Veritas/Gnosis split in a Chronosynclastic analyst produces an intelligence front. The same split in a Wildpath Warden produces a survival methodology front. Context determines expression.

**2. Personal Shaping:** For each external pressure point (from the positional reading), ask: how does this entity’s personal Schema shape their response to the positional demand? The position demands institutional maintenance. A headmaster with Constantine 95 handles this through absolute duty. A headmaster with Constantine 40 delegates and produces a completely different institutional culture. The personal Schema is the lens through which positional demands are processed.

**3. Intersection Check:** Check whether any internal and external fronts interact. Where does the entity’s attention divide between personal and positional fronts? An entity whose internal fronts consume all attention neglects their external obligations — producing observable symptoms of institutional decay that the party can detect. An entity whose external obligations consume all attention suppresses their internal tensions until they erupt — producing sudden behavioral anomalies that break the expected pattern.

The creative interpretation step cannot and should not be automated. It is the GM’s contribution to the campfire at this vertebra. The procedure’s job is to make the invitation explicit so the GM knows when they are calculating (pressure point identification) and when they are creating (contextual interpretation).

**THE REGULATORY DIAGNOSTICS**

Two regulatory systems are most active at this vertebra.

**McDowall Diagnostic (Information / Choice / Consequence)**

When you cannot find pressure points, one of three elements is absent from the situation:

|**Information missing**|You cannot extract pressure because you do not have enough data about the entity. The Schema is incomplete. The ten questions are unanswered. The solution is not deeper analysis — it is more data. Ask more questions. Observe more behavior. Read more of the profile.|
| :- | :- |
|**Choice missing**|You can see the pressure but it is not generating fronts because the entity has no meaningful options. They are locked into a single course of action. The solution is introducing competing demands — give them something else they want that conflicts with what they are already pursuing.|
|**Consequence missing**|The pressure points exist but nothing happens when they are stressed. The entity’s tensions have no stakes attached. The solution is connecting the pressure points to things the entity (or the party, or the setting) cares about. What happens if this tension breaks?|

The McDowall diagnostic is the fastest tool for unsticking extraction. When the ten questions and the Schema reads are not producing useful output, check which of the three elements is absent. Supply it. The extraction resumes.

**Red Team Method — First Application**

Adopt the adversary’s perspective on the pressure landscape. After identifying the entity’s pressure points through the personal and positional readings, ask: if I were an adversary targeting this entity, which pressure points would I exploit?

The adversary’s perspective reveals which pressure points are VULNERABLE — not just where tension exists, but where tension can be weaponized. The pressure point that the entity has managed for decades is stable tension. The pressure point that the entity does not know exists is exploitable tension. Red Teaming the pressure landscape identifies which points are managed, which are unmanaged, and which are invisible to the entity.

This is the method’s first of three applications. At Vertebra 3 (Fronts), Red Team validates whether a front’s behavior is strategically coherent. At the Catalysts layer, Red Team checks whether the operator (the GM or the campfire-lighter) has become visible.

**EXPRESSION GUIDANCE: EVIDENTIARY PRESENTATION**

Vertebra 2’s strongest expression bond is Evidentiary Presentation — presenting raw observational data rather than interpreted conclusions. This bond is strong because pressure extraction is fundamentally an analytical process that operates on evidence, not inference.

**The Journalism Principle**

Show the primary source. Let the audience interpret. At the table, this means the GM (or the investigating player) presents what was OBSERVED, not what it MEANS.

*The merchant touched his collar twice while saying the shipment was delayed. He looked toward the warehouse when he mentioned his supplier. His voice dropped when he said the word ‘bandits.’*

These are three pieces of raw behavioral evidence. The GM does not say “the merchant is lying.” The GM presents the behaviors. The party draws the conclusion. When the party draws its own conclusion, the conclusion is more convincing because they reached it themselves. The evidence was the call. The interpretation is the response. The participation makes the discovery feel earned rather than delivered.

**Applying Evidentiary Presentation to Schema Reads**

When the GM identifies a pressure point through the Schema diagnostic reads, the pressure point is not presented as “this NPC has an asymmetric split between Veritas and Gnosis.” That is an analytical conclusion. The pressure point is presented through its observable symptoms — the NPC’s specific behaviors that result from the split. The chirurgeon who flinches when asked about research methodology. The officer who changes the subject when philosophical questions arise. The merchant who maintains meticulous records but never reads them.

Each observable behavior is one piece of evidence that the party (or the reader, or the community member) collects and assembles into a picture. The picture they assemble is shaped by their own schema (Bartlett) — they see the pattern that their experience prepares them to recognize. Different participants may assemble different pictures from the same evidence. Both pictures are valid. The evidence is the same. The interpretation is the contribution.

**THE COMPLETE EXTRACTION PROCEDURE**

The full sequence from Schema to stored pressure points, incorporating all instruments.

|**Step**|**Phase**|**Action**|**Output**|
| :- | :- | :- | :- |
|**1**|**Analysis**|Outlier Detection|List of all scores above 80 or below 20 (or Load-bearing / Absent bands). Each is a pressure point.|
|**2**|**Analysis**|Asymmetric Split Identification|List of all paired dimensions with gaps exceeding 50 points. Each is a pressure point.|
|**3**|**Analysis**|Pattern Break Recognition|List of all scores contradicting the expected profile for college, role, and presented identity. Each is a pressure point.|
|**4**|**Analysis**|Positional Reading|List of pressures the ROLE generates independent of the person. Institutional demands, structural obligations, inter-entity diplomacy requirements.|
|**5**|**Creative**|Contextual Interpretation|For each pressure point: where it manifests operationally, how the personal Schema shapes the response, and where internal and external fronts intersect.|
|**6**|**Storage**|NPC Assessment Card|Record all findings on the persistent storage form. Want, fear, Goffman gap, contradictions log, pressure point summary, FP generation estimate.|
|**7**|**Validation**|Red Team Check|Adopt the adversary’s perspective. Which pressure points are managed, unmanaged, or invisible to the entity? Which are exploitable?|
|**8**|**Diagnostic**|McDowall Check|If extraction produced insufficient results: which of Information, Choice, or Consequence is missing from the situation? Supply it.|

Steps 1-4 are analysis — mechanical reads of the Schema and position that require no creative judgment. Step 5 is creative — the GM’s interpretive contribution. Step 6 is storage — recording the results persistently. Steps 7-8 are validation — checking the work and diagnosing gaps.

During play, the Ten Questions serve as the real-time version of Steps 1-3. The player asking Q7 (“Who is performing?”) is running a live Goffman gap detection. The player asking Q2 (“Who has the most to lose?”) is running a live outlier read on the motivational landscape. The Ten Questions are the field version of the diagnostic reads that the GM performs during prep with the full Schema available.

**FULCRUM POINT CALCULATION**

The extraction procedure produces the FP generation count as a byproduct.

**For individual entities:** Count all pressure points identified in Steps 1-3 of the extraction procedure. This count IS the entity’s FP generation per session. No additional formula is needed. The extraction procedure and the FP generation procedure are the same operation.

**For institutions:** Count the institutional pressure points identified in Step 4’s positional reading applied to the institution’s own Schema (Document 1B). This count is the institutional FP pool, available to whoever operates the institution.

**For composite entities:** Sum the personal FP (Step 1-3) and the applicable positional, institutional, territorial, and divine FP pools (Document 1B). The composite FP budget determines the GM’s total narrative energy budget for this entity in this context.

The pressure extraction procedure at Vertebra 2 is simultaneously the diagnostic tool (finding where the story lives), the front generator input (each pressure point maps to a front at Vertebra 3), and the economy tool (each pressure point generates one FP). These three functions are not separate operations. They are three readings of the same output.

*The extracted pressure points feed directly into Vertebra 3 (Fronts). Document 3A describes how each pressure point maps to a front through the contextual interpretation step, how fronts connect through relationship maps (Document 3B), and how fronts take action through faction turns (Document 3C). The NPC Assessment Card (Document 2B) is the physical form that stores all extraction output persistently across sessions.*

**NPC ASSESSMENT CARD**

Persistent Pressure Storage Template

Document 2B — Vertebra 2: Pressure Points (Companion)

*One card per NPC. Print and fill during play. The GM’s field intelligence file.*

This document contains the fillable NPC Assessment Card template and its usage instructions. The card is the persistent storage form for all pressure extraction output (Document 2A). One card per notable or major NPC. Minor NPCs do not require cards — their pressure is simple enough to hold in memory or note on a scrap.

Print the card on the next two pages. Fill it progressively across sessions. The card accumulates intelligence over time — first-encounter entries are sparse. Tenth-encounter entries are dense. The density of the card IS the GM’s measure of how well they know this NPC.

**USAGE INSTRUCTIONS**

**When to Create a Card**

Create a card when an NPC transitions from minor to notable — when the party interacts with them a second time, when they become relevant to an active front, or when the GM identifies them as a potential front driver during prep. Do not create cards preemptively for NPCs the party may never encounter. Create them when they matter.

**Progressive Fill**

The card is not filled in all at once. It accumulates across sessions.

**First encounter:** Name, college, position. Want if known. Leave everything else blank.

**Second encounter:** Fear if revealed. Performing or genuine assessment. First contradictions log entry if anything surprised you.

**Sessions 3-5:** Goffman gap observations. Relationship edges to other NPCs. Pressure points becoming visible through accumulated behavior.

**When the NPC becomes a front driver:** Front potential section. By now the card has enough data to identify which pressure points map to potential fronts and what clocks those fronts could drive. This is the prep-phase output described in Document 2A.

**The Contradictions Log**

The most important section on the card. Date each entry by session number. Record what the NPC said or did that contradicted their previous behavior, their stated position, or their expected profile. Each contradiction is a live Goffman gap detection — a moment where the back stage leaked through the front stage.

Over time, the contradictions log reveals the NPC’s actual character more reliably than their stated position. A log with zero entries after five sessions means the NPC is either exactly what they present (rare) or the observer has not been looking closely enough (common). A log with multiple entries is a goldmine — each contradiction is a potential mystery front waiting to be mapped.

**The Relationship Edges Section**

Records the NPC’s connections to other NPCs with three properties per edge:

**Trust:** How freely information flows between them. High trust = honest communication. Low trust = guarded, filtered, or deceptive communication.

**Debt:** Latent obligations. Who owes whom. Debts create leverage. Unresolved debts accumulate tension.

**Tension:** Current stress on the connection. High tension = the relationship is approaching a state change. When tension exceeds threshold, the state changes (Ally → Neutral → Rival → Enemy or the reverse).

This section is the raw data for the Relationship Map (Document 3B). The cards store individual edges. The map visualizes the network.

**The Front Potential Section**

Filled during prep, not during play. After the card has accumulated enough data (typically 3-5 sessions of observation), the GM reviews the pressure points and maps each to a potential front:

**Internal:** Fronts driven by the NPC’s personal Schema tensions. Outlier scores generate maintenance fronts. Asymmetric splits generate conflict fronts. Pattern breaks generate mystery fronts.

**External:** Fronts driven by the position’s structural demands. What the role requires regardless of who occupies it.

**Mystery:** Fronts emerging from pattern breaks where the NPC’s own behavior surprises them. These are the richest adventure generators.

Each front potential entry includes a one-sentence description of what clock that front could drive. These are narrative descriptions, not parameterized clocks — the clock is instantiated at play-phase when the front becomes active (see Document 4A).

**When to Retire a Card**

A card is retired when the NPC is no longer relevant to the campaign — deceased, departed from the region, or all associated fronts resolved. Retired cards are not discarded. They become the Feedback Loop’s raw material (Vertebra 7) — the record of past NPCs whose consequences may still propagate through the relationship map. A retired NPC whose debt edges are still live on other NPCs’ cards is not truly retired. Their influence persists through the network.

**QUICK REFERENCE: FIELD SOURCES**

Where each field’s data comes from in the extraction procedure.

|**Card Field**|**Source**|**When Filled**|
| :- | :- | :- |
|**Name / Codename**|Encounter|First encounter|
|**College / Position**|Setting knowledge or Q1|First encounter or prep|
|**Personal Clock**|GM shortcut Q5|Prep or after 2-3 encounters|
|**Want**|Ten Questions Q1|First encounter, refined over time|
|**Fear**|GM shortcut Q3 or play|When revealed through behavior or dialogue|
|**Performing / Genuine**|Ten Questions Q7|First encounter, updated as evidence accumulates|
|**Goffman Gap**|Schema Read + observation|After enough encounters to compare front/back stage|
|**Outliers**|Schema Read 1|Prep (full Schema) or derived from observed behavior|
|**Asymmetric Splits**|Schema Read 2|Prep (full Schema) or inferred from contradictions|
|**Pattern Breaks**|Schema Read 3|Accumulated observation over multiple sessions|
|**Positional Pressure**|Positional Reading|Prep (what does this role demand?)|
|**FP Generation**|Count of pressure points|After pressure points section is filled|
|**Contradictions Log**|Live observation|Progressive — one entry per observed contradiction|
|**Relationship Edges**|Observation + setting knowledge|Progressive — added as connections become visible|
|**Front Potential**|Contextual Interpretation|Prep — after card has 3-5 sessions of data|

The card’s density is the GM’s own metric. A sparse card means the NPC is not yet understood. A dense card means the NPC is ready to drive fronts. The transition from sparse to dense IS the pressure extraction process operating across sessions — the GM accumulating intelligence the same way an analyst builds a dossier.

*This completes Vertebra 2 documentation. The extracted pressure points and their front potential entries feed directly into Vertebra 3: Document 3A (Front Architecture), Document 3B (Relationship Map System), and Document 3C (Faction Turn Procedures).*

**FRONT ARCHITECTURE**

Organized Actors, Plans, and Connections

Document 3A — Vertebra 3: Fronts

*The organizational layer of the spine — where pressure becomes action*

**WHAT A FRONT IS**

A front is a motivated actor pursuing a goal through a specific methodology with specific resources along a trajectory that will produce a specific outcome if no one intervenes. It is the organizing structure that converts pressure points (Vertebra 2) into clocks (Vertebra 4). Without fronts, pressure points are static observations. With fronts, pressure points become stories in motion.

A front is not a villain. A front is not a plot. A front is a situation with momentum. The Wildpath Wardens running containment operations in the Titanwood is a front. The Sovereign Exchange expanding trade routes into contested territory is a front. A chirurgeon trending toward numbness while managing a hospital wing is a front. Any entity pursuing an objective through sustained action over time is running a front, whether they intend to or not.

The spine produces fronts from pressure points through the Contextual Interpretation step (Document 2A). Each pressure point identified at Vertebra 2 maps to at least one front. The front’s character is determined by which type of pressure point generated it.

**FRONT GENERATION: FROM PRESSURE TO ACTION**

The Contextual Interpretation step (Document 2A, Step 5) maps each pressure point to a front. The mapping follows the pressure point’s type:

**Maintenance Fronts — from Outlier Scores**

An outlier score (above 80 or below 20) represents a load-bearing conviction or an absent capacity. The entity is actively investing energy in sustaining or suppressing the extreme value. That investment IS a front — the entity’s ongoing effort to maintain the extreme. Kyril’s Constantine 95 generates the Institutional Maintenance front because the duty IS the institution’s operation. The front exists because the outlier demands continuous energy.

Maintenance fronts are the background hum. They run constantly. They rarely produce dramatic symptoms on their own. They become dramatic when OTHER fronts divert the entity’s attention and the maintenance front begins to degrade. The first sign that something is wrong is usually the maintenance front faltering — the institution’s routine slipping, the commitment wavering, the suppressed dimension leaking through.

**Conflict Fronts — from Asymmetric Splits**

An asymmetric split (paired gap exceeding 50 points) means the world will inevitably present situations that require the suppressed aspect. The entity either fails or breaks. The conflict front is the story of that collision — the entity’s dominant aspect encountering a situation that demands the aspect they cannot access.

Kyril’s Veritas 90 / Gnosis 15 split generates the Vestus Containment front because Vestus IS the Gnosis aspect made divine. The conflict is structural — Kyril cannot engage with decontextualized knowledge-seeking without distortion, and the world keeps presenting him with exactly that demand through Vestus’s institutional probing.

Conflict fronts produce the most dramatic clock moments because they force the entity into encounters they are structurally unable to handle well. The intervention threshold on a conflict front’s clock is the point where the entity’s coping mechanism fails and the suppressed aspect erupts.

**Mystery Fronts — from Pattern Breaks**

A pattern break (score contradicting expected profile) means the entity is doing something their conscious self-model cannot explain. The mystery front is the story of that hidden pressure surfacing — the entity’s behavior generating observable anomalies that neither they nor the observer can immediately account for.

Kyril’s Veyra 55 break generates the double-blind dynamic with Vestus. The mystery front is the richest adventure generator because it leads toward revelations that reshape the campaign’s understanding of the setting. Mystery fronts are the fronts the party investigates — the symptoms are anomalous behavior that doesn’t match the entity’s known profile, and the investigation leads toward the hidden truth.

**Operational Fronts — from Positional Demands**

The positional reading (Document 2A, Step 4) produces external pressure points — demands the role generates regardless of who occupies it. Each positional demand maps to an operational front. These are the fronts that any entity in this position would run: institutional obligations, structural dependencies, inter-entity diplomacy.

Operational fronts are not derived from the Schema. They are derived from the position. Any headmaster of Mortis Ævum runs the Institutional Maintenance front because the position demands it. Any senior Stormpeak officer runs a personnel management front because the role requires it. The personal Schema shapes HOW the entity handles the operational front, not WHETHER the front exists.

**THE FRONT TEMPLATE**

Eight fields define a front. Each field connects to a specific source in the preceding vertebrae.

|**#**|**Field**|**What It Does**|**Source**|
| :- | :- | :- | :- |
|**1**|**Identity**|Who or what is driving this front. Named and specific. A faction, institution, specific NPC, coalition, or location.|The setting’s existing actors. Colleges, guilds, governments, individuals with institutional backing, or reactive environments.|
|**2**|**Motivation**|WHY this front pursues its goal. Mapped to the driving NPC’s Schema AND college philosophy. Not “they want power” — which KIND of power, pursued for which REASONS, shaped by which persistent test.|Schema scores + college affiliation + personal clock position. A master-trending NPC has different motivation than a failure-trending NPC running the same institutional machinery.|
|**3**|**Methodology**|HOW the front pursues its goal. Determines clock segment structure, tick triggers, and observable symptoms. Expressed through the Spell Sentence vocabulary.|College operational approach. Chronosynclastic: information and probability. Stormpeak: military force. Unity: social engineering. Deepguard: legal and engineering. The 19 canonical effects describe institutional action.|
|**4**|**Assets**|What resources the front commands. Physical (troops, equipment, territory), Mental (intelligence, expertise), Social (relationships, reputation), Metaphysical (magical capability, planar connections, divine backing).|Four Condition Track pillars + college-specific capabilities + territorial isotope resources (Dual Periodic Table). A front’s isotope access determines what it can build, deploy, and sustain.|
|**5**|**Representation Chain**|How the front’s intent flows from driving actor through intermediaries to ground-level execution. At each link: what information is added, lost, or distorted. The party interacts with the BOTTOM of the chain.|Institutional hierarchy. Headmaster → faculty → regional representative → local operative. The distortion at each link IS the story.|
|**6**|**Clocks**|The front’s active operations. Multiple clocks per front (typically 2-4). Each follows the clock template (Document 4A). Prep produces clock POTENTIAL. Play produces parameterized clocks.|Created when the front enters active play. The front sheet lists potential clocks. The GM instantiates specific clocks when the party engages with the front’s area of operation.|
|**7**|**Relationships**|How this front connects to other fronts. Trust, debt, and tension on each connection. The connections are the channels through which clock effects propagate. Reference Document 3B.|The relationship map. Bidirectional edges with three properties. When this front’s clocks advance, the connected fronts feel pressure through these edges.|
|**8**|**Failure Trajectory**|What happens if the driving NPC trends toward slag. The front’s character shifts as the NPC’s personal clock advances toward the college’s failure state.|Personal clock position + college failure state. A Chronosynclastic front driven by a paranoid analyst starts seeing conspiracies in the data. A Unity front driven by a captured architect optimizes without seeing individuals.|

**FRONT TYPES**

Four types of fronts, distinguished by what kind of entity drives them. The template is identical. The content differs.

**Institutional Fronts**

Driven by colleges, guilds, governments, or other formal organizations. The institution’s charter defines the methodology. The headmaster or leader’s Schema shapes the expression. The representation chain is long — multiple layers of distortion between institutional intent and ground-level execution.

The nine colleges are nine permanent institutional fronts in the setting. Their methodologies are defined by their initiations (College Initiations Canon). Their interactions drive the world’s political, social, and metaphysical dynamics. Every inter-college relationship is a front connection with trust, debt, and tension properties.

**Individual Fronts**

Driven by a single NPC pursuing personal objectives. The representation chain is short or absent — the NPC acts directly. Individual fronts are generated from the NPC’s personal Schema pressure points (maintenance, conflict, and mystery fronts). They are the most common front type for adventure-scale play because the party encounters individuals more frequently than institutions.

An individual front operating within an institutional context inherits the institution’s methodology but applies it through the NPC’s personal lens. A rogue Chronosynclastic analyst running a personal intelligence operation is an individual front using institutional methodology without institutional authorization. The gap between the personal and institutional is itself a pressure point.

**Location Fronts (Project Slaughterhouse)**

Driven by a physical environment that behaves as a reactive organism. The dungeon, the contested zone, the living ecosystem. The location has assets (monsters, traps, environmental hazards), methodology (how it responds to intrusion), and a representation chain (the location’s inhabitants at different depths represent different levels of the environment’s defensive response).

The Titanwood IS a location front. It has motivation (biological expansion), methodology (Verdant Font isotope expression), assets (fauna, flora, failed Wardens), and a failure trajectory (if uncontained, the ecosystem consumes adjacent civilization). The Crash Site beneath Mortis Ævum is a location front. The Pythia Gauntlet between Stormpeak and the Chronosynclastic Institute is a location front.

Location fronts respond to the party’s actions as an organism responds to disturbance. The goblins in room 4 hear the fight in room 3 and react. The ecosystem shifts when resources are extracted. The environment has its own logic that produces consequences independent of any NPC’s intentions. The location front’s clocks track the environment’s state rather than an actor’s progress.

**Territorial Fronts**

Driven by the pressures inherent to a territory’s Zoetrope position, isotope distribution, and institutional landscape. Territorial fronts are not run by any single actor — they are the emergent properties of the territory’s internal tensions. The transition pressure between Zoetrope positions IS a territorial front. The resource competition for scarce isotopes IS a territorial front.

Territorial fronts operate at the slowest timescale. Their clocks measure decades, not sessions. They are tracked between campaign arcs rather than between sessions. The accumulated weight of resolved institutional and individual fronts within the territory IS the tick trigger for territorial clocks. When enough fronts resolve in a consistent direction, the territorial clock advances and the Zoetrope position shifts.

**THE REPRESENTATION CHAIN**

Field 5 on the front template. The mechanism by which intent distorts through human layers. The most important field for adventure generation because the party never interacts with the top of the chain — they interact with the bottom, where the original intent has been filtered, interpreted, and transformed by every intermediary.

**How Distortion Works**

At each link in the chain, three things happen:

**Information is added:** The intermediary adds their own understanding, biases, and institutional vocabulary to the message. A headmaster’s strategic directive becomes a faculty member’s operational order becomes a regional representative’s local interpretation.

**Information is lost:** Nuance, context, and conditional qualifiers are dropped at each link. “Exercise caution in the eastern district, there may be Verdant Font bleed activity” becomes “Watch the east side” becomes “Stay out of the east.”

**Information is distorted:** The intermediary’s own Schema shapes how they pass the message. A cautious intermediary amplifies warnings. An ambitious intermediary softens restrictions. A burned-out intermediary transmits the letter without the spirit.

The party encounters the bottom-of-chain version of the top-of-chain intent. They see the local chirurgeon implementing a policy that makes no sense at their level. Investigation leads upward through the chain. At each link, the party discovers how the message changed and why. The chain IS the adventure — tracing distorted information backward to its source.

**Chain Length and Front Type**

|**Individual fronts**|Short or absent chain. The NPC acts directly. Minimal distortion. The party sees the actor’s intent relatively clearly.|
| :- | :- |
|**Institutional fronts**|Long chain (3-5 links typical). Maximum distortion. The party sees heavily filtered output and must investigate upward to understand the source.|
|**Location fronts**|The chain is spatial rather than hierarchical. Surface encounters represent the location’s outer defenses. Deeper penetration reveals the location’s core logic. Each depth layer is a chain link.|
|**Territorial fronts**|The chain is structural. Civilizational pressures manifest through institutional behaviors, which manifest through individual actions. The party sees the individual action and must trace upward through institution to territory to understand the systemic cause.|

**THE DUAL-TRACK SYSTEM: GREAT AND LITTLE TRADITION**

Every front system operates on two parallel tracks. The gap between tracks is automatically a seed at Vertebra 5 (Symptoms).

**The Great Tradition**

The institutional narrative. Official histories, college curricula, sanctioned knowledge, formal doctrine. Information that flows through the representation chain of authorized institutions. The Great Tradition is curated, controlled, and deliberately shaped. It tells the story the institutions want told.

In the Ætherium Codex, the Great Tradition is maintained primarily by Praxis Collegium’s foundational curriculum — the shared conceptual vocabulary that every Tier 1+ practitioner learns. The curriculum is accurate but deliberately incomplete. The omissions ARE the Great Lie’s architecture. What the Great Tradition does not say is as significant as what it says.

**The Little Tradition**

The folk narrative. Nursery rhymes, drinking songs, local stories, tradesman’s wisdom, old wives’ tales. Information that exists outside institutional control. The Little Tradition is not curated — it accumulates through oral transmission, practical experience, and communal memory. It encodes information the institutions do not control and sometimes do not know about.

In the Ætherium Codex, the Little Tradition includes songs that encode planar hazard data (“don’t sleep where the moss glows blue”), trade secrets that describe isotope behaviors without the formal alchemical vocabulary, and folk remedies that work for reasons the colleges have not formalized. Tier 0 (Everyman) characters live entirely in the Little Tradition. They may know things the colleges do not — local knowledge that never entered the institutional record.

**The Gap as Seed Source**

The discrepancy between the Great and Little Traditions IS a symptom at Vertebra 5. When the institutional narrative says one thing and the folk narrative says another, the gap is a seed the party can investigate. The investigation leads to one of three outcomes:

**1.** The institution omitted information deliberately (the gap reveals institutional control).

**2.** The folk tradition preserved information the institution lost (the gap reveals institutional limitation).

**3.** Both traditions are wrong in different ways (the gap reveals that the truth is more complex than either account).

Each outcome produces a different kind of adventure. The GM does not need to decide which outcome is correct during prep. They prepare the gap. The party’s investigation determines which outcome emerges. This is Schrödinger’s Gun applied to institutional knowledge — the truth is in superposition until the party collapses it through observation.

**THE DEFAULT TIMELINE**

The sum of all active fronts’ terminal states reached without player intervention. Not a separate document. A derived forecast.

**Derivation Procedure**

List every active front. For each front, read the terminal state (Field 8 on the clock template, or the narrative description from the front’s clock potential if clocks are not yet parameterized). The terminal state describes what happens when the front achieves its goal without interference.

Now read all terminal states simultaneously. What does the world look like when ALL of them resolve? Some terminal states are compatible — they can coexist. Some are contradictory — one front’s success prevents another’s. The contradictions between terminal states ARE the setting’s central tensions. The compatible resolutions are the background changes. The contradictory resolutions are the dramatic collisions.

The Default Timeline is the answer to Ten Questions Q6 applied to the entire setting: “What happens if nobody intervenes?” The answer is the gravitational trajectory that the party’s actions bend. The further the actual outcome diverges from the default, the more narrative energy was required and the more dramatic the story becomes.

**Using the Default Timeline**

The GM does not read the Default Timeline to the party. The GM uses it as the hidden benchmark against which the party’s actions are measured. When the party disrupts a front, the default shifts. When the party ignores a front, the default advances toward its terminal state. The party does not need to know the default — they need to encounter symptoms that hint at what will happen if they do nothing.

Between sessions, the GM advances all active fronts whose tick triggers fired. The Default Timeline updates automatically. The world moves. The party enters the next session into a world that is slightly different from the one they left — not because the GM authored a change, but because the fronts advanced on schedule. The party’s sense that the world moves without them IS the Default Timeline operating.

**FRONT METHODOLOGY AND THE GAME SYSTEM**

The front’s methodology (Field 3) is not narrative flavor. It is the game system expressed at the institutional level.

**The Spell Sentence as Institutional Action**

When a front takes action, the action is describable as a Spell Sentence operation. A Deepguard front expanding its ward network is performing Protection and Nullify effects at institutional scale. A Wildpath front reclaiming territory is performing Growth and Transform effects through the Verdant Font’s biological processes. The 19 canonical effects are not just combat mechanics — they are the vocabulary of institutional action.

This connection means that the same grammar describes individual spellcasting (Vertebra 6: Adventures) and institutional operations (Vertebra 3: Fronts). A character casting Protection on themselves and a college deploying a ward network across a territory are performing the same effect at different scales. The rules do not change. The scale changes.

**The Dual Periodic Table as Front Resources**

A front’s assets (Field 4) include access to specific isotopes. The 104 isotopes across five planes determine what a front can build, deploy, and sustain. Territorial control of isotope-rich zones IS the resource game that fronts compete over.

A front operating in a Borderlands-dominant territory has access to inorganic chemistry resources — metals, minerals, structural materials. A front in a Verdant Font territory has biological resources — living materials, mutation catalysts, ecological leverage. The isotope distribution shapes what each front CAN do, which constrains how its clocks advance and what symptoms its operations produce.

**College Methodology Table**

|**College**|**Dominant Effects**|**Operational Tempo**|**Symptom Character**|
| :- | :- | :- | :- |
|**Wildpath**|Growth, Transform|Biological — slow accumulation, sudden eruption|Ecological shifts, creature behavior changes, terrain alteration|
|**Mortis Ævum**|Healing, Nullify, Senses|Clinical — methodical, patient, thorough|Queue fluctuations, theological shifts, archive access patterns|
|**Deepguard**|Protection, Nullify, Create|Engineering — precise, systematic, incremental|New regulations, ward installations, infrastructure changes|
|**Unity**|Mind Link, Enhanced Trait, Illusion|Social — gradual, ambient, accumulative|Behavioral shifts, preference changes, architectural nudging|
|**Chiaroscuro**|Illusion, Transform, Create|Performative — episodic, dramatic, cyclical|Narrative shifts, perception changes, reality/fiction blurring|
|**Sovereign Exchange**|Summon, Movement, Create|Commercial — transactional, opportunistic, responsive|Trade route changes, price fluctuations, contract patterns|
|**Stormpeak**|Damage, Protection, Enhanced Trait|Military — rapid, decisive, event-triggered|Troop movements, fortification changes, operational tempo shifts|
|**Chronosynclastic**|Senses, Chronos, Illusion|Analytical — slow, calculated, probability-triggered|Information pattern changes, prediction anomalies, probability distortions|
|**Praxis**|Variable|Distributed — parallel, redundant, adaptive|Inter-college coordination changes, curriculum shifts, humanitarian pattern changes|

**THE SMOOTHING EFFECT AS FRONT RESISTANCE**

The Smoothing Effect (regulatory system) governs how much resistance fronts encounter when they try to change the world. It is referenced here because it most directly shapes front behavior.

Small front actions pass through unnoticed. A local Deepguard operative installing a ward on one building does not trigger Smoothing resistance. The change is small enough to be absorbed by the setting’s natural coherence.

Large front actions trigger dampening. A Deepguard initiative to restructure an entire city’s ward network encounters resistance — institutional inertia, political opposition, resource constraints, unexpected complications. These are not authored obstacles. They are the Smoothing Effect expressing through the setting’s social physics. Large changes require accumulated pressure over multiple clock cycles to overcome the threshold.

This is why clocks have multiple segments rather than binary states. The front does not achieve its goal in one action. It advances incrementally through a resistant medium. Each segment represents one step of accumulated pressure overcoming one layer of Smoothing resistance. The front that fills its clock has pushed through enough resistance to produce irreversible change. The Smoothing Effect ensures that only sustained, committed action produces lasting results — which ensures that only fronts driven by genuine pressure (deep Schema tensions) have the energy to complete their clocks.

**EXPRESSION GUIDANCE: INFORMATION SEQUENCING**

Vertebra 3’s strongest expression bond is Information Sequencing — the order in which information reaches the audience. This bond is strong because the representation chain IS an information sequencing structure.

**Dramatic Irony**

The audience (or some participants) know something the characters do not. The representation chain creates dramatic irony naturally: the GM knows the headmaster’s intent at the top of the chain. The party knows only the local operative’s distorted version at the bottom. The gap between the two IS dramatic irony. The tension sustains as long as the party has not yet traced the chain to its source.

When one player character knows something the others do not (Kismet’s scouting report delivered only to the operator), intra-party dramatic irony exists. The player who knows can choose when and how to share. The delay IS the tension. The reveal IS the payoff.

**The Unreliable Chain**

Every link in the representation chain is an unreliable narrator. They are telling the truth as they understand it, but their understanding was shaped by their own Schema, filtered through their institutional vocabulary, and constrained by what their link above them chose to share. When the party receives contradictory information from two NPCs who are both telling the truth, the contradiction is not a lie — it is the representation chain distorting the same source through different filters.

Framing NPC contradictions as chain distortion rather than deception teaches the table that perspective shapes reality. Both NPCs are right from their position. The truth is at the top of the chain, visible only through investigation. This is the novelistic technique of the unreliable narrator applied structurally rather than stylistically.

**Sequencing Decisions**

The GM chooses which front’s symptoms to present first. This is an information sequencing decision. Presenting the Vestus containment symptoms before the institutional maintenance symptoms creates a mystery (why is the archive being locked down?). Presenting institutional maintenance symptoms first creates a different mystery (why is the institution faltering?). Same fronts, same clocks, different presentation order, different audience experience.

The order is not arbitrary. The pacing tracker (Document 4C) guides the selection based on the session’s current beat pattern. But within the pacing constraints, the GM’s sequencing choice shapes what the party investigates first, which shapes their understanding of the situation, which shapes their response. The sequencing IS a creative act with narrative consequences.

*This document defines what fronts ARE and how they are generated from pressure points. Document 3B (Relationship Map System) defines how fronts CONNECT to each other. Document 3C (Faction Turn Procedures) defines how fronts ACT between sessions. Together, the three documents constitute the complete Vertebra 3 architecture.*

**RELATIONSHIP MAP SYSTEM**

The Topology of Connection and Consequence

Document 3B — Vertebra 3: Fronts (Companion)

*How fronts connect to each other and how consequences propagate through the network*

**WHAT THE RELATIONSHIP MAP IS**

The relationship map is the wiring diagram for consequence propagation. Every front exists in a network of connections to other fronts. When a clock advances on one front, the advancement produces pressure that travels along the connections to other fronts. Which fronts feel the pressure, how much pressure arrives, and what form it takes are all determined by the properties of the connections between them.

Without the map, fronts are isolated countdowns. Each clock ticks toward its own terminal state in a vacuum. With the map, fronts are a system. Each clock’s advancement affects other clocks through the network of connections. The emergent complexity of the setting comes from these interactions — outcomes that no single front would produce on its own but that the network produces through propagation.

The map is simultaneously a prep tool (the GM draws it to understand the setting’s power structure), a play tool (the GM reads it to determine how consequences cascade), and a diagnostic tool (the GM examines it to find where the pressure is accumulating and which connections are approaching state changes).

**NODES**

Every entity that can drive a front or be affected by one is a node on the map. Nodes are not limited to NPCs. Any entity with a Schema or Schema-equivalent (Document 1B) can be a node.
